| Rate limits and hosting for the store launch | Part 1 planned and decided 2026-10-29 (audit step 4; dated entry "rate limits and storage caps: plan"): per-account limits in the database, storage caps and a new firewall rule. Built and released 2026-10-29 (dated entry "rate limits and storage caps: built"): migration 0033 applied to production with the user's go-ahead, then the app pushed. Next: the user replaces the firewall rule. The preview branch is at 0012 and needs catching up separately. Part 2 (Vercel plan) is audit step 7. Original note (added 2026-09-30 at the user's request): **1. Per-account limits on the server.** The one firewall rule is keyed by IP. Mobile carriers put many phones behind one shared address, and players at a table often share Wi-Fi, so several honest hosts could together reach 30 writes a minute. Recommended: add per-account write limits in the server, keeping the firewall rule as a coarse backstop against floods. This reverses the 2026-09-24 "no app-side counters" choice, so it needs the user's decision; limits, storage and the 429 message are designed then. **2. Vercel plan.** Hobby is for non-commercial use only; if the app earns money (price, in-app purchases or ads), move to Vercel Pro (about $20 a month), which also allows up to 40 rate-limit rules, longer firewall history and other keys than the IP. Recheck Neon Free limits at the same time |

## Decision history and planning convention

2026-10-27 Invite to Pokerize only on Profile (user request with a screenshot of a guest's opened card on Ranks: inviting "is better UX if only done from profile page and not the ranks page"). Removed the **Invite to Pokerize** button and its sheet from guest cards on your own standings; it stays in a guest's ⋯ options on Profile. Client-only. `FEATURES.md` sections 13 and 20 and What's New updated. **Checked on the testbed** as Bikram: all 15 guest cards on Ranks opened, no invite button. **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` 2026-10-27.

2026-10-27 sign-in page crash on a dead session (found while resetting the testbed; user: fix it). `/auth/sign-in` called `getHostSession()` unguarded; when the browser's sign-in cookie pointed to a session that no longer existed (revoked, "sign out everywhere" on a deletion request, or the account purged) and the 5-minute session cache had expired, the auth library tried to clear the cookie, which Next.js forbids in a page, and the page went blank ("This page couldn't load"). The invite page already caught this; `proxy.ts` covers `/` and `/admin`. **Fix:** the sign-in page catches the error and treats the visitor as signed out; signing in replaces the dead cookie. **Checked on the testbed:** reproduced with Farhan's sessions revoked and the cache expired (blank page, the error in the log); fixed, the sign-in page shows, signing in lands on Home, and a valid session still redirects past the page. What's New has a Fixed line. **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` 2026-10-27.

2026-10-27 no Pokerize badge on friend requests (user request with a screenshot of Home's "Meera wants to be friends" notice: "remove the pokerize badge from friend requests"). Removed from Home's request notice and Profile's friend request card; anyone sending a request has an account, so the badge said nothing there. It stays on Profile's player list, Find Friend's result and Ranks. Client-only. `FEATURES.md` badge section and What's New updated. **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` 2026-10-27.

2026-10-27 chosen names in friends' views (user report after joining from an invite as "Lakshmi": Profile and Home said Lakshmi, but Asha's group on Ranks and her games on Games still said "Lakshmi T."). **Cause:** a host's own lists name players through `player_display_names()` (the chosen name for anyone with an account, the host's name for the host's own player, otherwise the guest's current name), but `friend_group_sessions()`, which feeds a friend's group standings and a friend's games, returns `result.player_name`, the name written into each result when the game was saved. So friends' views also showed Bikram as "Bikram" rather than "Bikram Big Host", and kept a guest's old name after the host renamed them. Avatars already follow the account (`shown_avatar`). **User decision:** fix it so friends' views show players' chosen names and guests' current names; build on the testbed, production only with the user's go-ahead. **Design:** migration `0031_shown_names` adds `shown_name(owner, player)`, the name counterpart of `shown_avatar` (same three rules, no grant to the app role), and redefines `friend_group_sessions()` to use it for each result's `name` and for `myPlayerName`, falling back to the saved `player_name` if the player row is gone. Otherwise identical to 0028. No app code change: `/api/groups` and friends' games already take these names. **Privacy:** someone in a host's games now sees a co-player's chosen name where they saw the host's name for them, as they already see the chosen avatar; the privacy page's "everyone's result, as at the table" covers it. **Acceptance:** on the testbed, Lakshmi's view of Asha's group and games shows "Lakshmi" and "Bikram Big Host"; guests keep the host's names; a guest renamed by the host shows the new name to friends; the host's own views unchanged; tests, tsc, lint. **Rollout:** rehearse on development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`, then production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`, each with the user's go-ahead; rollback is re-running 0028's `friend_group_sessions()` (the helper can stay). **Built** (`migrations/0031_shown_names.sql`, appended to `schema.sql`; FEATURES.md section on names, What's New). **Checked on the testbed** (0031 applied by `testbed:setup`; the dev server needed a restart because setup rotates the role passwords): as Lakshmi, Asha's group read Sanjay T., Bikram Big Host, Lakshmi (me), and her games on Games read Lakshmi and Bikram Big Host; Asha renamed Sanjay T. to Sanjay Tiwari and her own list showed it as before; as Bikram, Asha's group read Sanjay Tiwari, Bikram Big Host (me), Lakshmi. The testbed keeps the rename until `testbed:reset`. **Then the host's own saved games (user: Lakshmi must see her chosen name everywhere, "strictly"):** a check of every place a name shows found the host's own Games list, Details, Settle up and Home's last session still used the saved `player_name` (the host's Ranks and the results card already looked names up by player ID). `selectSessions()` (`lib/poker/saved-sessions.ts`, read by the sessions API and the results card) now names each result through `player_display_names()` (one materialised lookup per query), falling back to the saved name for a player that no longer exists. App code only, no migration. **Checked that nothing relies on the saved names:** saving and Continue game resolve players by ID and store the current name; `isSameSession` leaves names out; re-sending a saved game, also with the old name "Lakshmi T.", returned `saved: 0` (same game, no conflict). **Checked on the testbed as Asha at 375 px:** Home, Games, Details and Settle up ("Lakshmi pays Sanjay Tiwari") show Lakshmi, Bikram Big Host and Sanjay Tiwari. **Left as is, on purpose:** the invite page names the guest ("You play as Lakshmi T.") before they've chosen a name, and a game already running keeps its seat names until it is saved. **Rehearsed on development** (user's go-ahead, 2026-10-27; `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`, one transaction, details in `migrations/README.md`): counts and net unchanged, grants as intended, the app's updated saved-games query runs as `menoka_app`; one host's own player now shows the account's name instead of the stored one in 24 results, as the rule intends (production likely has the same for the user's own player). **Applied to production** (user's go-ahead, 2026-10-27; `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`, one transaction): all counts and net unchanged; friends' views kept their shape (10 viewers, 18 groups, 104 games, 435 results), 60 results now show the current name. Friends' views changed at once; the host's own saved games change when the code is pushed. **Status:** committed on `main` 2026-10-27, not pushed; 0031 on the testbed, development and production.

2026-10-27 invite: no "Are you *Guest*?" step (user, after walking through the invite as a new person: the host made the link from that guest's profile, so joining from it means it's them; the second invite card after the name screen was one step too many). **User decisions:** accept the invite automatically once the name is saved; one **Accept invite** button for people who already have an account; a small **This isn't me** link beside it. **Design (agent's calls):** opening the link never accepts by itself (chat apps open links to draw previews), so the accept is sent by a tap: **Save and continue** on the name screen, or **Accept invite**. The signed-in part of `/join` is one client component (`JoinInvite`): it shows the name screen (`NameSetup` with an `onSaved` callback, which saves the name and then accepts) or the Accept card, and then "You and *Host* are now friends…". If the accept fails after the name is saved, the Accept card shows with the error, so a retry needs no reload. **This isn't me** declines as **No** did (the link is used up, nothing linked). A new person who isn't the guest can still decline only by not joining: the name screen has no "not me" (user chose the link for the Accept card). No migration or API change. **Acceptance:** signed out → invite card → sign in as a nameless account → name screen (guest's name filled in) → Save and continue → friends card, guest linked, link used up, no question card; an existing account opening the link → one card with Accept invite → friends card; This isn't me → "Nothing was linked", nothing linked; lint, tsc, tests; checked on the testbed at 375 px. **Built** (`components/join-invite.tsx`, `components/invite-card.tsx` with the card and heading shared by the server page and the client flow, `NameSetup` `onSaved`; the old `.invite-question`/`.invite-no` styles replaced by `.invite-not-me`). Errors on the invite page are sentence case too. The heading's line reads "You play as *Guest* in *Host*'s games." again, because guest names like "Anika S." end in a full stop. **Checked on the testbed at 375 px:** signed out → Asha's invite for Ishaan → New (no name) → name screen prefilled → Save and continue → "You and Asha Host are now friends…", Ishaan linked to New, link used; Ravi (already Asha's friend) → Accept invite → the already-friends refusal under the button; New (named) → Bikram's invite for Anika S. → Accept invite → friends, linked; Bikram's invite for Arjun G. → This isn't me → "Nothing was linked". The testbed keeps New named "Ishaan" and linked to Ishaan and Anika S. until `testbed:reset`. **Status:** committed on `main` 2026-10-27, not pushed.

2026-10-26 Pokerize badge trimmed (user, after removing it place by place: "it is not required everywhere"; asked for the full list, then "remove from games page"). **Kept only:** Profile player list, friend request cards (Profile and Home), Find Friend's result, own standings and friends' standings. **Removed:** setup chips and seating order, table player rows, in-game Session Standings, quick ledger rows, Settle up, and Games cards (own and friends'). With Games gone, the `onPokerize` flag on friends' shared games and the own-player lookup (`OnPokerizeContext`) were deleted, so the server sends exactly what it did before the badge. The dated entries below describe the fuller first build.

2026-10-26 tap the Pokerize badge (user asked whether a tap should say "Pokerize premium member", looking ahead to a paywall, or just that the player is on Pokerize; discussed, then "proceed"). **Decision (agent's recommendation, accepted):** the badge keeps meaning "has an account": if it meant "pays", free players with accounts would look like guests again in friends' standings, which is what the badge was made to fix, and paid ticks blur what a tick means (X). Premium, if shown at all, gets its own variant later (see the register row "Premium mark"). **Built now:** tapping the badge opens a small bubble above it (below it near the top of the screen): "*Name* is on Pokerize" ("You're on Pokerize" for yourself, "On Pokerize" without a name). On a friend's standings, a player who isn't your friend and has no request either way adds "Open their card to add them as a friend." The bubble closes on a second tap, a tap elsewhere, Escape, scrolling or resizing. The tap doesn't open or close a standings card or start Profile's long-press menu; the badge keeps its 14 px look with a larger invisible tap area; on a computer, hovering still shows "On Pokerize".

2026-10-26 Link to friend on every guest (user request: "it is confusing for me too so i imagine it would be confusing for regular users too. i'd say have the link to friend option available all the time until and unless the guest is linked to a friend"). The 2026-10-02 rule that hid the option unless a friend became your friend after the guest was added is dropped. **Kept:** only friends still on the player made at accept are listed (`madeAtAccept`), so a friend already linked to a guest can't be moved off it (the database function would delete that guest). Client-only; `friend_link_guest` never checked dates, so no migration. When no friend is free, the sheet explains that the person needs to be on Pokerize and to have accepted your request. Sheet wording simplified by the user the same day ("Which friend is *Guest*? Their games will move to that friend."; empty: "No friends to link yet. When *Guest* joins Pokerize and accepts your friend request, you can link them here."). Also by user request: the **Unfriend** confirmation became a title with three points ("You both keep every game." / "*Name* goes back to being a guest in your list." / "To link again, send a new friend request.") and a red danger button.

2026-10-26 Pokerize badge built (user: "Build the Pokerize badge as proposed: keep the Friend/Guest labels, no tap action, not on the live link or results card"). **Built:** `components/pokerize-badge.tsx` (inline SVG of the logo's chip face, 14 px, `role="img"`, "On Pokerize"). Own players: `OnPokerizeContext` (self or `linked`, by player id only) feeds `OwnPlayerBadge` / `OwnBadgedName`. Friends' standings: `row.isMe || row.account`. Friends' games: `buildGroupStandings` adds `onPokerize: true` to `sharedGames` results that are the reader or an active account in `relations` (no ids sent; tested); the phone keys them by name within that game. Places: Profile list (self, friends, sent requests), request cards on Profile and Home, Find Friend's result, own and friends' standings, not on the setup screen (user requests the same day: removed from the player chips, then the seating order), nothing during a game (user requests the same day removed it from the hand-by-hand table's player rows and Session Standings, then the quick ledger's rows), game cards (Settle up's Results had it too, removed by user request the same day). Pickers inside sheets (rebuy, cash-out, winner, side pots) are left plain. Wrapping names keep the badge on their last word's line (`BadgedName`, `.badge-keep`); one-line names ellipsize before it (`.name-with-badge`). **Checked** on the testbed as Asha at 375 px: badges match accounts on Profile (6 of 11), own and Bikram's standings, own and Bikram's games, Settle up and setup (before it was removed there); light and dark. Not checked: a friend's standings row with an account who isn't the reader's friend (no such row in the seed; covered by the existing `account` test).

2026-10-26 Pokerize badge beside names (planned, then built the same day; user idea: "similar to a blue tick on instagram or x.com, a pokerize logo can appear beside the names of users app wide which will be a indicator whether they have an account on pokerize or not"). **Problem:** on a friend's standings you only learn who can get a friend request (an account) and who can't (that host's guest) after opening each card. **Meaning of the badge:** this player is a Pokerize account, as far as the reader may know: yourself, your friends, your guests linked to a friend, and on a friend's standings any player whose row already carries `account` (migration 0029). Never inferred from names; a guest who has an account but isn't linked shows no badge. Inactive (locked or deleting) accounts show none, as today they get no request. **Mark (agent's proposal):** a small drawn SVG, the top face of the logo (green chip with a spade), about 14 px after the name, `aria-label="On Pokerize"`, legible in both themes. The app icon itself (a chip stack on black) turns to mud at that size. **Where (proposal):** Ranks (own and friends' standings), Profile players list, friend requests and friend list, the live table and setup seat lists, Games details and Settle up (own and friends' games). Friends' games need one new field per result (`onPokerize`, from the `accountId` the server already reads; no migration, still no account ids sent). **Not shown (proposal):** the no-sign-in live standings link and the results card image, which reach people outside the app and would reveal who has an account. **Labels:** the Friend / Guest words under names stay, since they say how the player relates to you; the badge says whether they're on Pokerize. On a friend's standings that gives three readable cases: badge + Friend, badge only (can be added; Add Friend stays inside the card), nothing (that host's guest). **Data:** no migration and no new disclosure: every place above already knows (or, for friends' standings, already sends) whether the player is an account. **Open for the user:** the places list, whether the badge replaces the Guest label, and whether tapping it does anything (proposal: no; it is a mark, like a tick). **Acceptance:** the badge appears exactly for linked accounts in every listed place, never on the live link or results card; checked at 375 px in light and dark; `FEATURES.md`, What's New and the Features page updated with the release.

2026-10-26 invite a guest to Pokerize. User request: "the previous position of the button was better i.e inside the card ... if a user doesn't have an account, clicking on that button would create a shareable link with social shares popping up (like results card) and when shared to the actual real user of that account, they can create an account on pokerize and automatically their account would be linked to their guest account. plan first, once approved, build it." A plan was proposed (a friend's guest invited by anyone, linked only with that host's approval). **User decisions on it:** "only the host who added guests from their profile should be able to send pokerize signup invites. dont allow linking a friends guest", clarified: "by not linking a friends guest, i meant not being able to send them a pokerize signup invite request". Other choices taken as recommended: 30 days, single use, the landing page shows only names.

**Step 0, Add Friend back in the card.** The + Add Friend pill under names (the earlier follow-up) is gone; the opened card of a friend's-standings player with an account shows **Add Friend**, "Friend request sent" or "*Name* sent you a friend request. Answer it on Profile." Friends are marked **Friend** under their name (no button); own standings keep Friend / Guest. **Then (user request):** "no need to keep the 'Friend request sent' text here, move it to the notifications button": after sending, the card shows nothing, and the Profile bell lists every waiting sent request ("You sent *Name* a friend request", `kind: "sent"` in `lib/friends/notifications.ts`, from the overview's `sent`), never counted in the badge. Checked on the testbed.

**Step 1, built.** **Invite to Pokerize** on your own current guests only: Profile guest **⋯** options and the opened card in your own standings. Never on friends, your own player, removed players, or anyone in a friend's standings. A sheet explains it, **Make invite link**, then **Share invite** (Web Share, where available) / **Copy invite** with "*Host* invited you to Pokerize, where your poker results as *Guest* are waiting." The link (`/join/<token>`, 32 random bytes, only its SHA-256 stored) works once for 30 days; a new link for the guest replaces the old; at most 20 usable per host; it stops when the guest is linked, removed or deleted, or the host is locked. **Invite page** (public, `noindex`, `no-referrer`): host's name and avatar, the guest's name, **Join Pokerize** → sign-in with `?invite=` (the magic link's callback URL carries the token, so another browser works; the testbed persona buttons pass it too) → name screen prefilled with the guest's name when needed → "Are you *Guest*?" **Yes** / **No**. Yes: any pending request between them is cancelled, a request from the host naming the guest is inserted and accepted through `friend_accept` (so the guest is linked, the joiner gets a player for the host under a free name, the friendship and audit rows are as usual), and the function checks the guest really is linked to the caller. **Host confirmation, built and removed the same day:** the agent pointed out that whoever holds the link can claim the guest, and offered a step where the host links or declines the claim first. It was built from a suggested reply button, but the user had not asked for it ("i didn't ask for the host confirmation step"), so the instant link is back. The host's undo for a wrong person is **Unfriend**, which unlinks the guest with every game kept. No: the invite is used up. Host opening their own link: "This is your invite for *Guest*". Already friends: refused ("ask them to use Link to friend…"). **Data:** migration `0030_guest_invites` (table `guest_invites`, row security on, no grants; `guest_invite_valid` (not granted), `guest_invite_create`, `guest_invite_preview`, `guest_invite_answer`). API `POST /api/invites` (`create`, `answer`). Privacy page (Invites data, who sees an invite link, retention) and account-deletion page (links stop while locked; invites deleted) updated, `DELETION_UPDATED` 2026-10-26. **Checked on the testbed at 375 px:** Asha → Profile → Dev → Invite → link; signed-out page; Meera signed in through it, Yes → Dev (9 games) linked to Meera, friends, Meera has "Asha Host", invite `accepted`, link then "expired or already used"; Meera's invite for Pranav C. opened by the no-name persona → name screen prefilled → No → `declined`, nothing linked (that persona was reset to no name afterwards); Meera's invite for her friend Asha's player refused (409); host's own link page; own standings guest card shows Invite; Bikram's group guests show nothing, Manish R. shows Add Friend. **Status:** released 2026-10-26: 0029 and 0030 rehearsed on development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` and applied to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (user's go-ahead each; clean, counts unchanged), then committed and pushed. **Next:** the first real invite, to check the email sign-in brings the person back to it. Production needs the magic-link callback with `?invite=` allowed by Neon Auth (same origin, so expected to work; check on the first real invite).

2026-10-26 add friends from a friend's standings (user request: "currently there is no easy way to add friends other than adding them individually via player code or qr scanner ... add a button to send friend request to those players as it is likely that the user already knows those players since they all played together"). **Design (agent's call, long-term choice):** in a friend's group on Ranks, an opened card of a player who has their own account shows **Add Friend**, or instead "You're friends", "Friend request sent" or "*Name* sent you a friend request. Answer it on Profile." Guests, your own row and inactive accounts show nothing. The button sits under the card's numbers (the card's header is already one button, so it can't hold another). **Data:** migration `0029_coplayer_friend_requests` adds two `SECURITY DEFINER` functions: `friend_relations(uuid[])` (how the caller stands with each active account, at most 500) and `friend_send_coplayer(uuid)`, which checks that the caller and the target both have a verified result in one saved, non-discarded game of an active host who is the caller's friend and has the caller linked (the target being the host's own player or a player linked to them; otherwise `friend:not-found`), then calls `friend_send` with the target's code, so limits and refusals are unchanged. `/api/groups` adds `account: { id, relation }` to those rows (`buildGroupStandings`); this is the one place a co-player's account id reaches the phone (it was server-only since 0016). It reveals only that the player has an account and how you stand; the server re-checks the shared game on send. Without the migration `/api/groups` logs the failure and sends no `account`, so the code can ship first. `POST /api/friends` gains `send-coplayer`. Not chosen: exposing co-players' user codes (lets anyone pass the code on); a request by player name (names change). Privacy page bullet added (date already 2026-10-26). **Status:** released 2026-10-26 with the guest invites (0029 on production). Checked on the testbed at 375 px as Asha in Bikram's group (Karan linked to "Manish R." for the test): Add Friend sent, card switched to "Friend request sent", Karan in Asha's sent list; Ravi (no shared game) refused with "No such user found". 475 tests, tsc, lint pass. **Next:** the user tries it; with their go-ahead, rehearse 0029 on development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`, apply to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`, then push. **Follow-up (user request, same day):** "in profile, we can see who are our friends and who are guests. do the same with the players in the rankings list. for personally hosted games, use friend and guest and for other host's ranking lists, use only friend and add friend." Built: the line under each standings name (shared with Newcomer / Unranked) says **Friend** or **Guest** on your own standings (`PlayerProfile.linked`, so a removed or unfriended player reads Guest), and on a friend's standings **Friend** or a green **+ Add Friend** pill, moved out of the opened card into the row. Agent's calls: after tapping, the pill becomes **Request sent**, and a request they sent you reads **Wants to be friends** (an Add Friend there would only be refused); guests show nothing on a friend's standings; send errors show as a toast. Because the row header is one button, a row with the pill uses a cover button under the row (`.standing-head`, `.standing-cover`) and lets taps through to the pill only; other rows are unchanged. Checked on the testbed at 375 px: own standings Guest/Friend; Bikram's group: Manish R. pill sent ("Request Sent") without toggling the card, row tap still opens it, Aditi T. reads Friend.

2026-10-26 standings graph: fitted height, Net view, column legend (user request: "due to percentages being the way to rank players, nearly all the graphs become the same looking"; asked to try a different y-axis gap and add a second metric; earlier the same day, legend names to read down each column). **Finding:** the axis was mirrored around zero and sized by the largest point, which is nearly always a one- or two-game average (the Bikram testbed group spanned −400%…+400% while lines settled between −90% and +150%). **Design (long-term choice):** `lib/poker/chart-axis.ts` (pure, tested): the axis ends just past the data (4% padding), always includes zero, gridlines at the 1/2/5 × 10ⁿ step nearest a fifth of the range; on Return % each line's first two games don't set the height (`settledValues`), the latest point always does, and lines are clipped at the edge. Graph 212 → 240 px tall. **Second metric:** a **Return % / Net** switch beside the heading; Net plots cumulative money won or lost over ranked games (`StandingsSeries.cumulativeNet`, already computed), from 0 the game before a player's first; legend sorts by net in that view. Friends' groups get it too: each `GroupChartLine` point gains a fourth number, money so far (allowed since every row's net is now sent). Ranking itself is unchanged. Not chosen: a log or symlog scale (harder to read), a fixed range, or hiding early games. No migration. **Status:** released 2026-10-26 (pushed with the user's go-ahead).

2026-10-26 everyone's money in friends' standings (user request: "bring back the invested amount, net profit/loss for players playing in games not hosted by a user"). **User decision:** a friend's group standings send every row's **Invested**, **Total** and **Net**, not just the reader's own; this replaces the 2026-10-05 "own row only" rule, as the 2026-10-26 friends' games already show everyone's result per game. The amounts still come only from the games the reader played in. `buildGroupStandings` sends `invested` and `net` on every row; the Ranks **i** "Hidden" list drops that item; privacy page, `FEATURES.md` section 20 and What's New updated. No migration. **Status:** released 2026-10-26 (pushed with the user's go-ahead).

2026-10-05 blinds in cents, no unit selector (user feedback: the **Whole / With Cents** selector is one more step before a game; asked for two rows of stacks from small stakes up to 50K, with the big blind adjusting to the stack). **Built:** the selector is gone. Stacks are 20, 50, 100, 200, 500, 1K / 2K, 5K, 10K (default), 20K, 50K, Other, in a two-row grid (`Segmented` `columns`). A chosen or typed stack sets the big blind to 1/100 of it and offers 1/200, 1/100, 1/50 and 1/20 (for 10K: 50, 100, 200, 500, as before). **The unit is derived, not chosen (`lib/poker/stakes.ts`):** the form works in hundredths; at the start the game is `cents` when the stack is under 1,000 or any amount (stack, big blind, typed small blind, an "add" increase, the rebuy closing blind) has cents, otherwise `whole`, and amounts are converted to that unit. Whole-chip stacks round their big blind choices and small blind shares to whole chips, so a 10K game starts exactly as before. Rejected: making every new game cents (it would change rounding in big games, such as the half small blind of an odd big blind). Everything in the entry below still holds except the selector. **Acceptance:** lint, tsc, 255 tests (4 new for stakes); on the testbed at 375 px: the two rows fit, 50 offers ₹0.25/₹0.50/₹1/₹2.50 with ₹0.50 picked and starts a cents game (pot ₹0.75); a default 10K game starts in whole chips with ₹50/₹100 and pot 150. Both test games were discarded.

2026-10-05 blinds in cents (user request: "add blinds below 1, i.e. in decimals"). **User decision:** steps of 0.01 (cents), not a coarser step. **Design (long-term choice):** each game has a chip unit, `whole` (every game before this) or `cents`, in which every stored amount is hundredths (0.25 is stored as 25). Amounts stay whole numbers end to end, so betting, side pots, the odd chip (now one cent), undo, rebuys and accounting checks are unchanged and exact; only entering and showing amounts know the unit. Rejected: decimals in state (floating-point drift in pots), and converting every stored amount to cents (rewrites all production data). The unit is chosen at setup (**Amounts: Whole / With Cents**) and can't change mid-game; a continued game keeps it, and a save that changes it is refused. **Totals across games** (Standings invested and net, the cumulative net series, Profile net and recent bars, friends' group standings, admin guest nets) are added in hundredths and returned in currency units, which may have cents; returns are unit-free, so rankings are unaffected and `STANDINGS_METRIC_VERSION` stays 1. **Display:** two decimals only when an amount has cents ("₹0.25", "₹1.50", "₹1"); Profile's all-time net drops cents from 1,000 up so it fits. **Data:** migration 0020 adds `poker_sessions.chip_unit` (`whole` default, or `cents`), the helper `chip_amount()`, and re-creates `friend_group_sessions()` (adds `chipUnit`), `admin_game_row`, `admin_guests` and `admin_account_detail` (net summed with `chip_amount`); no existing row changes. Live links carry `chipUnit` in the snapshot (no migration; stored as JSON). **Code:** `lib/poker/money.ts` (`ChipUnit`, `parseChips`, `chipsToInput`, `formatChips`, `chipsInCents`), validation, continue, duplicate check, live view, standings, profile stats, group standings, admin `inCurrencyUnits`; UI `ChipUnitContext` (per game or saved game; totals use the default `whole`) and `ChipInput`. **Acceptance:** lint, tsc and 251 tests (13 new in `test/cents.test.ts`); on the testbed (3006) as Asha: cents setup with presets and typed 40.75, odd 0.10/0.25, typed raise 1.15 ("Raise ₹1.25"), winner ₹1.50, between-hands standings, blind editor adding 0.05 (₹0.25, ₹0.30, ₹0.35 …), live link page, saved card and Home last session, Ranks totals mixing units (Bikram invested ₹80,040.75), Profile, and `/admin` games. A bug found there (the saved record dropped the unit) was fixed, and the one testbed game saved before the fix was corrected to `cents` by SQL. **Not yet:** rehearsal on dev, production, a real phone.

2026-10-05 seven-day sign-out (user report: some production pushes sign them out, others don't). **Finding:** deploys and migrations don't sign anyone out; nothing in the code or migrations 0001–0018 writes to `neon_auth.session`. The user's production session rows (read only, with the user's go-ahead) show Neon Auth sessions last 7 days and are extended with use (`expiresAt` = `updatedAt` + 7 days). But the Mac and Android sessions from 25 Sep were replaced by new sign-ins on 2 Oct, 7 days and about 1 hour after each was created, while the old rows were still valid until 8 and 9 Oct; the Android row had been extended 2 hours before. **Cause:** in `@neondatabase/auth` 0.5.0-beta, `trySessionCache` answers every `get-session` (from `auth.getSession()` and `auth.middleware()`) whenever a `session_token` cookie is present. It forwards only its own `session_data` cookie and discards the renewed `session_token` cookie Neon Auth sends when it extends a session, so the browser keeps the 7-day `Max-Age` from sign-in. Inferred from the code and timing; not yet confirmed by reading the cookie's expiry in DevTools. **Prediction:** the user's current sessions (2 Oct) end about 9 Oct 18:09 IST on the Mac and 21:02 IST on Android. **Upstream:** 0.5.0-beta (2026-08-11) is the latest release, and `packages/auth/src/server/session/cache-handler.ts` on `neondatabase/neon-js` main still has this behaviour; no matching issue (closest open: #195, #196, #200), so the user had one filed from their account on 2026-10-05: https://github.com/neondatabase/neon-js/issues/234. Remove the proxy workaround once a release fixes it. **Options (not decided):** (1) report it upstream and upgrade when fixed; (2) workaround: about once a day the proxy calls `get-session` with `disableCookieCache=true`, which goes through `handleAuthResponse` and does forward the renewed cookie, so active users stay signed in (someone away for 7 days is still signed out); (3) both. Old rows the browser has dropped stay valid in Neon Auth until they expire. **Decision (user, 2026-10-05):** build option 2. **Built (2026-10-05; pushed the same day with the user's go-ahead):** `fetchUncachedSession` in `lib/auth/server.ts` calls `get-session` with `disableCookieCache=true` through `handleAuthProxyRequest`; `proxy.ts` runs it for `/`, `/admin` and `/api/*` (not `/api/live/*`, which is public and CDN-cached) when the request has a session token and no `menoka-session-renewed` cookie, appends every cookie Neon Auth sends, and sets that marker (HttpOnly, 12 hours). A failed check changes nothing and is retried on the next request. Helpers `hasSessionToken` and `needsSessionRenewal` in `lib/auth/session-cookies.ts` have unit tests. **Testbed check (2026-10-05, persona new.person, session row aged by SQL so Neon Auth would extend it):** without the check, Neon Auth moved `expiresAt` to +7 days but only `session_data` reached the client (bug reproduced); with it, `/` and `/api/profile` each returned a renewed `session_token` with `Max-Age=604800`, and the row moved to +7 days; with the marker no check ran; `/api/live/<token>` set no cookies; signed out, `/` still redirects to sign-in and the API answers 401. The check adds one Neon Auth round trip, at most every 12 hours per browser (0.9–2.1 s on the testbed, including the extension write). tsc, lint on the changed files and 204 tests pass. **Not yet done:** checking on production that a browser's `session_token` expiry moves forward. Users signed in before the release still lose their current cookie on its original date unless they use the app after the release (the first request then renews it).

2026-10-02 admin dashboard (user request: "see every signed up user, guests and other details", mostly on a laptop). **Decisions (user):** read only, no actions on accounts; full detail (emails, every host's guests, per-game buy-ins and results), with each look at one person's detail recorded in the audit log. **Design:** `/admin` (`app/admin/page.tsx`, `components/admin-dashboard.tsx`) with a sidebar and five views: Overview (KPI tiles, sign-ups and games per week for 12 weeks, newest users, top hosts, latest games), Users (sortable, searchable table with filters; a row opens a drawer with that person's players, latest 50 games, friends and pending requests), Guests (every guest with host, games, net, last played; filter by host), Games (latest 300 across hosts, each expandable to its results) and System (accounts awaiting deletion, purge job, recent audit events, applied migrations). Laptop first; the sidebar becomes icons below 1100 px and a tab strip below 760 px. **Access:** row security stops `menoka_app` reading other accounts, so migration 0018 adds `SECURITY DEFINER` functions (`admin_overview`, `admin_accounts`, `admin_guests`, `admin_games`, `admin_system`, `admin_account_detail`) that each start with `admin_caller()`, which allows only an active account whose Auth user is in the new `app_admins` table. The runtime role has no grant on `app_admins`; an admin is added only by the migration owner with SQL, never from an email or name in code. Non-admins get a plain 404, signed-out visitors the sign-in page. Numbered 0018 because another session's uncommitted `0017_continue_saved_games` was already on the testbed. Checked on the testbed with Asha as admin: every view, search, sort, the drawer (one `admin.view_account` audit row per open), light and dark, 1440, 1280, 900 and 375 px wide; with Asha removed from `app_admins` the page is a 404; called as Ravi or with no session the functions raise `admin:forbidden`. lint, tsc, 189 tests and a build pass.

2026-09-20: User requested switch to main and documentation-only transition plan. Repository inspected. Initial private-group recommendation was superseded by the user's clarification: one orchestrating account owns a friend list and games; friends need no account or invitation. User delegated ranking choice. Average session return selected with stated limitations. No implementation or production data changes.

For each future feature, add: date, problem, accepted behavior, non-goals, architecture choices and tradeoffs, migration impact, acceptance criteria, open questions and status. Keep user decisions distinct from recommendations; update this document when scope changes.

2026-09-20 follow-up: User explicitly confirmed host-owned accounts with guest friends and average session return. Rank all eligible players immediately; do not use a provisional label. Neon Auth requested as preferred identity direction. Ask user about historical ownership during data separation, and revisit deletion/retention alongside login migration. Ask remaining architecture questions when relevant. Neon handles identity, while owner access rules still need explicit server checks and database policies. Reference: https://neon.com/docs/auth/overview.

2026-09-20 implementation step 1: Created `feature/multi-user-transition`. Removed the provisional threshold from the planned ranking. Verified Neon MCP is installed and enabled in Codex. Installed project-scoped Next.js DevTools MCP in `.codex/config.toml`. No application, database, auth, or production data changes in this step.

2026-09-20 implementation step 2: Confirmed email magic links as the sole initial login method. Authenticated Neon MCP and created the isolated Neon branch `multi-user-auth` from production. Provisioned Managed Better Auth there and enabled Magic Link with a five-minute expiry and new-user registration. Configured the local app to use the branch's database/Auth endpoints, added a custom magic-link sign-in screen, secure cookie session handling, logout, auth proxy and server-side API session checks. Verified the provider configuration from `neon_auth.project_config`, unauthenticated API rejection and the complete email-link-to-authenticated-ledger flow with the user's test address. The app-owned callback performs the one-time verifier exchange before setting session cookies. Production remains unchanged and the branch is not deployable until Step 3 scopes every data operation by owner. Patched Next.js to 16.3.5 for the current critical advisory.

2026-09-20 lifecycle decision: User approved immediate account lock/hide/sign-out on deletion request, a 30-day recovery grace period, and automated permanent purge afterward. Provider backup copies age out on the documented provider schedule. Implementation must include revocation, reauthenticated recovery, idempotent purge, audit state, failed-job handling and release-time disclosure of the current backup schedule.

2026-09-20 implementation step 3A: Queried only the isolated `multi-user-auth` branch and recorded a read-only inventory of 28 sessions and 13 players in `docs/HISTORICAL-OWNERSHIP.md`. Grouped adjacent sessions into 13 review cohorts without assigning ownership. No schema, data or production mutation was performed. Owner mapping is required before the backfill design can be finalized.

2026-09-20 ownership review: User assigned cohorts A–G and J–L to Rajarshi's ledger and cohort M to a separate Emon-led friend group. Abhirup intentionally belongs to both friend lists and must have an independent owner-local profile in each ledger. Cohorts H–I and players Aiush, Ashit and Rana remain unresolved. Ratan is confirmed for Rajarshi's friend list but has no historical database record. The eventual host account for the Emon-led group is not yet known. No backfill is authorized while these decisions remain incomplete.

2026-09-20 implementation step 3B: Added the versioned, expand-only `0001_owner_scope_foundation` migration and rehearsed it on the isolated `multi-user-auth` Neon branch. It introduces account lifecycle records, nullable owner keys, owner-local indexes, migration provenance and audit foundations while preserving compatibility with the global API. Reconciliation retained all 13 players and 28 sessions; zero historical rows were assigned and zero application accounts were fabricated. Evidence is in `docs/MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`. Row-level security, global-constraint removal, route cutover and historical backfill remain later coordinated steps.

2026-09-20 implementation step 3C: Added a server-only account data layer that provisions one internal account from the verified Neon Auth UUID, never from email or client input. All poker APIs now pass through the active-account lifecycle gate, and a minimal no-store account endpoint exposes only lifecycle status. The signed-in local browser provisioned exactly one active account linked to the isolated branch's Auth user; historical ownership remained zero players and zero sessions. This is an authentication/lifecycle boundary only: player and session queries remain global until they are switched together in the next reviewed step, so this branch is still not deployable.

2026-09-20 implementation step 3D: Applied `0002_owner_scoped_keys` to the isolated branch and switched every player/session API operation to the verified internal account ID. Player names, client session IDs and display numbers are now account-local. Session-number allocation uses a per-owner counter and transaction advisory lock so an idempotent retry does not create a duplicate or consume another number. Removed request-time DDL. A two-account database rehearsal verified same-name players, same client IDs, scoped mutations and retry behavior, then removed all fixtures. The signed-in browser now sees an empty private ledger rather than the 13 unowned players and 28 unowned sessions. Historical backfill is next; RLS and restricted runtime credentials remain required before deployment.

2026-09-21 implementation step 3E: Added a parameterized, restartable data migration and cloned only the explicitly approved cohorts A–G and J–L into Rajarshi's isolated private ledger. Created nine owner-local friend profiles including history-free Ratan, copied 24 sessions and 72 results, replaced player IDs with owner-local IDs, recorded eight player and 24 session provenance mappings, and set the next local game number to 25. Full field/result reconciliation passed and an idempotent rerun produced no duplicates. All 13 legacy players and 28 legacy sessions remain intact; H–I and M remain unowned. Browser verification showed the expected private list, standings and history. Production remains unchanged.

2026-09-21 implementation step 3F: Added transaction-local verified-user context to all application database operations and enabled Postgres row-level security for accounts, players, sessions, migration mappings and audit records. The app now uses a least-privilege SQL-created `menoka_app` role; it has no administrative or `BYPASSRLS` capability and cannot read the migration ledger. A real two-account rehearsal verified blocked foreign reads, updates and inserts and confirmed that context is cleared across pooled HTTP connection reuse. After test cleanup, the restricted local browser loaded the expected nine players and 24 sessions. Production remains unchanged. Next: key active-game and legacy browser storage by account, disable automatic unowned uploads and define an explicit adoption flow.

2026-09-21 implementation step 3G: Active games in browser storage are now keyed by the verified internal account ID, so switching accounts on one device cannot load another host's draft. The old global active-game and history keys remain unassigned and are never uploaded during page load. When legacy data exists, the home screen explains its status: an unfinished game can be explicitly adopted only when the account has no current game, while saved sessions open a review dialog where the host checks individual sessions after seeing their date, stakes and players. Only checked sessions are mapped into the current account; unchecked sessions remain unassigned on the device. The unused privileged rehearsal role was deleted with user approval. Production remains unchanged. Next: normalize session results and buy-in events, derive accounting values on the server and add integrity constraints needed by percentage rankings.

2026-09-21 implementation step 3H: Added owner-bound `session_results` and ordered `buy_in_events` records and rehearsed the backfill on the isolated Neon branch. All 24 owned sessions became 72 verified relational results and 72 buy-in events with zero player, event, snapshot or whole-session balance mismatches. Reads now use the relational records; the old JSON is retained only as an immutable compatibility snapshot. New saves validate distinct participants and chip conservation, derive investment and net relationships on the server, and insert the session, results and buy-ins atomically. A restricted-role smoke test saved one temporary game, verified an idempotent retry saved zero duplicates and consumed no second number, then removed every fixture. That test also caught and fixed an account-deletion ordering issue by deferring the player-history foreign-key check until transaction end. Runtime access to accounting tables is limited to select and insert. The local browser still shows 9 players and 24 sessions. Production remains unchanged. Next: replace raw-chip ranking and its default graph with average session return while retaining raw chips as supporting information.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3I: Added the pure `lib/poker/standings.ts` module (metric version 1) and removed the raw-chip `buildLeaderboard`. Players are ranked by the unrounded arithmetic mean of eligible session returns, `100 × (ending − invested) / invested`, including every rebuy. Each session is weighted equally. Returns are computed as one exact-integer division where possible, and summed in sorted order so identical sets of returns tie exactly. Tied scores share a competition rank and are ordered by stable player key. A session is eligible for a player only when `deriveSessionAccounting` verifies the whole session (distinct participants, net matching investment, balanced chips, safe totals) and the player's investment is positive. Otherwise the result is recorded with the reason `unverified-accounting` or `no-investment`. A player with no eligible session gets no score and is listed as unranked after ranked players, never at 0%. Raw net and the profitable-session count cover every recorded session; total invested covers eligible sessions. The standings row shows the score to two decimals with the eligible session count, plus ranked/total sessions, profitable sessions and rate, invested, and raw net in chips. Standings and graph no longer show ₹; game screens and session cards are unchanged. The default graph plots each player's running average from their first eligible session, carries it forward through absences, marks only score changes, and shows session return, running mean and sample count in tooltips. A Raw Chips toggle keeps the cumulative chip view. The graph palette grew from six to eight colours so the current eight ranked players are distinct. Seventeen new tests cover the ranking fixture list; the two legacy leaderboard tests moved into the new suite. Browser verification on the isolated branch at phone, tablet and desktop widths showed 8 ranked players over 24 sessions, both graph views and correct running means, with no horizontal overflow or console errors. The local preview configuration now uses port 3005. No database or production change.

2026-09-24 step 3I follow-up: At the user's request, the per-player hands count is back in the standings. It is the sum of each recorded session's hand count, not hands the player was individually dealt. The standings row was rebuilt for phones: rank, name and score (with the ranked session count) sit on one line, and five labelled stats (ranked/total, hands, profitable, invested, net chips) sit in a grid below. The grid shows three columns on phones, one row of five on tablet and desktop, and two columns at 320px. Verified at 320px, 375px, 768px and desktop with no clipped values or horizontal overflow.

2026-09-24 eligibility decision: The user asked for the most maintainable, easiest-to-debug option. A starting stack must now be at least 1 in both the setup screen and server validation, so the app can no longer save a zero-investment session. `no-investment` stays as a safety net for older or imported data. Hands, profitable sessions, invested, net chips and the raw-chip graph now cover eligible sessions only, instead of mixing eligible and all recorded sessions. The ranked/total count shows what was left out, and each excluded session is listed on the row with a plain-language reason. No current data is affected: all 24 sessions are eligible. A database CHECK on `sessions.starting_stack >= 1` would add defence in depth but needs its own versioned migration and rehearsal; it is optional because the runtime role can only insert through the validated API.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3J: User chose a time window for reauthentication (option A). Removed the shared `DELETE_PASSWORD` check, its header and dialog field, the `passwordMatches` helper and its environment variable. Permanent player and session deletion now call `requireRecentHostAccount`: it requires an active account, as before, and a verified Neon Auth session whose `createdAt` is within 10 minutes, allowing 60 seconds of clock skew. The rule lives in the pure `lib/auth/recent-sign-in.ts` and has unit tests. Otherwise the API returns 403 with code `recent-sign-in-required`, and the UI offers to email a fresh magic link to the signed-in address. The existing ownership, discard-first and saved-history rules and the SQL owner predicates are unchanged. Browser verification on the isolated branch with a 22-minute-old session: both DELETE endpoints returned 403 with the new code, and an old password header had no effect. A discarded throwaway player could not be deleted until a fresh link was used, and the dialog shows the email exactly as entered. Testing exposed an earlier callback bug: a link used while already signed in was answered from the SDK session cache and never exchanged, so the session stayed old. `/auth/callback` now strips existing Neon Auth session cookies before the exchange (`lib/auth/session-cookies.ts`, unit tested). After that fix, a fresh link produced a 9-second-old session and the throwaway player was permanently deleted, leaving 9 players and no discarded players. The README and `.env.example` were rewritten for the authenticated app, the stale `bun.lock` and unused helpers were removed, and `npm run dev` now serves on port 3005. Production and Vercel environment variables are unchanged; remove `DELETE_PASSWORD` from Vercel at cutover.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3K: The user asked to start the account deletion lifecycle. It was split so the request, lock and recovery could be built without the purge decisions. Migration `0006_account_deletion_requests` grants the runtime role SELECT/INSERT on `audit_events`, limited by row policy to its own account and its own actor ID. It also adds the `accounts_guard_lifecycle` trigger: for `menoka_app` it allows only active → deletion requested, stamping the database's `now()` and ignoring any supplied time, and deletion requested → active within 30 days. It rejects backdating and any move to `purging`. `POST /api/account` handles `request-deletion` (active account and a sign-in within 10 minutes) and `recover` (within the grace period, recent sign-in). A request locks the account in the database first, writes an audit event, revokes every Neon Auth session and signs out; revocation failure is logged and cannot reopen the ledger because every ledger API still checks the lifecycle state. Locked accounts get a "Deletion is scheduled" screen with the request time, the exact deadline and a Recover button; the sign-in page confirms the lock after a request. The confirmation discloses the 30-day window and Neon's 6-hour history retention. Rehearsal: twelve checks ran as `menoka_app` inside a rolled-back transaction and all passed, with zero fixtures left. The browser round trip on the isolated branch locked the real dev account, confirmed zero live Auth sessions and 401 ledger APIs, showed the 24 September → 24 October deadline after sign-in, recovered it to 9 players, 24 sessions and 72 results, and recorded `account.deletion_requested → account.deletion_cancelled`. Signing in with a second email during the test created a separate, empty active account, which is kept as a second host for isolation tests. Production unchanged. This version must not be released until Step 3L purges accounts after the deadline.

2026-09-24 purge decisions: The user chose the recommended option in each case. Scheduler: Vercel Cron, daily; the free plan allows one run a day within the scheduled hour, which is adequate for a 30-day deadline. GitHub Actions was rejected because the repository is public and GitHub disables schedules there after 60 days without activity. Auth identity: Neon's documented `DELETE /projects/{project_id}/branches/{branch_id}/auth/users/{auth_user_id}` with a project-scoped API key (Editor access to this project only). Direct SQL on `neon_auth` was rejected as undocumented, and keeping the identity as incomplete deletion. Alerts: Healthchecks.io, because it also catches a job that silently stops running. The user will create the API key, the Healthchecks.io check and the Vercel secrets; agents never handle those values.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3L: Built the automated purge using the recorded decisions. Migration `0007_account_purge` requires an SQL-created, unprivileged `menoka_purge` login with no table privileges, only `EXECUTE` on five `SECURITY DEFINER` functions:
- claim accounts more than 30 days past their request (move to `purging`) and return every unfinished purge
- check whether a Neon Auth user still exists
- record that the identity was deleted
- delete a claimed account whose identity is gone, cascading to everything it owns
- record a failure

Progress lives in `account_purges`, which has no foreign key so it outlives the account; the Auth user ID is cleared on completion. The pure `lib/accounts/purge.ts` runs each account in order: delete the Auth identity through Neon's branch endpoint, confirm in the database that it is gone (no reliance on the provider's status code), then delete the data. A failure stops that account at that step, is recorded, and is resumed the next day without blocking other accounts. Runs handle at most 10 accounts, to stay inside the 60-second function limit. `GET /api/cron/purge-accounts` accepts only `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`, compared in constant time. It reports start/success/fail to Healthchecks.io with counts, internal account IDs and errors only, and returns 500 when any account fails. `vercel.json` schedules it daily at 03:00 UTC.

Rehearsal on the isolated branch:
- **Purge login:** passed 10 permission probes (function calls only; no table, purge log or `neon_auth` access; no direct deletes).
- **Endpoint:** returned 401 without the secret or with a wrong one.
- **Overdue fixture:** a Neon Auth user, an account 31 days past its request, a player and an audit event were purged in one run. The Auth user, sessions, account, player and audit rows are all gone, and the purge log shows completion after 1 attempt with no error.
- **Idempotency:** a second run found nothing due.
- **Your data:** 9 players, 24 sessions and 72 results untouched.
- **Healthchecks.io:** pings sent without errors.

Neon's console cannot reset the password of an SQL-created role that has none, so the role first gets a random in-database password nobody sees, then a reset (see `migrations/README.md`). Production unchanged.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3M (security hardening, part 1): Three request protections, no database change.
- **Same-origin mutations.** `proxy.ts` now also runs for `/api/*`. Any request other than GET, HEAD or OPTIONS must carry `Sec-Fetch-Site: same-origin`, or, for browsers without that header, an `Origin` whose host equals `X-Forwarded-Host` (or `Host`). Anything else, including requests with neither header, gets 403 with code `cross-site-request` before any route code runs. This covers the Neon Auth handler (magic-link requests) and every future API route. The rule is the pure `lib/security/request-origin.ts`.
- **Bounded JSON bodies.** `lib/security/json-body.ts` requires a JSON content type (415 otherwise), rejects a declared or streamed size over the limit (413), and reports malformed JSON as 400 instead of the previous 500. Limits: 16 KB for players, account and discard/restore requests; 2 MB for session saves and imports (Vercel's platform limit is 4.5 MB).
- **Conflicting retries.** A session save whose client ID already exists must describe the same session, compared by `lib/poker/session-conflict.ts`: times, stakes, hands, blind history, resolved player IDs in seat order, ending stacks, nets and buy-ins (a missing buy-in list equals `[startStack]`). Player display names are ignored, and an unnamed incoming session matches the default "Game N" name, so a re-imported export still matches. The check reads existing sessions before any write, so a conflicting request saves nothing and returns 409 with code `session-conflict` and the conflicting IDs. If a concurrent request inserts the same ID between that check and the insert, the route re-reads and compares afterwards; that rare case reports 409 with the number of other sessions that were saved. Duplicate IDs inside one request are now rejected with 400.

Verification: 86 unit tests (14 new), types, lint and build pass. Against a production build on a spare port: POSTs with no provenance headers, a foreign `Origin` or `Sec-Fetch-Site: cross-site` (including to `/api/auth/sign-in/magic-link`) returned 403; same-origin requests reached the routes. In the signed-in browser: a 20 KB player name returned 413, `text/plain` 415, malformed JSON 400; re-sending an existing session returned `saved: 0`; the same session with two ending stacks shifted by one chip returned 409; re-sending all 24 sessions exactly as exported returned `saved: 0`. The ledger stayed at 24 sessions with next game number 25. Remaining hardening: rate limiting (needs a storage decision) and the production log check for the magic-link verifier. Production unchanged.

2026-09-24 implementation step 3N (security hardening, part 2): While checking rate-limit options, the isolated branch's Neon Auth config turned out to still allow **email + password sign-up without email verification** and **sign-in with Neon's shared Google app**, contrary to the magic-link-only decision. That permitted account pre-hijacking: anyone could register a password for someone else's address, and once the real owner signed in by magic link, the same identity would open the ledger by password. The organization plugin is also enabled, but the app never calls it. With the user's approval, only on `multi-user-auth`: `email_and_password` was set to `enabled: false, disable_sign_up: true` and the shared Google provider was removed, through the Neon branch Auth API with the project-scoped key. Neither dev user had a password or Google login (`neon_auth.account` was empty). Calling Neon Auth directly afterwards: password sign-up returned `EMAIL_PASSWORD_SIGN_UP_DISABLED`, password sign-in `EMAIL_PASSWORD_DISABLED`, Google `PROVIDER_NOT_SUPPORTED`, the magic-link endpoint still answered, and no probe user was created (2 users). End to end afterwards, the user signed out and requested a link from a production build on port 3006 (another chat held 3005). The request passed the new same-origin check, the pasted link created a fresh session, and the ledger loaded 24 sessions and 9 players, still with 2 Auth users and no password or OAuth logins. `next start` printed no request lines, so the verifier isn't in production-mode server output; Vercel's own request logs still need checking at the first preview deployment.

Rate limiting: the user chose Vercel Firewall. The Hobby plan allows one rate-limit rule per project, fixed window only, a 10-second to 10-minute window, keyed by IP or JA4 digest, with 1,000,000 allowed requests included. Counters are per region. The project has no firewall configuration yet, and publishing a rule affects the current production deployment, so the rule is written into the go-live checklist rather than applied. In the app, `lib/security/rate-limit-message.ts` turns any 429 (the firewall's or Neon Auth's) into "Too many requests in a short time. Wait a minute, then try again." for ledger, player, account and sign-in-link requests, instead of a generic failure. The 87 tests, types, lint and build pass. Known limit: one IP-keyed rule can't give sign-in emails a stricter per-address limit; Neon's shared email sender is rate-limited itself, and production needs custom SMTP anyway (Neon production checklist).

2026-09-24 implementation step 3O (import/export acceptance): Import now previews before saving. The pure `lib/poker/import-plan.ts` reads either backup shape (`[...]` or `{ sessions: [...] }`). It skips entries whose ID is already in the ledger (active or discarded), keeps the first copy of a repeated ID (the server rejects duplicate IDs since 3M), and counts entries without an ID, results or valid player names as unreadable. It then maps every player in the new games with the server's own rules: a player ID must belong to this ledger; a name alone matches an active or discarded player ignoring case (discarded players stay discarded, as `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` leaves them) or becomes a new player; an unknown player ID blocks the whole import, as the server would. The review dialog lists the counts, each player's mapping and game count, and the new games. Add is disabled when blocked; Cancel sends nothing; a server refusal is shown in the dialog. When nothing is new, the app says "Already up to date" without opening the dialog. Non-goal: choosing a different mapping by hand (for example "Sam" → "Samir"); rename the player or edit the file instead. Six new unit tests, including the export → re-import round trip (zero additions), bring the total to 93; types, lint and build pass. Browser check on a production build (port 3006; another chat held 3005): feeding the Import button a file identical to Export Backup's 24 sessions said "Already up to date" and sent nothing. A mixed file (2 saved, 2 new, 1 repeated, 1 unreadable) showed Abhirup matched by record under an old name, "DEBRAJ" matched to Debraj, and one new player across two games; Cancel left 24 sessions and game number 25. A foreign player record disabled Add with an explanation. An unbalanced game reached the server, which refused it with "Session chips must balance across all players", shown in the dialog, with no sessions or players added. The dialog fits a 375px phone. A short local DNS failure resolving the Neon Auth host briefly returned 401 and 502 during testing; it cleared by itself. No database or production change.

2026-09-24 rebuy change (step 3Q): The user reported that half-size rebuys are unplayable once rising blinds reach a big blind of 1,000 or more (a 5,000 rebuy is five big blinds). Options offered: full starting stack, rebuy in big blinds, early-only rebuys, or a per-game host choice. The user chose the simplest: **every rebuy is the full starting stack**, with no per-game setting. `nextBuyIn` now returns `startStack`, limited to 64 buy-ins per player to match the existing validation and the `buy_in_events.sequence` limit (halving used to stop by itself). The rule lives in the new pure `lib/poker/buy-ins.ts`. Server validation accepts each rebuy that equals the starting stack or half the previous buy-in, so the 24 saved sessions, old backups and games in progress on a device when the rule changed stay valid; none needed a migration, because the database only requires positive rebuys. The standings formula is unchanged; every rebuy still counts as invested. The buy-in hint, rules text and feature list were updated. Five new unit tests (full rebuys, a halved-then-full crossover, the 64 limit, validation of full and crossover sequences and rejection of other amounts) bring the total to 95. Browser check on a production build (port 3006, whose browser storage is separate from 3005's): a throwaway 2-player game was played to a bust, the offer read "Buy In · ₹10,000", confirming it showed 20,000 invested and a 10,000 stack, and the game was discarded unsaved, leaving 24 sessions and game number 25.

2026-09-24 quick bets (step 3S), revised the same day: The user didn't like the quick-amount buttons and asked for a slider up to all in instead, without the separate All In button. Now `betStops` gives the minimum, exact 1.5×, 2×, 5× and 10× multiples, and the whole stack; a range input snaps between them, and the Bet/Raise button follows it, becoming "All In ₹X" (the `all-in` action) at the last stop. Moving off the first stop disables Check/Call and Fold, as a typed amount does; the typed box remains for exact amounts. Checked at 375px on a game already in progress on port 3006 (not started by the agent, left untouched): the stops read ₹100, ₹150, ₹200, ₹500, ₹1,000 and All In ₹9,900 with matching button labels and disabling; the slider was returned to its start. 97 tests. The first version is recorded below. Original: The Bet/Raise button now shows and, with nothing entered, bets the minimum (`minimumRaise`). The pure `betPresets` in `lib/poker/game.ts` gives exact amounts for 1.5×, 2×, 5× and 10× the minimum, rounded to whole chips and deduplicated, and hides any that aren't above the minimum or that reach the stack (All In covers those). The quick buttons fill the amount rather than betting at once, which keeps the existing rule that an entered amount disables Call and Fold. Amounts are chips added by this action, like the typed box. One new unit test brings the total to 96. Browser check at 375px: an under-the-gun player saw ₹300/₹400/₹1,000/₹2,000 and "Raise ₹200"; tapping ₹400 filled the amount, disabled Call and Fold, and raised to ₹400; the next player's plain button raised by the minimum. The throwaway game was discarded. **Open question for the user:** the existing minimum re-raise is one chip above the current bet (`roundHigh + 1`), so after a raise the minimum button shows odd amounts such as ₹351 (to ₹401), and the quick amounts scale from it. Standard poker requires a re-raise at least as large as the previous raise; changing that is a game-rule decision.

2026-09-24 raise sizes (step 3T): Answers the open question from 3S. The user shared a list of common minimum-raise rules; the agent split them into six logics. Already in place: all in for less is always allowed, and minimums reset each street. Missing: a re-raise only had to be ₹1 above the current bet; the 2× big blind pre-flop minimum only applied to under the gun's first action (after a fold or call the next player could raise to BB + ₹1); and a short all-in reopened raising for everyone. The user accepted the agent's recommendation. Accepted behaviour: the minimum raise is the current bet plus the last full raise on this street, starting at the big blind (so pre-flop is always at least 2× BB); a short all-in (raising by less than that) makes others match it but doesn't let players who already acted raise again, so they see only Call and Fold with a note; a later full raise reopens raising; the rules page (section 05), the setup hint, `FEATURES.md` and the action log ("short of a full raise") explain it. Non-goals: adding several short all-ins together to reopen betting (TDA's cumulative rule), which the user skipped; treating a short-posted big blind as a full one. Architecture: `Hand` gains optional `raiseSize` and `raiseOpen`, and `PlayerAction` gains `raiseBefore` for undo. The pure `applyRaiseRules`, `undoRaiseRules`, `resetRaiseRules`, `raiseSize` and `mayRaise` in `lib/poker/game.ts` hold the rules; `act` also refuses a raise from a player whose raising is closed. Migration impact: none on the database (hands live only in browser storage); a hand saved before this change has no fields and falls back to raises of one big blind, open to everyone, until the next street or hand. Known limit: undoing an action out of turn restores that action's own snapshot of the raise rules, which can overwrite a later player's changes; in-turn undo is exact. 105 tests (8 new or rewritten). Browser check on port 3005 with the in-app browser's in-progress Game 25: backed up its saved state, loaded a copy with Debraj short-stacked at ₹450, then played it: Abhirup raised to ₹400; Debraj could only go all in (₹500 total, "short of a full raise"); Pratik, who hadn't acted, could still raise to ₹800; Abhirup then saw only Call and Fold with the note, also checked at 375px. The original saved game was restored exactly afterwards. Follow-up the same day: the user confirmed they don't want the two casino rules the app still lacks (short all-ins adding up to reopen betting, and a short-posted big blind still counting as a full ₹100 call). Open: the "Min Raise ₹X" label shows chips to put in (call plus raise), which the user read as the raise size shrinking; options offered were showing both (recommended), switching to raise-to totals, or renaming the label. The user took the recommendation: the row now reads "min raise to ₹1,300 (₹200 more)" (the "more" part is left out when the player has nothing in yet, and "all in ₹X" replaces it when the stack can't cover the minimum), and the button reads "Raise to ₹X", where X is the chips already in plus the entered amount. The box and slider still take chips to put in, so entry is unchanged. "Raise" now means any bet over a standing bet (`roundHigh > 0`), so the big blind's option reads "Raise to" instead of "Bet". Checked at 375px by loading a test position into the in-app browser's saved game and restoring it afterwards: "To Call ₹300 · Min Raise To ₹1,300 (₹400 More)", button "Raise to ₹1,300", and "Raise to ₹8,800" for a typed ₹7,900 (the button wraps to two lines but fits).

2026-09-24 undo-hand fix (step 3U): The user suspected Undo last hand didn't restore the exact earlier state. Reproduced in the in-app browser on a backed-up copy of Game 25: snapshot when hand 2 was dealt, finished hand 2, undid it, and compared the saved state. The dealer button had moved one seat (Pratik → Abhirup), so the wrong players paid the blinds and all three stacks differed. Cause: undo dealt the hand again from the current dealer instead of the dealer before the undone hand; it also re-read the blind level at the current time, which could raise timed blinds. Fix: the pure `undoLastHand` in `lib/poker/game.ts` replaces the component logic. The completed-hand record (`completedHandRecord`, type `CompletedHand`) now keeps the hand number, deal time and the dealer and blind state from before the deal; undo restores them and deals again at the original time. Hands record `dealtAt`. Records saved before this change fall back to stepping the dealer back one funded seat from the undone hand's dealer, which deals the same positions. Unchanged: only one hand can be undone, and undo is only reachable after the winner card's Next. Five new tests compare the whole game state with a snapshot (plain, next hand already dealt, hand-based and timed rising blinds, a rebuy in between, an old-style record); four of them fail against the old dealer logic. 110 tests, types and lint pass. Browser: after the fix, undoing hand 2 matched the snapshot except for the new `dealtAt` field and the cleared record of hand 1, and undoing hand 2 after hand 3 was dealt and acted on matched the snapshot exactly. The user's game was restored afterwards.

2026-09-24 ranking review (no code change): The user asked whether one lucky game ranks too high under average session return (a single 2-player game won outright scores +100%, while regulars' averages include losses), and which of win % or amount-won % is used. Answer: the rank is average session return (amount won as a percentage of chips put in, averaged per game); "Profitable" shows game win %, and hand win % isn't recorded. Known weaknesses: small samples rank fully, and the maximum possible return grows with table size. Options offered: minimum games, pulling small samples toward zero, table-size adjustment, ranking by game win %, recording hand wins. The agent recommended the small-sample adjustment. The user kept the current formula and asked to save the small-sample formula as a future option (see the Pending choices table). Noted but not changed: the Profitable percentage divides ranked profitable games by all games played.

Both lists now use one `ExpandingList` component in `components/poker-ledger.tsx` in place of `<details>`: 5 items at first, 10 more per press, with the button always after the last visible item. It shows "N left" only when more than 10 remain, becomes "Show fewer" (back to 5) once everything is shown, and scrolls itself back into view after collapsing. Checked at 375px on the user's ledger (8 ranked players, 24 games): games went 5 → 15 → 24 with the button after the last card each time, "Show fewer" returned to 5 with the button still on screen, players went 5 → 8, and no horizontal overflow. Types and lint pass.
