The redesign follows the handoff in `design_handoff_sporty_glass_redesign/` (direction 1a, Big Shoulders + Barlow, dark and light themes, green and blue accents, glass cards, five-tab bar). Game rules, APIs and persistence are unchanged. Where the prototype was simpler than the app, the app's behaviour was kept and restyled, by the long-term choice rule: free-form stack and big blind (preset buttons plus "Other"), the full blind-plan options (By hands/By minutes with interval, multiply or add), drag and keyboard seat reordering under the tap-to-seat chips, the "Deal Flop" step instead of automatic street advance, per-player Undo, the typed bet amount, rebuys, the between-hands card, the winner overlay, discarded lists and permanent deletion. New UI-only behaviour: theme toggle saved in `localStorage` (`menoka-theme`, applied before paint), Standings and Game Sessions as separate screens, Hand Rankings as a screen instead of a sheet, quick bet sizes (Min, ½ Pot, Pot, All in), seat status pills, collapsible standings rows (4 shown; the handoff's season-leader card was removed at the user's request; at the user's request the session count moved from under the name into the details as "Sessions", ordered Sessions, Profitable, Hands, Invested, Net chips, and an "i" button beside the heading explains the ranking; the graph's Raw chips view was removed, so it shows average return only), chart legend highlighting, a client-side duplicate-name message, and the action log trimmed to 6 lines with "Show all". The blinds pill uses 22px display type rather than the handoff's 15px, keeping the 2026-09-24 request for larger blinds. Player colours use the handoff's named hues with a stable name-based hue for everyone else. Checks: types, lint, 110 tests, production build; browser checks at 375px and 320px on the dev server and a production build (port 3006), dark and light, without saving, adding or acting in the in-progress games found in both origins' storage. Not yet checked: the sign-in and locked-account pages, the modals, the winner overlay and dragging, by touch on a real phone. No database, auth or production change.

2026-09-25 next-street button (redesign branch): The button under the seats read "Finish the betting round" while players still had to act, which looked like a permanently dead control. The user briefly had streets advance automatically, then reverted that: the manual button stays, and it now always reads "Deal FLOP/TURN/RIVER →", faded and disabled until the round ends. To keep it under the same finger, the user first tried pinning it above the tab bar, disliked that, and chose instead to keep the card of the player whose action closed the round open (locked and faded, Undo still available) until Deal is pressed. The closer is found from the newest log line matching a player's last action, so no game-state field was added; a closing fold keeps its seat position until the next street. The active card also always reserves the "Clear amount" line's space, so every open card is the same height. Checked on a production build at 375px with the page's storage writes blocked (reload restored the saved game): the locked card and the next active card both measured 306px. No rule change.

2026-09-25 live standings link (branch `live-view`, from the committed redesign `ed56525`): **Problem:** only the host's phone holds the game in progress, so players ask the host for their stacks. **User decisions:** a share link or QR code that anyone with it can open without signing in (players signing in was offered and declined); the page shows this game's standings only (the live table, all-time standings and the action log were offered and declined); about 5-second updates by polling (a realtime service was declined); the `qrcode-generator` package (MIT, no dependencies, already in the lockfile through another package) is allowed. **Agent choice, shown to the user before building:** stacks are live, but net and rank use each player's stack at the start of the current hand (`hand.stacksBeforeHand`), so they change when a hand ends. **Accepted behaviour:** see `FEATURES.md` section 19. **Architecture:** migration `0009_live_views` adds `live_views`, one row per host (`owner_id` primary key, cascades on account deletion so the purge covers it). It stores only the SHA-256 of a 32-byte base64url token, the server-derived standings, and `expires_at` at most 12 hours after `updated_at` (a check constraint). Owner row security for `menoka_app` follows the other tables. Public reads go through `read_live_view(token_hash)`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function granted only to `menoka_app`. It returns the standings and time only while the link hasn't expired and the owner's account is `active`. `POST/PUT/DELETE /api/live` (host session, same-origin, 16 KB body limit) start or replace, update, and stop a share; start and stop write `live_view.started` / `live_view.stopped` audit events. The pure `lib/poker/live-view.ts` builds the device snapshot (names, live and settled stacks, buy-ins; no player IDs, log or cards), validates it on the server (names, safe whole numbers, 2–10 players, first buy-in equal to the starting stack, rebuys by `isValidRebuy`, at most 64 buy-ins, live stacks equal to settled ones between hands and never above them during a hand), and derives standings through `deriveSessionAccounting`, so settled chips must balance against buy-ins exactly as a saved game must. Ties share a rank and are ordered by name. `GET /api/live/[token]` is public and returns 404 "This game has ended" for any unknown, stopped or expired link. It sends `Cache-Control: public, s-maxage=2, stale-while-revalidate=3` so many phones share one database read, plus `no-referrer` and `noindex`. The `/live/[token]` page is outside the proxy's sign-in matcher. The host's device keeps the token under `pokerLedger.account.<id>.live.v1`. It sends updates after 1 second of quiet and at least 3 seconds apart, plus a heartbeat after 60 seconds without one. That keeps it far below the 30-writes-per-minute firewall rule, which counts only non-GET requests; public polling uses GET. Saving or discarding a game stops the share. **Non-goals:** changing the game from the link; the live table or action log; realtime push; ending the link on sign-out (the game stays on the device and can be resumed, and the link still expires after 12 hours). **Testing found:** without a heartbeat, a slow hand would have shown the "host may be offline" warning after 30 seconds. The fix is the one-minute heartbeat and a 2½-minute warning threshold. **Checks:** 19 new unit tests (129 total), types, lint, a production build (run in a copy, because another chat's `next start` on port 3006 uses `.next`). Migration 0009 was applied to `multi-user-auth` (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`) and rehearsed: 12 of 12 checks passed as `menoka_app`, see `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`. In the browser, the throwaway game on port 3007 (a copy of the working tree, since Next allows one dev server per folder and another chat held 3005) was checked at 375px in dark and light. Checked there:
- create link, QR and URL;
- the viewer with no session cookie;
- live stack after a raise, and net and rank after the hand;
- an all-in bust and a full rebuy (1 rebuy, ₹20,000 in, −10,000);
- the highlight;
- the heartbeat (update age reset every 60–75 s);
- Stop sharing → "This game has ended";
- a new link works while the old one stays 404;
- discarding → 404 and no `live_views` rows left;
- unauthenticated PUT → 401 and cross-site DELETE → 403.

No game was saved. **Not yet checked:** a real phone (iOS Safari share sheet, QR scanning, background tabs), a Vercel preview, and CDN caching behaviour on Vercel. **Production:** unchanged; applying 0009 to `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` needs the user's go-ahead. **Rollback:** `DROP FUNCTION read_live_view(bytea); DROP TABLE live_views;` removes only live links.

2026-09-25 release (redesign + live standings): The user confirmed the paused release at the start of the next session. `main` fast-forwarded from `a057ca2` to `13f9838` (the redesign `ed56525`, the live standings link and its two phone-test fixes) and was pushed; Vercel's production build was Ready in 51 seconds. No database step was needed, because migration 0009 was already on `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`. On the live site, the redesigned sign-in page loaded with no console errors, and `GET /api/live/<made-up token>` returned 404 "This game has ended" with `no-store`.

2026-09-25 friend network (accepted): **Status:** the user accepted the model on 2026-09-25 and asked to proceed with the build steps below. Every open question is answered (two rounds of user decisions below). It replaces the earlier "read-only members first" phasing from `HANDOFF.md`. Decisions are marked **User decided**; everything else is the agent's recommendation.

**Problem.** Today each host keeps a private list of friends who are only names. **User's direction:**
- the app stays behind sign-in;
- people find each other by user ID and send a friend request; once accepted, they are connected and in each other's friend list;
- a host can still add a player who hasn't signed up; that player gets a unique ID;
- when that person later creates an account, they ask the host who recorded their games to link that history to their account;
- today's players (such as the 9 in Rajarshi's ledger) join the same way: they sign in with their email and connect their account to their existing player;
- the goal is a graph of friends, designed as one model now, because the app will later become a mobile app.

The user dropped the earlier ideas of public all-time standings, read-only members as a separate first phase, and host-sent invite links.

**Starting facts.**
- Every magic-link sign-in already provisions its own account and empty ledger (`lib/accounts/server.ts`). So a new user can already host their own games; what's missing is the connection between people.
- Accounts hold no display name or user ID, only the Neon Auth identity (email). Player profiles belong to exactly one host (`players.owner_id`).
- Row security ties every table to `current_app_account_id()` (`migrations/0004_database_enforced_isolation.sql`). Any view of another account's data needs new, narrowly scoped `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, like `read_live_view` in `0009_live_views.sql`.
- The firewall rule "Limit API writes" counts only non-GET `/api/*` requests.

**One mechanism for everyone (agent's observation).** An existing player and a future host-added player are the same case: a player profile in some host's list with no login. So no special migration is needed for today's players. They sign in, and the host links them through the same request-and-approve step.

**Model.**
- **People are accounts.** The graph's nodes are accounts. Player profiles stay host-owned, so each host keeps their own names, history and standings exactly as today. Linking never moves or copies history.
- **User ID. User decided: an app-generated code.**
  - Each account gets a random code, for example `7KQ4-M2XP`, and a display name the person chooses.
  - Recommended: 8 characters from an unambiguous alphabet (no 0/O or 1/I/L), unique, and replaceable by its owner if it leaks.
  - Searching finds only an exact code and shows only the display name. There is no browsing, name search or directory, and emails are never shown to other users. Search is a POST, so the firewall's write limit also slows guessing.
- **Player ID.**
  - Every player profile gets its own short code, visible to that player's host, for players who haven't signed up.
  - The host shares it with the person so their request can say "I'm your player `P-…`".
  - It is a convenience, not a key: nothing links without the host's approval.
- **Friend request.**
  - A sends B a request by B's user code.
  - A decides A's own side at sending time: B becomes a new player in A's list, or is linked to A's existing player for B.
  - A may also add "I'm your player `P-…`" to claim a profile in B's list.
- **Accepting. User decided: the host approves every link.** B sees the request and chooses Accept or Decline. On Accept, B decides B's own side: link A to one of B's existing players (preselected when A gave a valid player code), or add A as a new player. A wrong claim is simply declined or pointed at the right player. Ownership is never inferred from names, stakes or chip history.
- **After accepting. User decided: both friend lists.**
  - The two accounts are connected, and each appears as a linked player in the other's friend list, so either can host the other.
  - Each side's choice of which existing player to link prevents duplicates.
- **Request limits. User decided:** at most 20 outgoing pending requests per person; after a decline, the same person can't send another request to the same person for 7 days.
- **Links.**
  - A profile links to at most one account, and an account to at most one profile per host.
  - A host can unlink a profile later, for example after a mistake, and link it again through a new request.
- **Group standings. User decided:** besides their own ledger, a signed-in person sees the standings list of every host whose ledger has them as a linked player: all players ranked, exactly as that host sees it. That shows how they do in each group.
  - The standings are computed on the server by `lib/poker/standings.ts` from that host's data, using that host's names for the players.
  - It covers only the standings list, not the host's game history, graph, discarded items or anything editable.
  - Access ends as soon as the link or the connection ends.
- **No combined score across hosts. User decided:** it would only create confusion; each group's standings already show how a person does in that group.
- **Leaving and deletion.**
  - **User decided:** removing a friend ends the connection and unlinks both sides' profiles; each host keeps their history.
  - **User decided:** a person sees which hosts have them linked, and can unlink themselves from any of them.
  - Deleting an account removes its codes, requests, links and connections. Other hosts' history stays, because the profiles are theirs.
  - The 30-day purge must cover the new tables, and the deletion disclosure must say so.
- **Legacy claims** (the Emon-led group and Rahul Basak) are unchanged. Those hosts first get their games through `claim_reviewed_history`, then connect with friends like anyone else.
- **Later phases (named, not designed):** co-hosts recording games for another host; cloud game handoff between devices; push notifications; showing your user code as a QR code for the mobile app.
- **Mobile-ready.** Every step is an API with typed contracts.

**Non-goals.** A combined score or screen across hosts, public profiles or search by name, groups or clubs, co-hosting, merging two hosts' histories into one ledger, changing the ranking formula, invite links by email or SMS, real-money features.

**Migration impact (to plan, not to do).**
- Accounts gain a user code and a display name. Existing accounts need codes on first use or in a backfill.
- Players gain a player code and an optional linked account (unique per owner).
- New tables: friend requests (pending, accepted, declined or cancelled, with each side's mapping) and connections.
- All new data gets owner row security and cascades on account deletion. `SECURITY DEFINER` functions cover the cross-account steps: code search, sending, accepting and reading linked results. Each returns only what the viewer may see.
- Versioned migration 0010+, rehearsal on `multi-user-auth` (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`) with at least three accounts (host, friend, stranger), then the user's go-ahead for each production step on `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`. A rollback plan with each migration.

**Acceptance criteria (draft).**
- A stranger can't see a host's players, games or standings, even with a guessed user code, player code or IDs. Search reveals only a display name for an exact code.
- A player code alone links nothing. Accepting one request twice, or two requests for the same profile, links exactly one account.
- Neither side can change the other's players, games or standings. Each side's list changes only by that side's own choice at send or accept time.
- Unlinking, unfriending or deleting either account immediately removes cross-account access. The purge removes the new rows.
- Existing two-account isolation tests and every game flow still pass.
- `FEATURES.md` documents every limit, such as code format, pending-request limits and what a linked person sees.

**User decisions, round 2 (2026-09-25).**
1. A linked person sees each linked host's standings list, next to their own ledger (see Group standings).
2. Removing a friend unlinks both sides.
3. No combined cross-host view or score at all: the user said it would only create confusion.
4. The request limits (20 outgoing pending, 7 days after a decline) are accepted.
5. A person sees which hosts have them linked and can unlink themselves.

**Proposed build steps (each one reviewed, rehearsed and released separately).**
1. **Identity basics.** Migration 0010: account user codes and display names, player codes, and the per-owner optional linked account on players. A profile section to set a display name and see or replace your code, and player codes on the host's player rows. There is no cross-account access yet.
2. **Friend requests.** Migration 0011: requests and connections, with exact-code search (POST), send, accept or decline with each side's player choice, cancel, the request limits, unfriend (unlinks both sides) and self-unlink.
3. **Group standings.** A `SECURITY DEFINER` read function returning a linked host's standings data only while the viewer is linked and connected and both accounts are active, plus a screen listing each group's standings.

Each step extends the purge, the deletion disclosure, `FEATURES.md` and the isolation tests (host, friend, stranger), and is applied to production only with the user's go-ahead.

2026-09-25 friend network step 1 (identity basics): Built as the first of the three accepted build steps; not released. **Change from the step list:** the players' optional linked-account column moves to step 2, where friend requests first use it, so this migration adds nothing unused.

- **Migration `0010_identity_codes`:**
  - `public.new_identity_code()` makes eight characters from `23456789ABCDEFGHJKMNPQRSTUVWXYZ` using fully random `gen_random_uuid()` bytes (modulo 31, a negligible bias), executable only by `menoka_app`.
  - Accounts gain a unique `user_code` (format check) and an optional `display_name` (1–40 characters, trimmed). Players gain a unique `player_code`.
  - Existing rows get their own codes when the columns are added. Row security is unchanged, so nobody can see another account's code or name yet.
  - Deleting an account removes its codes and name with the row, so the purge needs no change.
- **API:**
  - `GET /api/account` now also returns `profile` (`userCode`, `displayName`) for an active account.
  - `POST /api/account` accepts `update-profile` (validated by the pure `cleanDisplayName`, which accepts only text) and `replace-code` (writes an `account.user_code_replaced` audit event). Locked accounts get 423, as on other routes.
  - `GET/POST/PATCH /api/players` return each player's `code`.
- **UI (Players screen):**
  - a **You** card with the user code shown as `XXXX-XXXX`, **Copy**, **Replace** (with a confirmation) and a name field with **Save**;
  - each active player's code as `P-XXXX-XXXX` under their name, tap to copy.
  - At 320px the Copy and Replace buttons move under the code together.
  - The pure helpers live in `lib/accounts/identity-code.ts`.
- **Checks:**
  - 5 new unit tests (134 in total), types, lint and a production build (run in a copy).
  - The rehearsal on `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` passed 12 of 12 checks as `menoka_app` (see `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`).
  - Browser check on a copy of the working tree on port 3007 (another chat held 3005), at 375px and 320px in dark and light:
    - profile load;
    - name save with spaces tidied;
    - replace with confirmation;
    - invalid names refused with 400;
    - player codes on all 9 rows;
    - no horizontal scroll.
  - Testing caught a bug, since fixed: a name sent as an object was saved as "[object Object]"; `cleanDisplayName` now accepts only text.
  - The in-app browser blocks the clipboard, so Copy (which silently leaves the code on screen) still needs a real-phone check.
  - The dev account now has the name "Rajarshi Roy" and one code replacement.
- **Release order:** 0010 is additive, so production gets it before the code (the released code ignores the new columns). The new code needs it, because the players list reads `player_code`. The preview branch `br-tiny-forest-ayt6f3fe` also needs it before a Vercel preview works.

2026-09-25 friend network step 2 (friend requests): Built as the second accepted build step; not released. The user asked to release steps 1 and 2 together.

- **Agent's choices (the user's rule: long-term, easy to debug):**
  - **One action removes both sides.** Every friendship has exactly one linked player on each side, so "remove a friend" and "unlink myself from a host" are the same action, **Remove**: it ends the friendship and unlinks both sides, and each host keeps their players and games. A host who linked the wrong player removes the friend and accepts a new request.
  - **Each side chooses its own player.** The sender picks theirs at send time; the recipient picks theirs, or names a new player, at accept time. A player code only preselects; it never links on its own.
  - **A new player on the sender's side** takes the recipient's display name, numbered ("Name (2)") on a clash, so accepting never fails because of the sender's list.
  - **Both people need a display name** to send or accept, so requests always show who they're from.
  - **No notifications.** Requests appear when the Players screen opens or on **Refresh**.
- **Migration `0011_friend_requests`:**
  - `players.linked_account_id` (unique per owner, never the owner, set to NULL when that account is deleted);
  - `friend_requests` and `friend_connections`, with row security on and no grants;
  - seven `SECURITY DEFINER` functions: `friend_find`, `friend_send`, `friend_overview`, `friend_accept`, `friend_decline`, `friend_cancel` and `friend_remove`. Each derives the caller from the verified session and requires an active account; refusals are `friend:<code>` errors;
  - the `players_guard_links` trigger, which stops the runtime role from setting or changing links or deleting a linked player.
  - Limits: 20 pending sent requests, one pending request per pair, and 7 days after a decline.
  - Locked accounts are hidden from search, requests and friend lists. The purge needs no change, because deletion cascades.
- **API:**
  - `GET /api/friends` returns the overview.
  - `POST /api/friends` takes `find`, `send`, `accept`, `decline`, `cancel` and `remove`. Codes are parsed by the pure `lib/friends/requests.ts`; a player code typed into Find gets its own message.
  - `/api/players` returns `linked`, and refuses to delete a linked player (409).
- **UI (Players screen):**
  - a **Friends** card under **You**: Find by code; "In your list, they are" and an optional player code; the send button; received requests with the claim note, player choice, new-player name, Accept and Decline; friends with "Your player X · you're Y in their list" and Remove (with a confirmation); sent requests with Cancel; and Refresh.
  - Player rows show "· friend", and linked players have no Delete button.
- **Checks:**
  - 5 new unit tests (139 in total), types and lint.
  - Rehearsal on `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` as `menoka_app` with host, friend and stranger accounts: 40 of 40 passed, plus an owner-side purge cascade check (see `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`). The first apply failed safely on a check that refused legacy players with no owner; it was fixed and rerun.
  - Browser round trip on a copy on port 3007 at 375px and 320px, with a temporary "Test Friend" fixture account acting through the same functions:
    - accept with a claimed player (Debraj preselected);
    - friend listed, and "· friend" on the row;
    - Remove with a confirmation, unlinking both sides;
    - Find answers: friends, self, a player code, unknown and malformed codes;
    - send with a chosen player and a claimed code;
    - the fixture accepting, linking Debraj and its own player;
    - Cancel and Decline, including an unmatched claim note.
  - Fixes found by testing: Refresh now also reloads the player list, and error bodies no longer repeat the status.
  - Afterwards the fixture was deleted and the branch is back to 2 accounts, 22 players, no links, requests or friendships.
  - **Not tested:** two real sign-ins in two browsers; the fixture played the second person through the database. The Vercel preview is the place for that.
- **Release (with the user's go-ahead for each production step):**
  1. apply 0010, then 0011, to `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`; both are additive, so the live code keeps working;
  2. push `main`.
  - A Vercel preview first needs both migrations on `br-tiny-forest-ayt6f3fe`.

2026-09-25 friend network step 3 (group standings): Built as the last of the three accepted build steps; not released. The user asked to release all three together, after a preview test. **Preview test of steps 1–2 (before this step):** the user signed in on the preview with two more emails ("Debraj Test" and "Saheb") and sent requests to the host account. The host accepted both. The preview database showed "Debraj", who has history, linked to Debraj Test; a new "Saheb" player; and "Rajarshi" linked in both friends' lists. That gave 2 friendships, and the 27 games were untouched. The user then noticed that the friend can't see the host's rankings yet, which is this step.

2026-09-25 friend network release: The user checked group standings on the preview as Debraj Test ("Rajarshi's games" with the 27-game standings and Debraj marked You) and asked to release all three steps.
- **Baseline on production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`:** migrations 0001, 0002 and 0004–0009; 1 account, 24 players (9 owned), 27 owned games, 82 results, and no open live links.
- **Migrations:** 0010, 0011 and 0012 applied in that order, one transaction each, without errors. Afterwards: every account and player has its own code; the counts are unchanged; there are no links; row security is on for `friend_requests` and `friend_connections`; `menoka_app` has no table access to them but can run the friend functions; `menoka_purge` can't.

2026-09-25 name on first sign-in: **User decision:** saving a name is the first thing a person does after signing in with email; it is not optional or for later. A full onboarding flow is future work, not designed. **Behaviour:** `app/page.tsx` reads the profile on the server for an active account; with no display name it renders `components/name-setup.tsx` (a "What should friends call you?" card in the sign-in page's style, with the "Signed in as" bar and Sign out) instead of the ledger. Saving uses the existing `update-profile` action and `cleanDisplayName` (1–40 characters), then refreshes the page, which now opens the ledger. Locked accounts still see the locked screen first. Existing accounts without a name, including the live production account, get the screen on their next visit. The APIs are unchanged: the gate is the page, and friend actions already require a name on the server. **Checks:** types, lint and tests pass. In the browser (a copy on port 3007 with the gate forced, because the auth-mode classifier blocked clearing the dev account's name in the database), the screen was checked at 375px and 320px: Save stays disabled for a blank or spaces-only name, enables with text, and nothing scrolls sideways. The user then signed in on a fresh dev server (port 3005) as roystark24@gmail.com, which had no name on `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`, got the screen, saved a name and landed in the app. No production change.

2026-09-30 player codes removed from the UI (user decision): the user asked why a guest has a code at all. A player code only preselected the matching guest on the accept card; the host already picks any guest from "In your list, they are", and that choice is what links. The second code confused people, so the app no longer shows it: no "Copy invite code" on guests, no "Your invite code from them" field when sending a request, and `POST /api/friends` `send` ignores `claimedPlayerCode` and passes `NULL` to `friend_send`. Kept on purpose, so it's easy to undo and needs no migration: `players.player_code`, the `friend_send` claim parameter, the accept card's claim wording for requests sent before the change, and the Find message for a pasted P- code (reworded to point at the Profile card). Acceptance: lint, tsc and tests pass; the guest menu and the Add Friend sheet show no player code; sending and accepting with an existing guest still links. Status: built, uncommitted. Verified end to end on the testbed (3006): Farhan sent Bikram a request (201) with his guest "Dev C." chosen and no code field shown; Bikram's card read "Farhan Solo wants to be friends"; he chose guest "Aditi T." and pressed Accept and link. Bikram's list then showed Farhan Solo · On Menoka · 32 games (Aditi T.'s history and net kept), and Farhan's list showed Bikram in place of Dev C., with the 32 games on Farhan's card. A pasted P- code in Find showed the new message. The testbed keeps this state until `npm run testbed:reset` and `testbed:seed`.

2026-10-01 link a guest after accepting (user decision): sending a friend request had an optional "Already in your list?" picker. The user asked whether the receiver could choose instead; they can't, because each person links only players in their own list and the lists are private. Chosen over preselecting a same-name guest: sending is one tap with no picker, and the sender links a guest afterwards. **Built:** migration `0014_link_guest.sql` adds `friend_link_guest(friend_account_id, guest_player_id)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, granted to `menoka_app`): it requires a friendship, a guest of the caller's that is active, unlinked, not the own player and not on a pending request, moves the friend link to that guest and deletes the previously linked player. **Merging (user decision, same day):** if the previously linked player already has games, they move to the guest first: `session_results` and `buy_in_events` in one statement (so the buy-ins' foreign key is checked after both move) and each `poker_sessions.results` entry's `playerId`; the stored `player_name` of each result is kept. The sheet warns that this can't be undone and the button reads Merge. Refused with `friend:same-game` when both played in the same game, and in the browser while the friend is seated in this device's game in progress (a game in progress on another device would fail to save; accepted as rare). `POST /api/friends` gains `link-guest`; `send` always passes `NULL` for the player (the parameter stays). UI: the Add sheet has no picker; friend rows get **Link to guest…** with a small sheet. The accept card is unchanged. **Later the same day (user decision):** the accept card's picker and its "says they're X in your list" wording were removed too, so Accept always makes a new player and linking happens only through Link to guest…; `friend_accept` and `POST /api/friends` still take a player id, which the app no longer sends. Acceptance: lint, tsc, tests and build pass; on the testbed, linking a guest removes the duplicate and keeps games, a merge keeps every result, buy-in and net; the refusal cases hold. Status: built, uncommitted. Committed as `2721c36`. 0014 applied to the testbed (`br-young-night-b5xphgw8`) and rehearsed and applied on dev (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`, 2026-10-01; details in `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`), then applied to production (`br-small-sea-ayumyssr`, 2026-10-01, user's go-ahead; counts unchanged). Checked there: Farhan linked guest Tony to Bikram (list 3 → 2, "Guest Linked"); a request to Asha sent with no picker; a rollback-only rehearsal as the owner gave not-friends, player-unavailable for another owner's player, the own player, a linked guest and a guest on a pending request, has-games when the linked player had 32 games, and a successful link that kept those 32 games and deleted the empty player; as `menoka_app` the function runs (grant present). 2026-10-02 same-name guest on the accept card (user request): accepting failed with "You already have a player with that name. Pick them…", though the card no longer had a picker. 2026-10-01 avatars (user decisions, built, not released): **Problem:** players have no picture, and the user doesn't want photo uploads. **User decisions:** cartoon avatars, one given to everyone by default; people with an account choose their own and every host sees that choice (the same rule as names), and hosts choose for their guests; no initials option; avatars show everywhere a player shows, including the public live link. The user first asked for an avatar CDN. DiceBear's hosted API is free only for non-commercial use (50 requests a second, no uptime promise; it recommends self-hosting), which conflicts with the planned App Store launch, and the styles the user liked (big-smile, adventurer) are CC BY and need a credit line. **Chosen (user's request):** original drawings in that general style, drawn in code, so there is no outside service, licence or network call. 20 cartoon people: six skin tones, varied hair, glasses, beards, caps and headbands, two with a hijab and two with a turban (added at the user's request). **Built:** `lib/avatars.ts` (the list; ids `p01`–`p20` are stored, so an id is never reused for a different drawing; a stable stand-in from the name for unknown ids and games saved without ids) and `components/avatar-art.tsx` (the SVG). Migration `0015_avatars.sql`: `accounts.avatar` and `players.avatar` (`NOT NULL`, format checked, default `public.random_avatar()`, so every existing row got its own random pick); `shown_avatar(owner, player)` (internal) gives a linked active friend's own choice, the host's choice for their own player, otherwise the stored one; `player_avatars()` (granted to `menoka_app`) returns it for the caller's list; `friend_find`, `friend_overview` and `friend_group_sessions` are re-created with avatars added and are otherwise unchanged. `POST /api/account` gains `set-avatar`; `PATCH /api/players` gains `avatar` for guests only (409 for a friend or your own player); both accept only ids in `lib/avatars.ts`. The live snapshot carries each seat's avatar; the server drops unknown ids. Group standings rows carry an avatar but still no player ids. **UI:** your card's avatar opens a picker sheet (tap saves); guests' ⋯ menu has **Change avatar**; the drawing replaces the initial on your card, the player list (friends keep a green-and-blue ring), friend request cards, the find result, Table Setup's seating order, every seat, the buy-in and split choices, the Ranks cards and the live link. **Not changed:** chart colours per player (still from the name). **Acceptance:** lint, tsc, 171 tests and a build pass; on the testbed (`br-young-night-b5xphgw8`, 0015 applied through `npm run testbed:setup`): every account and player got a valid avatar (15 different ones across 30 players); your own and a guest's avatar change in the browser and show on the card, the list, Table Setup, the table (with D/SB/BB badges) and the live link; unknown ids and the own player are refused by the guest route; as each persona, friends, requests and every group result carry an avatar, and friends and own players show the account's choice. **Not yet checked:** the Ranks screen in the browser, a friend's group in the browser, a real phone. Committed as `74a5bca`. 0015 rehearsed and applied on dev `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` (2026-10-01, user's go-ahead; details in `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`). Applied to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (2026-10-01, user's go-ahead): counts unchanged, every account and player has a valid avatar, and all 14 owned players show the expected one (the 15 unowned legacy players are never shown). **Released** 2026-10-01 as `340ea1b` with the user's go-ahead (Vercel success; signed-out checks: 401 for the APIs and both avatar writes, 403 cross-site, 404 for a made-up live link).

2026-10-01 avatar at sign-up (user request, released as `7e136bc`): **Request:** choose an avatar while saving the name on the first-sign-in name screen, and while adding a guest. **Built:** `components/avatar-picker.tsx`, a select-only grid of the 20 avatars (tapping selects; the form saves). The name screen (`components/name-setup.tsx`) starts on the account's current (random) avatar, passed from `app/page.tsx`, and sends it with `update-profile`; its description paragraph is removed (user request), leaving the heading, name box and grid; `POST /api/account` accepts an optional `avatar` there (checked against `lib/avatars.ts`, 400 otherwise) and `updateDisplayName` saves it in the same update (`COALESCE`, so leaving it out keeps the current one). The Add sheet's **Add Guest** mode shows the picker under the name box with a random avatar selected; `POST /api/players` accepts an optional checked `avatar` (without one the database still picks at random) and, when it brings back a removed guest of the same name, sets that guest's avatar to the one picked (never a linked friend's). **No migration.** **Acceptance:** tsc, lint and tests pass; on a copy on port 3007 against the testbed: as Bikram, a guest added with a chosen avatar was stored with it (`p07`, the testbed keeps "Zoya Guest"); bad avatars are refused by both routes; `update-profile` with an avatar saves it and without one keeps it (Bikram's avatar was restored); as the "new" persona the name screen shows the grid with the current avatar ringed and selection moves on tap (not saved, so the persona stays nameless). **Not yet seen:** a real phone.

2026-10-05 Lorelei avatars (user request: "not really liking the avatar options"). **Explored:** DiceBear styles rendered locally from the library (no CDN): Big Smile and Adventurer (CC BY, need a credit line), Lorelei and Notionists (CC0). **User decisions:** Lorelei, in the "Bright" colouring (the app's six skin tones, natural and playful hair colours, coloured glasses, earrings and flowers); 15 men and 5 women ("poker is mostly played by males"); no bald faces; no hijab or turban (Lorelei has none; drawing them by hand was offered and declined); every existing account and player gets a random male face, since everyone using the app today is a man. **Built:** `lib/avatars.ts` now lists the 20 faces (same ids `p01`–`p20`; each entry names its Lorelei hair, skin, hair colour, beard, glasses, earrings, flowers and, for men, plain eyes without lashes and a closed or grinning mouth, because the seeded eyes and pink lips made several men read as women). `scripts/generate-avatars.ts` (`npm run avatars:generate`) writes `public/avatars/<id>.svg` with `@dicebear/core` and `@dicebear/lorelei` 9.4.x, pinned as dev dependencies only; `AvatarArt` is now an `<img>` of that file (about 8 KB each, 164 KB in all), so the app ships no DiceBear code and calls no service. `test/avatars.test.ts` fails if a committed SVG differs from the generator's output or the split stops being 3 in 4 men. The hand-drawn SVG code is gone. **Data:** migration `0021_male_avatars.sql` copies every account's and player's avatar into `avatar_backup_0021` (row security on, no grants), gives each a random male id, checks none is left female, and records itself; a rerun changes nothing. `random_avatar()` is unchanged, so new accounts and guests still get any of the 20 (3 in 4 male). **Acceptance:** tsc, lint, 257 tests and a production build pass; 0021 applied to the testbed (`br-young-night-b5xphgw8`, via the Neon connector so the running testbed server's credentials stayed valid): 7 accounts and 33 players, all 40 backed up, none left female, 14 different faces among players; in the browser at 375 px the Ranks cards, Profile card and avatar picker show the new faces with no console errors and every file served (200). **Released 2026-10-05** with the user's go-ahead: 0021 rehearsed on dev `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` (3 accounts, 23 players, rerun changed nothing) and applied to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (7 accounts, 39 players, 46 rows backed up, none left female), then the code pushed. **Not yet:** a real phone; dropping `avatar_backup_0021` once settled.

2026-10-01 home redesign (user request, built, not committed): **Source:** `Menoka Card Games Redesign/HomeScreen.dc.html` (designs 4a–4c) and `ProfileScreen.dc.html` (account ⋯ menu). **User decisions:** follow 4a, 4b and 4c but leave out **Table leaders**; take the design language without changing what the app does where the design doesn't fit the feature set. **Built:** Home has the greeting and your name, one game card (Start a game, or the Live card with pot, blinds and level, your stack or the player count, chip leader, Back to the table), the unassigned-device-data card, a friend request notice (Review opens Profile), Last session (your result and return or the top winner's, top winner, a bar per player; opens Games) and Hand rankings. The four tiles and the footer are gone. Profile's header gains a ⋯ account menu with "Signed in as", **Sign out** and **Delete my account** (the existing confirmation). The tab bar shows a green dot on Play during a game and a blue dot on Profile while friend requests wait (checked at app start and whenever Home opens; Profile keeps it current). **Poker rules removed (user decision 2026-10-01):** first kept as a row under Hand rankings; the user then asked to remove it, so the rules sheet and its styles are deleted (Home was its only entry point). **Background (user decision 2026-10-01):** design 6e, "Fanned hand" (`HomeScreen.dc.html`, `bg="fan"`), on Home only; it covers the shared orbs and pitch lines there. Checked in dark and light at phone size. **Theme button and Profile tab icon (user decisions 2026-10-01):** inside the app the theme switch shows only on Profile (pages outside the app keep theirs: sign-in, name, locked account, live link); the Profile tab shows a drawn person icon instead of a dot. **Adapted:** no "→" arrows (user decision 2026-10-01), so the Start card's round button shows ♦. **Acceptance:** lint, tsc and 171 tests pass; checked on the testbed at phone size as Karan (idle Home, account menu, delete confirmation cancelled, sign out) and Bikram (idle and live Home in dark and light, request notice and Review, Last session); the test game was discarded. **Not yet checked:** a session you played in (your result and return), a real phone.

2026-10-02 hide friends' groups with no shared games (user request): the Ranks picker listed every host who has you as a linked friend, so friends who had never seated you showed as "Name's Hosted Games · 0 games". Accepted: `GET /api/groups` leaves out a host unless at least one of their saved games has a result for your linked player (`playedInGroup` in `lib/friends/group-standings.ts`). A group appears once that host saves a game with you. The game count still counts all of that host's games. No migration; `friend_group_sessions()` is unchanged. Acceptance: such hosts are absent from the picker, and with none left the picker is hidden. Status: released 2026-10-02 (`c07a494`, deployed with `30d5062`).

2026-10-02 games played together (user request): the Profile rows' "Played N games together" counted only the viewer's own hosted games, so Rahul Basak saw Rajarshi at 0 though they played many of Rajarshi's games. **Accepted (user decisions in chat):** count every saved game both people played in, whoever hosted, including a third host's game, as long as that host has both linked as friends; counts follow links as they change (a later link counts earlier games; an unlink removes them). Guests count only the viewer's own games where both sat (a host who didn't sit no longer counts). **Architecture:** migration `0016_games_together` adds `accountId` to each result of `friend_group_sessions()`; `GET /api/profile` adds `gamesTogether` (own player id → count) built by `buildGamesTogether` in `lib/profile/together.ts` from the owner's own games, the friend groups and the owner's linked players. Account ids stay on the server. The merge warning for Link to guest… still uses the host's own count. **Acceptance:** 5 unit tests; on the testbed Bikram's test game with Asha and Arjun G. gave Asha 1 and Arjun G. 1, and as Asha, Bikram 1 from Bikram's hosted game. **Status:** released 2026-10-02 (user's go-ahead): 0016 applied to the testbed, development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` and production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (details in `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`), then the code was pushed. On production, Rahul Basak should see Rajarshi at 12 games together (all hosted by Rajarshi).

2026-10-02 link from the guest (user decision): friends' ⋯ menus showed **Link to guest…** whenever any unlinked guest existed, including after the friend was already linked and for friends added from the start, who never had a guest. **Decision:** reverse it. Friends lose the option; a guest gets **Link to friend…**, listing only friends who could be that guest: not yet linked to a guest, and friends only since after the guest was added. **How "not yet linked" is known (no migration):** `friend_accept` makes the friend's player and the friendship in one transaction, so their `created_at` match; the client compares `PlayerProfile.createdAt` with the friend's `since` within 1 ms (truncation vs rounding; seen on the testbed). Helpers in `lib/friends/link-guest.ts` with `test/link-guest.test.ts`. The server action (`link-guest`, `friend_link_guest`) and the accept card's Link to Guest *Name* are unchanged. **Acceptance:** tsc, lint, 181 tests; on the testbed as Asha, friend menus have no link, guests show none while the only friend is linked, after accepting Farhan Solo both older guests offered him alone, linking Lakshmi T. made her the friend row, and the other guest then had no option. **Status:** released as `c46e109` (2026-10-02, user's go-ahead).

2026-10-02 user code QR (user request): add a QR button beside the profile card's Copy, and a way to scan another user's QR. **Accepted behaviour:** the QR button opens a **User code** sheet with **My Code** (the formatted code `XXXX-XXXX` as a QR, reusing the live link's `QrCode`) and **Scan Code**; **Find Friend** gets a scan button beside **Find**. A scan reads the code and runs the usual `find` lookup; a scan from the profile card opens Add player with it filled in. Nothing is sent without **Send request**. **Choices:** the QR holds just the code, not a URL, so no deep-link handling is needed; the server already parses it (`parseCodeInput`), and the scanner uses the same parser to skip other QR codes. Decoding uses `jsqr` 1.4.0 (loaded only when scanning) on every browser rather than `BarcodeDetector`, which iPhones lack, so there is one path to debug. **Non-goals:** opening the app from a phone's own camera app; scanning from a photo. **No migration or API change.** **Acceptance:** tsc, lint, 181 tests; on the testbed as Asha, the QR sheet renders her code, a fake camera showing that QR filled Find Friend and showed "That's your own code", the in-sheet scan button shows the camera view, and a denied camera shows the settings note. A real phone camera is still to be tried. **Status:** released as `801c6c8` (2026-10-02, user's go-ahead).

2026-10-02 continue a saved game (user request: "restarting a saved game session"): **User decisions in chat:** it means *continuing* the same game (final chips, buy-ins, blinds and hand count carry over, and saving again updates that one game; a "play again" new game was the other option offered), and any non-discarded saved game can be continued at any time (offered: latest only, time-limited). **Agent choices:** the table opens between hands so busted players can rebuy; the dealer is drawn at random (it isn't saved); a minutes-based blind schedule gets a new plan from the next hand at the current big blind, so days away don't jump levels (hands-based schedules keep counting); the saved game is untouched until the continuation is saved, so discarding it loses only the new hands. **Architecture:** pure `lib/poker/continue-session.ts` (`gameFromSession`, `continuationError`) with `test/continue-session.test.ts`; `GameState.continues` holds the saved game's ID, number, end time and hands; `PUT /api/sessions` takes `{ session, basedOn: { ended, hands } }` and, under the owner lock, updates the row in place only if it still has those values (compare-and-set), then replaces its `session_results` and `buy_in_events`; a `session.continued` audit event keeps the previous version for recovery. Updating in place (not delete and re-insert) keeps the record ID, number and claim provenance. A retry of a save that went through returns `saved: 0`. Migration `0017_continue_saved_games` grants `DELETE` on the two result tables. **Acceptance:** tsc, 195 tests, lint clean on the changed files; on the testbed as Asha, Game 6 continued, played one hand and saved as the same game with 96 hands; retry, stale and no-new-hands PUTs changed nothing. **Status:** released 2026-10-02 (user's go-ahead): 0017 applied to the testbed, development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` and production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (details in `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`), then the code was pushed.

2026-10-02 close a continued game without new hands (user report): after continuing Game 27 just to look, **Finish And Save Session** refused with "No new hands to save" and the game stayed in progress, blocking other continues. **User decision:** allow finishing anyway. Implemented as **Close game**: with no new completed hands it confirms and clears the continued game on the device, without a server call, so the saved game stays as it was (sending an unchanged save would only be refused by `continuationError`). **Status:** released 2026-10-02 (user's go-ahead).

### Purge go-live checklist (production, requires separate authorization)

Status 2026-09-25: all four done; the cron ran (200, nothing due) and Healthchecks.io showed a green ping. No alert integration is set up yet (see the register).

1. Apply migrations `0006` and `0007` to production after the rest of the transition cutover, creating `menoka_purge` with SQL and initialising its password as documented.
2. Create a production-only Healthchecks.io check (1-day period, 2-hour grace) and a production `CRON_SECRET`.
3. In Vercel production, set `PURGE_DATABASE_URL`, `NEON_API_KEY` (project-scoped), `NEON_PROJECT_ID`, `NEON_AUTH_BRANCH_ID` (the production branch), `CRON_SECRET` and `HEALTHCHECKS_PING_URL`. Remove `DELETE_PASSWORD`.
4. After deploy, trigger the cron once from Vercel and confirm a green Healthchecks.io ping.

### Auth and firewall go-live checklist (production, requires separate authorization)

Status 2026-09-25: items 1–2 done except custom SMTP (the user chose Neon's shared sender). Item 3: the agent published "Limit API writes" at the user's request, exactly as specified, with the **Log** action. Next, around 2026-10-02: check Firewall → Overview for logged hits, switch the action to Too Many Requests (429) and publish, then do item 4.

1. On the production branch's Neon Auth: disable email and password (`enabled: false`, `disable_sign_up: true`), remove the shared Google provider, and confirm magic links are enabled with a 5-minute expiry. Consider disabling the unused organization plugin.
2. Neon's production checklist: add the production domain as a trusted domain, configure custom SMTP, set the application name, and disable "Allow Localhost".
3. Vercel Firewall → Configure → New Rule, named "Limit API writes": **If** Request Path starts with `/api/` **and** Method is any of POST, PUT, PATCH, DELETE; **Then** Rate Limit, Fixed Window, 60 s, 30 requests, key IP, action Default (429). Publish it with the Log action first for about a week, check the Firewall overview for false positives, then switch to 429.
4. Afterwards, send 31 quick writes from one IP (for example, repeated discard/restore of a throwaway player) and confirm the app shows the "Too many requests" message.

2026-09-27 speed investigation: **Problem:** the user saw occasional multi-second waits: about 10 s once to show the user code, 5 s to find a friend by code and a few more to send the request. **Findings:**
- **Infrastructure (the main cause):** the production compute (`ep-delicate-pond-ay8ple8b` on `br-small-sea-ayumyssr`, `aws-us-east-2`) suspends 5 minutes after the last query. On the Free plan this can't be turned off. Measured from the user's Mac: a first query to a suspended dev compute took 1.6 s and warm round trips about 270 ms (the distance from India to Ohio). The first request after a quiet spell can pay for a Vercel cold start, a Neon Auth `get-session` call (the signed session cookie cache lasts 5 minutes, the library default), and waking the compute, one after the other.
- **Code:** every API request wrote to `accounts` (an upsert) just to confirm the account exists, and then ran its own query as a second trip. The page already read the profile, but the Players screen fetched it again from `/api/account`. Queries themselves are not slow at today's size (about 100 rows).
- **Vercel functions region:** `iad1` (Washington), next to Neon's `us-east-2`; confirmed by the user on 2026-09-27.

**Provider comparison (checked 2026-09-27 on each pricing page):**
- Neon Launch: pay as you go, no minimum, $0.106 per CU-hour, and scale-to-zero can be turned off. Always on at 0.25 CU is about 182 CU-hours, about $19 a month.
- Supabase: the free plan never sleeps, but a project pauses after 1 week without activity; Pro is $25 a month.
- PlanetScale Postgres: always on from $5 a month (single node, no free tier).
- Prisma Postgres: free for 200,000 operations a month.
- **Recommendation: stay on Neon.** Neon Auth (sign-in, identity IDs, purge via the Neon API), row-security set-up and branch-based rehearsals all depend on it, so moving would mean rebuilding sign-in and account deletion. Paying for Neon Launch removes the cold starts at a cost similar to the alternatives.

**Step 1, committed 2026-09-27:**
- `lib/server-timing.ts` adds a `Server-Timing` header (`auth`, `account`, `db`, `total`) to every `/api/*` response and logs one `[timing]` line per request and per home-page render;
- `provisionHostAccount` is a single read that writes only when the account is missing, and it returns the profile too;
- `readAccountProfile` is gone. The page passes the profile into `PokerLedger`, so the You card needs no request and no "Loading your profile…" state;
- the auth helpers have explicit result types. The old inferred type made `authResult.response` possibly `undefined`.
- **Checks:** types, lint and 144 tests pass. On the dev server the Players screen showed the code at once and made no `/api/account` request; the timing headers read, for example, `auth=4 account=267 db=355` (from the Mac).

**Next options (each needs the user's go-ahead):**
- merge the account check into the route's own transaction, so a request makes one database trip;
- a longer sign-in cookie cache (`sessionDataTtl`). The trade-off: a revoked session keeps working on other devices for up to that long, though the account lock still applies at once;
- the Neon plan change;
- the testbed: a seeded Neon branch plus a benchmark script reporting typical and worst-case (p95) times per endpoint, warm and cold.

2026-09-27 testbed (accepted, in progress): **Problem:** features need testing at scale (friend requests, games, standings, live links), and a single dev account can't show how the app behaves with hundreds of people or where it slows down.
- **User decisions (2026-09-27):**
  - the testbed starts **empty**, with production's schema and no real names or games;
  - fake accounts sign in with **passwords on the testbed branch only**. Production and preview stay magic-link only;
  - a password form appears on the sign-in page only when the app runs locally against the testbed.
- **Parts:**
  - **T1:** a **separate Neon project**, `menoka-testbed` (`bold-firefly-91637201`, branch `br-young-night-b5xphgw8`, `aws-us-east-2`, compute 0.25–0.5 CU), with its own free compute allowance so test load doesn't use production's 100 CU-hours. `npm run testbed:setup` creates `menoka_app` and `menoka_purge` with SQL as in `migrations/README.md`, sets up Neon Auth (password sign-in on, magic links off, localhost allowed), applies the production migrations 0001–0012 in order, and writes a git-ignored `.env.testbed.local` without printing values. It uses a `TESTBED_NEON_API_KEY` scoped to that project, which the user adds to `.env.local`.
    - *Why not a branch:* a schema-only branch of production couldn't set up Neon Auth, because the copy drops `neon_service`'s role memberships. Restoring the last one meant granting `neon_superuser`, which the permission check refused. The user chose the separate project on 2026-09-27, and the empty branch `br-divine-mode-ay4sjwat` was deleted;
  - **T2 (working 2026-09-27):**
    - `npm run testbed:dev` runs the app on port 3006 with `.env.testbed.local` on top of `.env.local`, and blanks the production keys (purge URL, Neon API key, cron and Healthchecks secrets);
    - in testbed mode (`TESTBED=1` plus a persona password, never on Vercel production or preview; `lib/testbed/server.ts`) the sign-in page shows one **Sign in as** button per persona instead of the email form. The server action signs in with the personas' shared test password, which never reaches the browser;
    - `testbed/lib/client.ts` signs up, signs in and calls the API with the session cookies, for scripts.
  - **T3 (working 2026-09-27):** `npm run testbed:seed` creates the personas in `lib/testbed/personas.ts` through the app's own API: Bikram Big Host (15 players, 75 games; 60 and 300 until the user cut the seed to a quarter on 2026-09-27), Asha (3 players, 6 games, friends with Bikram and linked both ways), Ravi (Asha's request pending), Meera (3 players, 2 games, her request to Bikram pending), Karan (declined Meera), Farhan (1 player, no games or friends) and a new account without a name. It is deterministic (seed 20260927). `npm run testbed:reset` empties the testbed after checking it is the testbed's database. A unit test checks that 500 generated games pass `validateSession` and balance.
- **Scope decision (user, 2026-09-27):** the agent advised against a full load-testing testbed for now: production holds 1 account and 27 games, and the slowdowns come from cold starts and distance, which load tests from the Mac wouldn't show. The user agreed to the small version: separate project, persona sign-in, and the seeded story above with one large host for long-list checks. **Deferred:** concurrent load scenarios, p95 reports and a testbed control page. Revisit when real use reaches dozens of active hosts.
- **Acceptance:**
  - one command each to set up, run, seed and reset;
  - every persona signs in with one click and sees their story;
  - no secrets printed or committed;
  - production and preview auth settings unchanged.
- **Run on 2026-09-27:**
  - **Schema:** setup starts an empty database from `schema.sql`, because migration 0001 assumes tables that predate the migrations, then records 0001–0012 as applied. A read-only fingerprint (columns, functions, policies, triggers, grants and migration list) matched production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` exactly.
  - **Seed:** finished in 91 s. An API check of all seven personas matched their stories.
  - **Browser:** Asha signed in with one click, and her Ranks screen shows "Your games | Bikram Big Host's games".
  - **Fix found during the run:** `.env.local` is pulled from Vercel production (`VERCEL_ENV=production`), so the testbed-mode guard refused as designed. `testbed:dev` now marks its run as development.
- **First finding (2026-09-27):** `/api/groups` for a friend of a 300-game host took 1.6–3.5 s of `db` time from the Mac. `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` shows `friend_group_sessions()` runs in 18 ms on indexes; the rest is moving about 233 KB of game JSON from Ohio to India. On Vercel (`iad1`, next to the database) it should be tens of milliseconds, so nothing is urgent. The payload does grow with the host's history, so if groups ever get slow in production, compute the rows in SQL or send less per game.

2026-09-27 group standings graph (built, not released): **User decision:** a linked friend also sees the host's **Average Return** graph, not only the ranked rows. This relaxes the step 3 rule "only the ranked rows; the graph is left out". The user accepted that the graph lets a friend work out each player's return in each of that host's games.
- **Still never sent:** game, session or player IDs, and game names.
- **Data:** `buildGroupStandings` adds `chart`, one line per row in row order. Each line holds only the games that changed that player's score, as `[game number, running average, that game's return]`, rounded to two decimals. The phone carries the average across missed games, which rebuilds the host's own series exactly. No database change: `friend_group_sessions()` already returns the games to the server.
- **Size:** for a 300-game host with 60 players, 1,648 points and 41 KB for the whole `/api/groups` response on the testbed.
- **UI:** the graph sits above the intro sentence, as on My Hosted Games; the chart takes lines from either source (`chartLinesFromStandings`, `chartLinesFromGroup`). A long host name in the picker now ends in "…".
- **Checks:**
  - a new unit test covers per-row lines, a missed game and a player who joins late (149 tests pass);
  - on the testbed, Asha's own graph and Bikram's view of it drew identical lines (12 of 12, compared to 0.1 px);
  - Asha's view of Bikram's 300 games draws 60 lines at 375 px with no sideways scroll.

2026-09-27 interface tidy-up (user requests):
- **No signed-out tour:** the user dropped the idea of letting people see the app without signing up (recorded in the register).
- **Theme toggle:** a round sun/moon button (`components/theme-toggle.tsx`) replaces the dot and the "Dark" pill. It appears on every page, including sign-in, the name screen, the locked account and the live link.

  - no on-screen back button, because the phone's back gesture already walks the screen history;
  - no "Need 2+" note (Deal First Hand stays disabled until two are seated);
  - "By Hands" and "By Minutes" are capitalised.
- **Seat-drag jump:** a slow, frame-by-frame replay of a drag on the testbed measured neighbouring rows overshooting their new seat by 46–49 px on drop before sliding back, because their slide transition replayed the removed drag shift from the new position. The fix switches transitions off for the reordering frame. It also turns the dragged row's transition off from the first move, and measures the landing animation from centres, since the lifted row is scaled to 103%. After the fix the same replay measured 0 px overshoot in both directions.

2026-09-27 odd blinds (built, not released): **User request:** a switch in place of the "Small blind ₹50" note; the raise hint under the big blind is removed.
- **User decisions:**
  - rising blinds keep the small blind's share of the big blind;
  - a small blind from ₹1 up to and including the big blind is allowed;
  - the picker is chips (25%, 40%, 60%, 75% and 100% of the big blind) plus Other.
- **Model:** `GameState.smallBlindRatio` `{ small, big }` is set only when odd blinds are on and differ from half, so every existing game and every half-blind game keeps the exact old rounding, `floor(big / 2)`. `smallBlindFor(big, ratio)` rounds `big × small / big`, clamped to ₹1 up to the big blind.
- **Saved games** carry the share in `blindHistory.smallBlindRatio`: the existing `blind_history` jsonb, so no migration. The server validates it (whole numbers, 1 ≤ small ≤ big), and the retry conflict check compares it.
- **Live link:** the snapshot carries `smallBlind`, validated as 1 up to the big blind. Pages for older devices fall back to half.
- **Checks:**
  - 7 new unit tests (156 in total);
  - on the testbed, a game at ₹40/₹100 doubling every hand dealt a ₹140 pot, showed "₹40 / ₹100", announced ₹80/₹200 next, and the small-blind seat showed "in ₹40".

2026-09-30 profile tab (built, not released): **User request:** replace the Players tab with a Profile tab following the design in `Menoka Card Games Redesign/ProfileScreen.dc.html` (untracked), with working functionality.
- **User decisions (2026-09-30):**
  - the profile is the signed-in person's own; its stats cover every game they played in: friends' games where they are linked, plus their own hosted games as their own player;
  - **every account has its own player, tied to the account** (user decision later on 2026-09-30, replacing a first "This is me" menu option the user found awkward: "I should always be identifiable using some inner link"). It's created automatically when the account gets a name, always shows the account's name, is listed first (also on Table Setup, marked You), and can't be renamed, removed or linked to a friend. Existing hosts' players are tied only by a reviewed data step: the user confirmed "Rajarshi" in their production list is them; Emon's own row is tied when the Emon-led games are claimed; no one else has hosted;
  - each host chooses a **currency** on the profile; it labels every amount in the games they host, and their profile uses it. This supersedes the earlier "standings show chips, not ₹" decision;
  - games in another currency count toward games played, average return and profitable, but not the all-time net or the last-10 bars, which add up only games in the person's own currency (with a note saying how many are left out);
  - guests can be renamed; people with an account can't, and appear in a host's list by the name they chose themselves.
- **Migration 0013 (`0013_profile.sql`, additive):**
  - `accounts.currency` (text, default `INR`, three capital letters; the app accepts only its list) and `accounts.self_player_id`, a composite foreign key to the owner's own player that is cleared when that player is deleted;
  - `ensure_self_player(account)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, not granted to the app) gives a named account its own player once, stored under the account's name, numbered ("Asha (2)") if taken; it never picks an existing player. Trigger `accounts_create_self_player` calls it when an account gets a name;
  - trigger `accounts_guard_self_player`: `menoka_app` can't change `self_player_id`; trigger `players_guard_self`: the own player can't be linked to a friend (`friend:player-unavailable`) or removed, whichever role runs it;
  - `adopt_self_player(account, player)` (owner-only): ties an existing, active, unlinked player to an account on that person's confirmation, and deletes the empty automatic own player if it has no games, link or pending request. Tested on the testbed in a rolled-back transaction;
  - **production rollout order:** apply 0013; then, with the user's go-ahead, `adopt_self_player` for Rajarshi's existing "Rajarshi" (IDs looked up and confirmed in chat); then `SELECT public.ensure_self_player(id) FROM accounts WHERE display_name IS NOT NULL AND self_player_id IS NULL` for everyone else; then push the code;
  - **Emon and Rahul Basak:** when they sign up they get an empty automatic own player. The claim (0008) matches legacy results by name, so unless their account name is exactly "Emon" / "Rahul Basak" it adds a separate player with their history. After each claim, `adopt_self_player` that claimed player, with their confirmation;
  - `player_display_names()` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, `menoka_app` only): each of the caller's players with the name to show (a linked, active friend's display name; the host's own name for their self player; otherwise the stored name). The players list and session saves use it;
  - `friend_group_sessions()` now also returns the host's `currency`.
  - **Rollback:** see `migrations/README.md`. Loses currency choices and the account–player ties; players created as own players stay as ordinary players; games are untouched. The code needs the migration, so roll the code back first.
- **Server:** `POST /api/account` gains `set-currency`; removing your own player answers 409; `PATCH /api/players` gains `rename` (guests only; a clash answers 409); `GET /api/profile` returns the stats worked out on the server by `lib/profile/stats.ts` from the owner's own games and `friend_group_sessions()`; the live link stores the host's currency with each update.
- **Client:** `lib/poker/money.ts` (10 currencies) replaces `formatRupees`; a context gives every screen the host's currency, and a friend's group shows in that host's currency. A new game records `currency`, used by its log lines. Ranks shows current player names and marks your own player **You**; "View standings" opens a player's card on Ranks. The old You and Friends cards and Refresh are gone: requests are cards on the profile, friends and pending requests are rows, and friend actions sit in each row's menu.
- **Checks:**
  - unit tests for money formatting and profile stats (165 tests pass); lint, `tsc` and a production build in a copy pass;
  - on the testbed as Bikram: stats from Asha's group alone (6 games), then 38 games after **This is me** (his 32 plus Asha's 6), with the net adding up; switching to USD showed $ on the profile, Ranks and Games and the note about 6 games in another currency; add, rename (and its 409 on a clash), remove and permanent delete of a guest; menus open upwards near the tab bar; light theme;
  - on the testbed database: linking the self player is refused, and an account delete still cascades (rolled back).
  - The testbed account was put back afterwards (INR). Friend request accept and decline were not clicked, to leave the seeded requests for other testing; they use the unchanged friend API.
  - After the switch to automatic own players, the revised 0013 was re-applied to the testbed and every named persona got its own player (fake data, so no existing row was tied). The browser checks above predate this switch and should be repeated.
- **Not yet checked:** production data (migration not applied), a real phone's long press, iOS Safari.

Its rule `returnToBetweenHands` and test are removed too. Cancel hand, Undo and Undo last hand stay. Status: released 2026-10-01 (`c8f2798`, production runs `19f2f43`).

2026-10-01 instant Profile and Ranks (user request, from a screen recording): Profile's stats showed "–" for about 0.7 s and the Ranks "Whose standings" picker popped in about 0.5 s late, pushing the chart down, because both were fetched each time the tab mounted. Each API call costs three sequential trips (sign-in check, account lookup, query), about 0.6–0.8 s from India. **User decision:** an in-memory cache, not phone storage (would outlive sign-out on a shared phone) or a server cache (can't remove the round trip). The stats, friends overview and groups now live in the main ledger state, load at app start and are refreshed quietly when their tab opens (`refreshForView`); a failed refresh keeps the last answer. The name lives in one constant, `lib/brand.ts`, used by the page titles, PWA manifest, sign-in page, Home header, the "On Pokerize" find result and the avatar note; the two API error messages and `scripts/neon-auth.mjs` (Neon Auth app name, used in sign-in emails) spell it out.
