Open: App Store name/domain/handle availability, Status: released 2026-10-01 (`defced1`); the Neon Auth app name on production was changed to Pokerize the same day with the user's go-ahead.

2026-10-02 table view (design 7a): The user asked for design 7a ("Oval + action dock", `Menoka Card Games Redesign/TableGame.dc.html`) as a Table view of the live hand next to the existing List, with the app's feature set as the truth. Accepted behaviour: a List | Table switch in the hand's header (remembered per device in `localStorage` key `pokerLedger.handLayout.v1`, default List, hidden between hands); the oval with stage pips, pot, blinds, avatars with D/SB/BB, stacks, statuses and chips per seat; the existing betting panel docked under the table; the existing Deal button, Pick the winner/Split cards (seats on the table can also award or toggle the split), Cancel hand, standings, finish, undo last hand, discard and action log unchanged. Adjusted from the design: no "Skip to showdown" (the app has no such feature; streets are dealt with the Deal button), no pot-won toast (the app's winner card stays), no back button (the phone's back gesture is used). Added to the design for existing features: per-player Undo by tapping a seat, the blind schedule note, up to 10 seats (smaller avatars and a taller table above 6). Non-goals: the 7b full-felt variant, any change to rules or persistence. Acceptance: tsc, lint and 195 tests pass; checked on the testbed (Bikram's account, local game only) at 375px and 320px, dark and light, with 4 and 10 seats, through a full hand: actions, seat Undo, Deal, showdown split from seats and award with confirmation. Status: released (pushed to `main` 2026-10-02).

2026-10-02 rebuy limits (user decision, branch `claude/pokerize-buy-in-analysis-731bce`, not released): **Problem:** a buy-in analysis against standard practice found Pokerize matches a simple home cash game with fixed blinds (100 big blinds, full-stack rebuy when busted), but with rising blinds it allowed unlimited rebuys for the whole game, which is neither cash nor tournament practice (a rebuy late in a doubling game is only a few big blinds). **User decision:** the host chooses **Max rebuys** per player (No limit / None / 1 / 2 / 3 / Other, 0 to 63) and, with rising blinds, a **rebuy cut-off**; rebuy amount and "rebuy when short" stay as they are. **Agent decisions (delegated, long-term option):** the cut-off is stored as a big blind (`closeAtBigBlind`), not a level number, because `blindLevel` restarts at 0 whenever the blind plan is edited or a game is continued; the setup offers the plan's next three big blinds. Limits live in `GameState.rebuyRules` and `PokerSession.rebuyRules` (`{ maxRebuys, closeAtBigBlind }`, null = no limit, absent = none, so every older game is unchanged). They can be edited between hands (**Edit rebuys**), never below the most rebuys a player already made; the log line starts "Before hand N:" so Undo last hand doesn't remove it. The server validates the shape and rejects a save with more rebuys than `maxRebuys`; the cut-off can't be checked there because buy-ins don't record their hand. Saved in a new nullable `poker_sessions.rebuy_rules jsonb` (migration `0019_rebuy_rules`; 0018 is taken by `0018_admin_dashboard` on the testbed), carried by continue, compared by retries, shown on Games cards and the live standings page (live views are jsonb, no migration). **Status:** 215 tests, tsc, lint pass after merging `main`. 0019 is on the testbed. Browser-checked on the testbed at 375px: limits offered and enforced, Edit rebuys floor and log line, "Rebuys closed" at the closing big blind, save with rules, Games card, Continue game. The "No limit" chip was cut off at 375px, so it reads **∞** (the card note says "Unlimited rebuys"). On 2026-10-05, with the user's go-ahead, 0019 was applied to development and production and the branch was merged into `main` and pushed.

2026-10-05 cancel-hand fix: The user suspected Cancel hand had the same bug as Undo last hand (step 3U). Confirmed with a script running the component's exact steps: with 4 players, hand 1 dealt as dealer A, SB B, BB C came back after Cancel as dealer B, SB C, BB D, because the hand was dealt again from the cancelled hand's dealer; it also re-read the blind level at the moment of cancelling, so timed blinds could rise (100 → 200 when dealt at 9 minutes and cancelled at 11). `FEATURES.md` had described the moving button as the behaviour. User decision: keep the cancelled hand's dealer and blinds (a true do-over, like Undo last hand) rather than the blinds as of now. Fix: the pure `cancelCurrentHand` in `lib/poker/game.ts` replaces the component logic; it refunds `stacksBeforeHand`, removes the hand's log lines and restores the dealer and blind state the hand recorded when dealt (`dealerIndexBefore`, `anteBefore`, `blindLevelBefore`, `blindsBefore`, `blindLevelsBefore`), then deals again at the hand's `dealtAt`. Hands without that record fall back to stepping the dealer back one funded seat and the current blinds. No migration (hands live only in browser storage). Four new tests compare the whole game state with a snapshot taken at the deal (first hand and later hand, hand-based and timed rising blinds, an old-style hand, between hands); three fail against the old logic. tsc, lint and 206 tests pass. Browser: on a second testbed server from the worktree (Bikram, local game only, 4 players), Arjun called, Cancel hand dealt hand 1 again with the same dealer and blinds and Arjun's ₹100 back; the test game was discarded. Status: released with the raise-undo fix below.

2026-10-05 audit and raise-undo fix: Before committing the cancel-hand fix, the user asked for a check for other behaviour that doesn't do what it should. Reviewed every game action that changes chips or turns (per-player Undo, streets and first to act including heads-up, Pick the winner, the automatic win, Split, rebuys, blind-plan edits, Undo last hand, Continue game, Finish and Save). Found two: (1) **no side pots** (see the plan entry "side pots" below); (2) **undoing a bet or raise made everyone who had already called act again**: a raise clears the other players' `acted` flags and the undo record didn't keep them, so after D, A and B called, C raised, C's raise was undone and C checked, D, A and B were asked to act again and Deal stayed faded. Fix: `RaiseRecord` gains optional `acted` (who had acted before the action), recorded by `applyRaiseRules`; `undoRaiseRules` marks a player as having acted if they had before the action or have since, so out-of-turn undo keeps later players' actions. Actions recorded before the change lack it and behave as before. Two new tests (one fails without the fix). tsc, lint and 208 tests pass. Browser: on a second testbed server from the worktree (Bikram, local game, discarded), three calls, a raise by the big blind, Undo, Check: "Betting round complete" and Deal FLOP enabled. Status: released.

2026-10-05 side pots (plan, not built; user request after the audit above). **Problem:** `awardPot` and `splitPot` in `components/poker-ledger.tsx` always pay out the whole `hand.pot`. A player who is all in can only win what they matched from each opponent, so today a short stack can win chips they never risked. Example: A (₹1,000) is all in; B and C call and then bet ₹4,000 more each. The pot is ₹11,000; the app gives A all of it if A wins, where A should get ₹3,000 (₹1,000 from each of the three) and the other ₹8,000 goes to the better of B and C. The same happens heads-up: if B bets ₹10,000 and A calls all in for ₹1,000, A can win ₹11,000 instead of ₹2,000, because B's unmatched ₹9,000 should simply go back to B.

**Rule (standard poker):** each player's total in the hand is `stacksBeforeHand[i] - players[i].stack` (no rebuys happen mid-hand). Sort the distinct totals of the players still in who are all in. Each level makes a pot: every player (folded or not) adds up to that level's share, and only the players still in who reached that level can win it. The first is the main pot, the rest are side pots. A top layer that only one player put in is an uncalled bet and goes straight back to them. Folded players' chips stay in the pots they reached; they can't win any.

**Proposed behaviour:**
- **One pot, as today.** If nobody still in is all in for less than another player still in, nothing changes: the Pick the winner card, Split, the Table view taps and the confirmation all work as now.
- **Pots shown during the hand.** Once a player still in is all in for less, the pot reads "Pot ₹11,000" with a line "Main ₹3,000 · Side ₹8,000" under it (List and Table views). The action log notes "₹X returned to B (not called)" when an uncalled bet goes back at the showdown.
- **Showdown with several pots.** The winner card lists each pot with who can win it, smallest (main) first: "Main pot ₹3,000 · A, B, C" with a winner button per player and Split; then "Side pot ₹8,000 · B, C". Each pot gets one winner or a split. When every pot has a choice, one confirmation summarises it ("A wins the ₹3,000 main pot, B wins the ₹8,000 side pot") and pays out. A pot with only one possible winner (for example after the others in it folded) is awarded automatically and shown as such.
- **Everyone else folds:** unchanged; the last player gets everything (they are eligible for every pot).
- **Winner celebration and log:** one log line per pot ("Hand 12: A wins main pot ₹3,000", "Hand 12: B wins side pot ₹8,000"); the confetti card lists each pot's winner.
- **Undo last hand and Cancel hand:** unchanged; both already restore stacks from before the hand.

**Architecture:** a pure `potsFor(game)` in `lib/poker/game.ts` returns `[{ amount, eligible: number[] }]` plus any uncalled refund, and a pure `awardPots(game, winnersPerPot)` pays out, logs and records `lastHand`, replacing the component's `awardPot`/`splitPot` arithmetic. `WinnerAnnouncement` gains an optional per-pot list (older saved games keep `names`/`pot`). No database or API change: hands live only in browser storage and saved sessions only keep end stacks, which stay correct.

**Open questions for the user:**
1. Award all pots on one card with a single confirmation (recommended, fewer taps), or one pot at a time, each confirmed?
2. Odd chips in a split side pot: keep today's rule (seat order, as in the main pot), or switch everywhere to the casino rule (first player to the left of the dealer)? Recommended: keep seat order, so splits behave the same in every pot.
3. Table view: with several pots, should tapping seats still award (choosing the pot first), or should the table only show the pots and leave the choice to the card below it? Recommended: the card only, since a tap can't say which pot it's for.
4. Show the main/side split during the hand (recommended), or only at the showdown?

**Acceptance:** unit tests for pot building (two and three all-in levels, folded contributors, uncalled bet refund, split side pot with odd chips, everyone folds, a hand with no all-in unchanged); total chips before and after each hand are equal; browser check of a three-player hand with one short all-in on List and Table views at 375px, dark and light; `FEATURES.md` sections 6 and 7 updated. **Non-goals:** run it twice, rake, antes.

2026-10-05 side pots built (user decision: "go with your recommendations, build side pots"). **Answers:** (1) all pots on one card with one confirmation; (2) odd chips in seat order in every pot; (3) Table view seats can't award when there are side pots, the card under the table picks; (4) the main/side split shows during the hand. **Built:** pure `handContributions`, `potsFor`, `potLabel`, `hasSidePots` and `awardPots` in `lib/poker/game.ts`. Pots close at each all-in amount of a player still in, plus the highest amount still in; players with chips left count for every pot (so mid-street a player still to call doesn't close one); the uncalled excess of the single top contributor is refunded first; folded chips stay in the pots they reached (any above every player still in join the last pot). `awardPots(game, winnersPerPot)` validates every pot (a pot with one eligible player ignores the choice), refunds, pays with seat-order odd chips, logs one line per pot, records `lastHand` and sets `WinnerAnnouncement.pots` (new optional `PotResult[]`). UI in `components/poker-ledger.tsx`: `PotBreakdown` under the pot (List scoreboard and Table centre), `SidePotPicker` replaces `WinnerPicker` at the showdown when `hasSidePots` (List and Table), `awardSidePots` confirms with a summary, `WinnerCard` lists pots. Hands with a single pot and nothing to refund use the old Pick the winner/Split flow unchanged. Styles under "Side pots" in `app/globals.css`. No database, API or saved-format migration (older games have no `pots` on the announcement). **Checks:** 11 new unit tests (short all-in, uncalled refund, folded contributors, three pots and labels, odd-chip split with chips conserved, a pot one player can win, ordinary hand, mid-street, refused winners, undo last hand); tsc, lint and the full suite pass. Browser on a second testbed server from the worktree (Bikram, local three-player game with Anika's stack set to ₹1,000 in this device's saved game for the test, discarded afterwards): List view showed "Pot ₹11,000 · Main ₹3,000 · Side ₹8,000", the card awarded Anika the main pot and Bikram the side pot (₹3,000 / ₹13,000 / ₹5,000) with the Pots won card; Undo last hand dealt the hand again from the original stacks; Table view with Aditi folding to Bikram's ₹4,000 bet showed "₹4,000 goes back to Bikram", disabled seats and Pick the winner, and paid ₹3,000 to Anika with ₹4,000 back to Bikram (₹21,000 conserved); at 320px, dark and light, a split side pot paid ₹4,000 each with no sideways scroll. Production build passes. Status: released (pushed to `main` 2026-10-05 with the user's go-ahead).

2026-10-05 group standings privacy (plan, not built; user question: "users can see other players in the games hosted by other players. Is this favourable?"). **Problem:** a linked friend sees the host's whole ledger: every player's rank, name, return, sessions, hands, invested and net, plus the Average Return graph across all of the host's games, including games the friend never played and players they have never met (guests included, who never agreed to it). Totals also leak the host's activity (the dropdown's "· 8 games", how many players the host has). **User decisions (2026-10-05):** a friend's view of a host's group is built **only from saved games that friend played in** ("what you could have seen at the table"):
- **Who is listed:** only players who sat in at least one of those games, guests included by name.
- **Ranking:** worked out among those players only, from those games only, numbered 1…N with no gaps. Nothing reveals how many players or games the host has in total; the user rejected keeping the host's rank numbers with a "6 of 15" note for that reason.
- **Other players' numbers:** rank, return and sessions from shared games only. **No money** (no invested or net) for anyone but the viewer.
- **The viewer's own numbers:** sessions, return, invested and net in that host's currency (every game they played is a shared game, so these match the host's view of them); rank as above ("3rd of 6").
- **Graph:** only shared games and those players.
- **Dropdown:** "Meera's Hosted Games", **no game count**. A host is listed only when the viewer has played at least one saved game with them (unchanged).
- **The i text:** "Your ranking among the players you've sat with in games hosted by Meera.", followed by how players are ranked.
- **No host setting:** rejected by the user as extra complexity; every friend gets the same rules.
**Supersedes** the step 3 rule (a friend sees the host's standings exactly as the host sees them) and the 2026-09-27 graph decision. **Consequence accepted:** two friends of the same host can see different rankings, and neither need match the host's. **Built (2026-10-05, user: "go ahead and build it"; not released):** migration `0022_shared_group_games.sql` re-creates `friend_group_sessions()` with only games where the caller's linked player has a verified result (Profile stats and games-together counts already used only those, so they're unchanged). `buildGroupStandings` in `lib/friends/group-standings.ts` filters the same way (so the code is safe with or without 0022), ranks the remaining players with `buildStandings`, and puts `invested`/`net` only on the caller's row; `games` is now the shared count (the graph's x axis). Ranks (`components/poker-ledger.tsx`): the dropdown has no game counts (also dropped from **My Hosted Games** for a consistent list), `StandingCard` hides Invested/Net when absent, new i text. **Checks:** tsc, lint, 259 tests (group-standings tests rewritten: shared-games-only, no money on others, graph numbering); on the testbed (3006) as Asha viewing Bikram (77 games, 16 players; Asha in 25 with all 16, confirmed by SQL): `/api/groups` returned `games: 25`, ranks 1–16, no `net`/`invested` on any other row; Anika's opened card shows Sessions, Profitable, Hands only, Asha's shows Invested and Net; dropdown "My Hosted Games" / "Bikram Big Host's Hosted Games"; the new i text; no console errors. Rehearsed on dev `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` 2026-10-05 (`MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`). **Released:** code pushed 2026-10-05; 0022 applied to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` the same day (`MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`). **Not yet:** 0022 on the testbed (the agent's attempt was blocked by the permission check; the app-side filter covers it), a real-phone check.

2026-10-05 settings and info pages (user request: a features page, a feature log, a privacy policy, account deletion details "and whatever else is needed to not get sued", all inside an additional Settings page on Profile, with the action-click switch moved there). **User decisions:** contact email therajarshiroy@gmail.com (public); operator "Rajarshi Roy, India", Indian law (DPDP Act 2023); feature log by date, newest first, items tagged New / Improved / Fixed; minimum age 18. **Agent choices (long-term, debuggable):** the five pages are public server-rendered routes (`/features`, `/whats-new`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/account-deletion`; outside the `proxy.ts` matcher, so no sign-in), so app stores, the sign-in page and anyone else can open the same address, and there is one rendering path. In the app, Settings is a new ledger view (`"settings"`, tab Profile) in `components/settings-view.tsx`; its rows link to the pages with `?from=app`, the pages' Back (`components/info-back-link.tsx`) then steps back through history, and the ledger reopens on Settings when it mounts on a history entry whose `appView` is `"settings"` (every other start still opens Home or the game). Facts the pages state live in `lib/info/legal.ts`, reusing the app's own constants (30-day grace, 10-minute recent sign-in, 12-hour live links) so policy text can't drift from behaviour; the feature log is data in `lib/info/changelog.ts` (tests: dates descending and unique, tags valid). Long prose on the info pages is sentence case (exception to the Title Case house style; headings and buttons keep it). Delete My Account is in Settings as well as the ⋯ menu. The sign-in page now says signing in accepts the Terms and Privacy Policy and that users must be 18+. **Not legal advice:** the texts were written from the code's actual behaviour, not by a lawyer; the user should have them reviewed before an app-store launch, and recheck them whenever data handling changes (new providers, custom SMTP, analytics, payments). **Open:** the Terms name no court city; a dedicated contact address could replace the personal one later (one constant). **Checks:** tsc, lint, 263 tests; testbed (3006) at 375 px: all five pages render with no sideways scroll; Profile → Settings → switch toggles `pokerLedger.turnSound.v1` (off/on); Privacy Policy → Back reopens Settings, back again reaches Profile, the game in progress kept; Delete My Account opens the existing confirmation (dismissed). **Follow-up fixes (user screenshots, same day):** "Fixed" tags overlapped their text (class `fixed` is also Tailwind's `position: fixed`; tags are now `change-tag-new/-improved/-fixed`); the Profile Settings row's chevron sat mid-row (the flex rule caught both spans; now `:first-child`); Delete My Account drew a boxed button (class `danger` is an existing button style; now `settings-row-danger`); and, older and unrelated, the admin dashboard's top nav grew tall on short views under 760 px (the one-column grid stretched its rows to the screen's height; `.admin-shell` now has `align-content: start`). Rechecked on the testbed. **Then (user decisions):** Delete My Account removed from the ⋯ menu beside the name (Settings only; the `onDeleteAccount` props through ProfileView/ProfileCard/AccountMenu went with it); Contact Us did nothing on devices without a mail app, so the row keeps the `mailto:` link and adds a Copy button (same icon and tick as the user code). Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 pot pick check, casino odd chips, big blind ante and rake (user requests after reviewing what the side-pot rules lacked). **User decisions:** (1) warn when winner picks can't all be right; (2) odd chips follow the casino rule; (3) no per-player hand total; (4) **big blind ante** (only the big blind posts one ante of one big blind for the table), not every-player antes or a setup choice between them; (5) **rake goes to the house, outside the game**, recorded with the saved game (not to the host's stack); **percent with a cap, no flop no drop**. **Agent choices (long-term, debuggable):** the pick check is a pure `inconsistentPotPicks(pots, winners)`: later pots' players are a subset of earlier pots', so a pot's winners who are in a later pot must be exactly that pot's winners; the confirmation names only the first slip and its button reads **Award anyway** (the app can't see cards, so it never blocks). Odd chips: pure `oddChipOrder`/`splitShares` (first chip to the winner nearest the dealer's left, clockwise), used by `awardPots`, the single-pot split and both pickers' previews. The ante is `Hand.ante = { playerIndex, amount }`, posted after the big blind from what is left (TDA order), kept out of `committed` and `roundHigh`, and `potsFor` takes it out of the poster's contribution and adds it to the main pot, so it is never refunded as uncalled. Rake: `GameState.rakeRules = { percent: 1–10, cap }` and `GameState.rake` (taken so far); `handRake` is percent of the pots after any uncalled refund, rounded down, capped, zero before the flop (`stage < 1`); `rakedPots` takes it from each pot in proportion (leftover chips from the main pot first); `takeRake`/`awardPots` add it to `game.rake` and log "house rake"; `CompletedHand.rakeBefore` lets Undo last hand give it back. Accounting changes from "nets add up to 0" to "nets add up to minus the rake" (`deriveSessionAccounting`, used by save validation, standings, profile stats, friends' groups and the live link); the server also refuses rake without rules or above cap × hands. **Migration 0023** adds `poker_sessions.big_blind_ante boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT false`, `rake_rules jsonb` (object) and `rake bigint NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (≥ 0, and 0 unless `rake_rules` is set), and re-creates `friend_group_sessions()` with each game's `rake`, so friends' views can verify raked games. Older games read as no ante and no rake, so nothing changes for them. Continuing a game keeps the ante and rake rules and refuses less rake than saved. Rake shows on the setup card, the showdown cards and confirmation, the Session Standings card, the saved game card and the live link. **Checks:** tsc, lint, 280 tests (new: odd chips from the dealer's left, the pick check against the eight scenarios from the user's guide plus three slips, ante posting, short big blind, dead ante in the main pot and with a short all-in, cancel; rake no-flop, percent and cap, uncalled chips, proportional cut, award and undo, takeRake; accounting, validation, continuation and live view with rake). Testbed (3006, 375 px): 0022 and 0023 applied with `testbed:setup`; as Bikram, picks Asha / Bikram / Asha on a three-pot showdown gave one "Check:" warning and **Award anyway** (dismissed, game left at the showdown); as Asha, a three-player game with ante and 5% rake (cap ₹300): pot ₹250 preflop with "Ante ₹100", showdown ₹400 → ₹380 to the winner after ₹20 rake, chips 29,980 + 20 = 30,000, saved as Game 9 with "Big blind ante · 5% rake, up to ₹300 · house took ₹20"; as Bikram, Asha's group counts Game 9 (9 of 9 eligible). **Not done:** rehearsal on the development branch and production; 0023 must be applied before this code is released (`GET /api/sessions` selects the new columns). Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 custom rake (user request: "wouldn't it be good to have a custom rake option as well?"). **Built:** the rake chips gain **Other**, a typed percent from 0.1 to 10 in steps of 0.1 (`isValidRakePercent`). `RakeRules.percent` may now have one decimal; `handRake` works in tenths of a percent (`floor(pots × tenths / 1000)`), so 2.5% or 0.7% is exact integer arithmetic, and `normalizeRakeRules` stores the percent rounded to tenths. The 10% ceiling stays. 0023 isn't on production yet, so the stored shape (`{ percent, cap }`) is unchanged and needs no new migration. Also: the Most per hand box shows the currency symbol inside it (`.money-field`), and the custom percent box a % sign (`.percent-field`). Tests: exact 2.5% and 0.7%, accepted and refused percents. Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 later pots follow earlier winners (user question: can someone win the main pot but not a side pot they're in? If not, "why even allow awarding"). **Answer:** no; each side pot's players are some of the previous pot's, so the previous pot's winners who are in it have its best hand (ties included); the only exceptions are players chopping by agreement or a deliberate correction, which Undo last hand covers. **User decision:** prevent it instead of warning. **Built:** pure `resolvePotWinners(pots, picks)` replaces `inconsistentPotPicks`: a pot with one player goes to them; a pot the previous pot's winners are in goes to those of them (shared if they shared), with `from` naming the pot they were picked in; otherwise the pick stands. `awardPots` uses it, so a contradictory payout can't happen even if the screen sent one. `SidePotPicker` shows those pots locked with "Goes to X · best hand in the main pot" / "Split by X and Y · they split the main pot", needs picks only for the rest, and clears later picks when an earlier one changes. The confirmation lost its warning and **Award anyway**; the confirm dialog's bold-lead option added for it is gone too. Tests: the guide's eight scenarios unchanged, carried winners, splits carried and narrowed, one-player pots, and an award whose side-pot picks are overruled. Testbed: picking Bikram for the main pot locked both side pots to him and enabled Award pots; Asha locked only side pot 1. Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 one Undo button, amount box, buy-in during a hand (user requests). **Undo:** tapping seats (Table) and per-card Undo pills (List) replaced by one `UndoTurnButton` that undoes the latest action this street and names it ("Undo Asha's call"; full name when first names clash). The user suggested "Undo A's hand"; **agent choice:** name the action, since "Undo last hand" already means a whole hand. **Amount box:** bordered field, "Enter amount" caption, pencil icon, green focus ring; picked quick size highlighted white (user decision). **Buy-in during a hand** (user request: players sometimes deal without rebuying a busted player; "can be done via tapping the player"). **Agent choices (long-term, consistent accounting):** only a player bust at the deal (not in the hand and `stacksBeforeHand` 0; never someone all in) can buy in; they sit the hand out and play from the next deal (no mid-hand seating). `buyInPlayer` also sets `stacksBeforeHand[i]` to the new stack and records `Hand.boughtIn`, so finishing mid-hand, the live link, Cancel hand and Undo last hand all see the buy-in as made before the deal and stay balanced. Consequence: Cancel hand (and Undo last hand of that hand) deals again *with* that player. The log line is "During hand N: …" so Cancel hand keeps it; Undo last hand removes "During hand N+1" lines with the next hand's buy-ins it takes back. Seat status "Busted" / "Next hand" instead of "Folded". No migration (in-progress games live on the device). Tests: bust-only rule, cancel keeps it, undo of the hand before takes it back. Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 locked pots keep their list (user feedback with a screenshot: show the UI, not only a line of text). A pot that follows an earlier one now shows its player list with the winners highlighted (`selected`, share shown) and every row disabled (`.contender:disabled`; winners keep full opacity), plus the "Goes to … · best hand in …" line instead of the split button. **Bug found in the screenshot and fixed:** with **Split this pot** on and one player chosen, later pots already locked to that player; `SidePotPicker` now passes a split pot with fewer than two players as unpicked (`picks`), both to `resolvePotWinners` and to Award. Testbed checked both. Status: built, not committed or released.

2026-10-12 timed blinds one level per deal (user report from the testbed: a By Minutes game at L4, 600/800 doubling, left idle and then dealt at about L52 with a big blind near 2.25e17, which the screen never warned about). **Cause:** `blindLevelFor` counted wall-clock minutes since the plan started, and `applyBlindLevel` applied every level that had passed at the next deal, while `blindStatus` only ever named level + 1. Continue-session already restarted the clock (`lib/poker/continue-session.ts`); a live game left open did not. Side effect found: big blinds above 2^53 are not safe integers, so the server's save check (`blind level big blind`) would also have rejected that game. **Options offered:** (A) at most one level per deal; (B) pause the clock after idle time; (C) keep the clock and show the real level the next deal will use. **User decision (chat):** A, the recommended option. **Rule:** a timed level lasts `every` minutes from when it began (`GameState.blindLevelStartedAt`); a deal at or after its end moves up exactly one level. The new level starts where the last one ended (keeping the schedule during play, so a long hand doesn't stretch it), unless that end is a whole level or more in the past, in which case its clock starts at the deal. The first hand of a plan (new game, edited plan, continued game) counts from the plan's `effectiveAt` at level 0. Games stored before this change have no start time and fall back to `effectiveAt + level × every`, so they also move up at most one level. FEATURES §5's "keeps counting between hands" still holds; the countdown now counts to the level the next deal really uses. Cancel hand and Undo last hand restore the level start (`blindLevelStartedAtBefore` on `Hand` and `CompletedHand`). Hand-based schedules are unchanged. No migration: the field lives in the browser game state, and saved blind history is unchanged. **Not addressed:** a hand-based or very long timed schedule can still multiply past safe integers and then fail to save; a cap could be a separate step. **Acceptance:** four new tests in `test/game.test.ts` (idle table moves one level and the note matches the deal; schedule kept during play and restarted after a long break; older games without the start time; an edited plan) and the existing timed-level test updated from a two-level jump to one; tsc, lint and 289 tests pass. **Browser check** (worktree testbed server, Bikram, throwaway 2-player game, 20 minutes ×2): between hands at L4 $400/$800 with the level clock moved back 17 hours, the note read "Blinds go up to $800/$1,600 on the next hand"; the deal went to L5 $800/$1,600 (log "level 5") with a fresh 20-minute countdown; Cancel hand dealt it again at L5 with the countdown still running. The game was discarded unsaved. **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` 2026-10-12 with the user's go-ahead.

2026-10-12 pause the blind clock (plan; user request after the one-level-per-deal fix, asked "this is how home games are played?"). **Why:** real home tournaments pause the timer for dinner or a long break and come back at the same level; the app has no pause, so it treats a break longer than a level as a pause but still goes up one level. **User decisions (chat, all recommended options):** (1) the host can pause **any time**, between hands or during a hand, like a tournament clock; blinds still only change on a deal, so a mid-hand pause just stops the countdown; (2) **dealing a hand resumes the clock**, so a forgotten Resume can't freeze the blinds all night; the countdown carries on from where it stopped; (3) a small **Pause / Resume button beside the countdown** under the blinds, in List and Table view and between hands; while paused the note reads "Clock paused · $100/$200 in 12:34" instead of ticking. **Only for By Minutes plans;** fixed and By Hands games show no button.

**Rules (proposed):**
- Pausing records `GameState.blindClockPausedAt`. While paused, `blindStatus` measures the time left against that moment (adds `paused: true`), so the countdown and "goes up on the next hand" freeze.
- Resuming moves the current level's start (`blindLevelStartedAt`) later by the paused time, and a pending timed plan's `effectiveAt` too, so its first level also loses no time. The level stays the same.
- `dealNewHand` resumes a paused clock at the deal time before working out the level (decision 2). Cancel hand and Undo last hand are do-overs, not new deals: they keep the clock paused or running as it is, and pause time taken since the hand was dealt still counts as paused. To make that exact, the game keeps a running total of paused time (`blindPausedMs`, never restored by Cancel/Undo); each hand records the total at its deal, and the re-deal moves the level start by the difference.
- Log lines without a hand prefix, so Cancel/Undo don't remove them: "Blind clock paused (L3, 12:34 left)" and "Blind clock resumed".
- Editing the blind plan while paused leaves the pause on. If the next deal brings a fixed or By Hands plan, the pause is cleared.
- The one-level-per-deal rule stays as the safety net for tables nobody paused.
- No server, database or saved-history change: the pause lives in the browser game state; saved blind history keeps plans and dealt levels only. Continued games already start a fresh clock. The live standings link and the Home live card show no countdown, so they don't change.

**UI (proposed):** the button sits at the end of the `.blind-timer` note; pause and play icons with the words "Pause" and "Resume", `aria-pressed`; `useBlindClock` stops ticking while paused. Check at 375 and 320 px in both views and both themes; the note and button must stay on one line or wrap cleanly.

**Acceptance:** pure `pauseBlindClock` / `resumeBlindClock` in `lib/poker/game.ts` with tests in `test/game.test.ts`: the countdown freezes while paused and resumes with the same time left; a deal while paused resumes and keeps the level; a pause spanning a level break doesn't raise the blinds; a pending edited plan loses no time; Cancel and Undo keep the pause state and pause time (snapshot tests as for the existing ones); fixed and By Hands games can't be paused. Then `FEATURES.md` §5, a What's New entry, lint, tsc, tests, and a browser check on a worktree testbed server.

**Open:** none blocking. Agent choice to confirm during review: the paused wording "Clock paused" (offered alongside the countdown) rather than "Break".

**Built** (user: "build it"): `hasBlindClock`, `pauseBlindClock`, `resumeBlindClock` and `formatClock` in `lib/poker/game.ts`; `dealNewHand` resumes a paused clock (only when the hand will really deal), while Cancel and Undo go through `redealHand`, which re-deals at the original time and moves the level start by the pause time taken since (`blindPausedMsAtDeal`; a hand dealt before the first pause counts as 0). A plan edited during a pause loses only the paused time after it was chosen. `blindStatus` gains `paused`. UI: `.blind-clock` row with the `.clock-toggle` button (pause/play icons, `aria-pressed`), `useBlindClock` stops ticking while paused; the component's countdown formatter now uses `formatClock`. Five new tests (`blind clock pause` in `test/game.test.ts`); tsc, lint and 294 tests pass. **Browser check** (worktree testbed server, Bikram, throwaway 2-player game, 20 minutes ×2): paused mid-hand, the note read "Clock paused · $100/$200 in 19:52" and stayed there; same in Table view, at 320 px and in the light theme (one line, no overflow); between hands it stayed paused, left-aligned; dealing resumed it from 19:52 and logged "Blind clock resumed"; Pause then Resume froze and restarted it; no console errors. The game was discarded unsaved. User review: the label sat about 1px below the icon (Barlow's capitals sit low in the line box); the label is now a `span` with `text-box: trim-both cap alphabetic`, which centres the capitals on the icon in current Chrome and Safari (older browsers keep the 1px offset). The user still saw the word as low: the label is mostly lowercase, so its weight sits between x-height and cap height. The pause bars and play triangle were shortened to 12/24 of the icon and moved down so they rest on the baseline and reach between x-height and cap height. The paused wording is "Clock paused" as planned. **Status:** committed and pushed to `main` 2026-10-12 with the user's go-ahead.

2026-10-12 who wins? hand checker (plan; user request: "enter the 5 cards for each player and then tell users who won along with explanation. could be very useful for beginners"). **Why:** at the showdown the host has to pick the winner by hand (section 7 of `FEATURES.md`); beginners often can't tell a straight from a flush, miss that the board plays, or don't know how kickers settle a tie. The app already teaches the order of hands (Hand Rankings) but can't apply it to real cards.

**Problem to settle first, the card model.** The app runs Texas Hold'em (flop, turn, river, blinds). There, nobody holds five cards: each player has 2 hole cards and shares 5 board cards, and their hand is the best five of those seven. "Five cards per player" fits only if the user means the finished five-card hand (or five-card draw). **Recommendation:** enter the 5 board cards once and 2 cards per player; the app finds each player's best five and shows them. It is less typing (5 + 2 each instead of 5 each), it is what really happens at the table, and the hardest beginner mistakes (the board plays, a flush on the board, three pairs on seven cards) only show up this way.

**Accepted behaviour (proposed):**
- **Where.** Step 1: a **Who Wins?** row on Home under Hand Rankings, usable any time, with or without a game. Step 2 (separate, after step 1 is reviewed): a **Check the hands** link on the showdown card that opens the same checker with the players still in, and can fill in the picks.
- **Entering cards.** Board: five slots (Flop, Flop, Flop, Turn, River). Players: 2 to 10 rows, each with a name (prefilled "Player 1", "Player 2"… on Home; the real names from step 2) and two card slots. Tapping a slot opens a picker: 4 suit rows × 13 ranks (A K Q … 2), big enough to tap at 320 px. A card already used anywhere is shown faded and can't be picked, so duplicates are impossible. After a pick the next empty slot opens, so a whole hand is a run of taps. Tapping a filled card clears it. **Clear all** starts over.
- **The answer** appears once the board and at least two players are complete (players left empty are ignored, so a folded player can be skipped). Winner card: "**Asha wins** with a Flush, Ace high", her best five shown as cards, with any board or hole card she didn't use faded (the Hand Rankings style). Then every player in order, best first: name, hand name, best five.
- **The explanation**, in plain sentences, chosen by why the winner won:
  - Different hands: "A flush beats a straight." (one line per beaten hand type, from the ranking order).
  - Same hand, higher hand: "Both have a straight; Asha's goes up to the King, Bikram's to the Queen."
  - Same hand, settled by a kicker: "Both have a pair of Kings. Next highest card: Asha's Ace beats Bikram's Queen."
  - Tie: "Split pot: both play the board's straight, 9 to King; neither player's own cards improve it." / "Split pot: same two pair, same fifth card."
  - Beginner traps, said out loud when they happen: the wheel ("A-2-3-4-5 is a straight; the Ace counts low"), the board plays, a flush or straight on the board, only five cards count (a sixth heart or a third pair doesn't help), two three-of-a-kinds make a full house.
- **Nothing is saved.** Cards stay on the screen only; leaving the screen clears them. No server call, no database, no change to games, backups or the live link.

**Step 2, showdown (proposed, decided later):** the checker opens with the names of the players still in, in seat order. **Use this result** fills in the winner, or the split, of each pot from the evaluated order, honouring side pots (each pot goes to the best hand among the players eligible for it; this agrees with "later pots follow earlier winners"). It only fills the picks; the host still presses Award and confirms, so a wrong card entry can be corrected and the app never awards on its own. The picks the host makes by hand stay possible. Cards are not stored in the game state, so undo, backups and the saved game don't change.

**Non-goals:** winning odds or equity before the river; Omaha, short deck, five-card draw, low/hi-lo games (revisit only if the user wants a five-card mode); reading cards from a photo; storing hands or showing them in game history; awarding pots automatically.

**Architecture (proposed):**
- Pure module `lib/poker/hands.ts`, no React or persistence: card codes (`"Ah"`, `"Td"`, …), `bestHand(cards)` over every 5-card choice from 5 to 7 cards (21 choices for seven; plain enumeration rather than a lookup-table evaluator, because it is easy to read, easy to test and far fast enough for 10 players), returning `{ category, ranks, cards }` where `ranks` is the tie-break order (e.g. pair rank, then kickers). `compareHands`, `rankPlayers` (best first, with ties grouped) and `explainResult` (the sentences above, built from the comparison). Duplicate or malformed cards are rejected.
- UI in `components/` as its own file (`hand-checker.tsx`) rather than inside the 11,000-line `poker-ledger.tsx`, reusing the existing `mini-cards` card style and the sheet component. `poker-ledger.tsx` only gains the Home row and the screen switch (and in step 2 the showdown link). Note: `poker-ledger.tsx` currently has uncommitted quick-ledger work; step 1 is built on top of it without disturbing it, or after it is committed.
- No migration, no API, no privacy-page change (nothing is collected). `FEATURES.md` section 17 gains the checker; its money note ("It does not handle real money, payments or cards") becomes "…or cards in play; the hand checker only reads the cards you type, and keeps none". What's New entry and a line in `app/features/page.tsx` under playing a hand.

**Acceptance criteria:**
- `test/hands.test.ts`: every hand type; the wheel and steel wheel; the highest straight among six or seven in a row; the best five of six suited cards; three pairs (best two plus best kicker); two sets (full house); quads with the best kicker; the board plays (split); the counterfeited two pair; kicker wins and ties at every position; Royal Flush named as such; duplicates rejected; each explanation case produces the expected sentence.
- In the browser at 375 and 320 px, both themes: a full hand is entered with taps only, used cards can't be picked twice, the answer and explanation update as cards change, and a three-way split reads correctly.
- `npm run lint`, `npx tsc --noEmit`, `npm test`.

**User decisions (2026-10-12):** (1) board 5 + 2 cards per player; (2) Home first, the showdown link as a later separate step; (3) the name **Who Wins?**.

**Status:** plan accepted with the recommended answers.

**Step 1 built** (user: "build it"), in worktree `.claude/worktrees/who-wins` on branch `claude/who-wins` from `ac6b461`, because the main checkout held another session's uncommitted quick-ledger work. `lib/poker/hands.ts` (evaluator, `judgeShowdown`, `handDetail`), `components/hand-checker.tsx` (the screen and picker), view `whoWins` and the Home row in `poker-ledger.tsx`, `.who-*` styles in `globals.css`, 37 tests in `test/hands.test.ts`. **Choices made while building:** the picker is a suit row plus a 7-column rank grid (13 ranks × 4 suits in one grid is too small to tap at 320 px), remembering the last suit and opening a filled slot at its card's suit; players without cards are skipped (folded), players with one card left out with a note; the **Why** and **Good to know** sentences use sentence case like the info pages' long text (the rest of the screen keeps the Title Case house style) — the user to confirm; the headline keeps the uppercase heading style. Checked in the browser on a worktree testbed server (port 3007) at 375 and 320 px, dark and light: a flush-beats-straight hand entered by taps, a three-way split with the three-pairs note, used cards faded, reopening and removing a card. **Changes after review (user request, 2026-10-12):** (1) each player row is a dropdown of the host's players (own player first, as in Table Setup; names chosen in other rows left out) ending in **Type a name…**, which swaps to a name box that returns to the dropdown when left empty; the screen gets the list through `knownPlayers` and reloads players when it opens. (2) The answer no longer appears by itself: a **Check Winner** button, enabled once the board and two players are complete, shows the Winner and Every hand cards and scrolls to them; any change to cards or players hides them again until it is pressed. Checked in the browser at 375 and 320 px. (3) **No silent skipping (user report with a screenshot: Player 3 had no cards, yet the winner was named between the other two):** every row now needs both cards; Check Winner stays disabled and a red warning names them, "Enter the cards for Player 3, or remove them, to check the winner." (without "or remove them" when only two rows are left). Worded without "Player 3's" because the house Title Case shows it as "3'S". This replaces the plan's "players left empty are ignored". The warning shows only once the board is full, so a fresh screen isn't red. (4) **Sizes (user feedback with a screenshot: cards too small, name fields too long):** players' cards match the board cards (`clamp(44px, 15vw, 58px)`: 56 px at 375, 48 px at 320) and the name dropdown takes what is left with tighter padding (143 px at 375 with ×; at 320 a long name such as "Bikram Big Host" is cut short). (5) **Layout (user request, 2026-10-13):** each player is a bordered box with the name dropdown and × on top and the two cards below, so full names fit (dropdown 247 px at 375, 200 px at 320); the tighter dropdown padding from (4) was dropped. Next: user review, commit and merge with the quick-ledger work (expect small conflicts in `poker-ledger.tsx`, `globals.css`, `changelog.ts` and the docs).

2026-10-12 quick ledger (user request: "implement 3", item 3 of the build order from the feature review; built with the agent's choices below, since the user delegates trade-offs to the long-term option). **Problem:** most home hosts don't want to tap every bet into a phone; they only track who bought in and the final chips, so hand-by-hand play is the reason some hosts would not use the app. **Model:** a quick ledger is a `GameState` with `kind: "ledger"` (no hand, `handNo` 0, `ante` 0, empty blind fields; a seated player's `stack` is 0 until they cash out, when it holds what they left with). Reusing `GameState` keeps device storage, backups and restore working unchanged. Pure rules in `lib/poker/ledger.ts` (`createLedgerGame`, `ledgerBuyIn`, `ledgerJoin`, `ledgerCashOut`, `ledgerReturn`, `undoLedgerStep`, `ledgerTotals`, `ledgerCountGap`, `ledgerSession`); tests in `test/ledger.test.ts`. **Saved games:** `PokerSession.kind = "ledger"` with `hands` 0 and `ante` 0 and no blind history, ante, rake or rebuy limits; `validateSession` enforces that shape, `isSameSession` compares the kind, and `continuationError` refuses to continue one. **Database (0025):** `poker_sessions.game_kind text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'hands'` ('hands' or 'ledger'); the old `ante > 0` and `hands > 0` checks are replaced by one per-kind check (`poker_sessions_kind_shape`; ledger rows must have ante 0, hands 0 and none of the per-hand rules); the migration stops if any other `ante > 0`/`hands > 0` check remains under another name. Existing rows become 'hands' and pass unchanged. `admin_game_row` also returns `kind`. **Agent choices (user may revisit):** (1) the final count must balance exactly before saving, because the server's accounting check requires it and an unbalanced game can't be ranked; the screen says how much it is short or over. (2) Anyone seated can buy in at any time (top-ups included), with any amount above 0; a different amount turns on own amounts, as in hand-by-hand games. (3) Cash-out records the chips typed by the host; Return puts those chips back on the table. (4) Undo takes back the last 30 changes (snapshots of the player list in `GameState.ledgerUndo`). (5) Not in this version: the live standings link (stacks are unknown until the count), continuing a saved quick ledger, and switching a game between the two kinds. (6) The admin Overview's hands-per-game average still divides by every game; left as is. **Checked on the testbed** (2026-10-12, Asha at 375 px): setup with own amounts (Farhan 5,000), a 10,000 rebuy, Sanjay joining for 10,000, Asha cashing out with 18,000, Undo and a reload keeping the game, backups sent, a count 1,000 over blocking Save, then a balanced save as Game 10 (Quick ledger card, no Continue, in the standings graph, Last session on Home); the Home live card and Discard of a second game (its backup deleted). The hand-by-hand setup still shows every card and Deal First Hand. tsc, lint (app code) and 353 tests pass.

2026-10-12 quick ledger from Home (user request: separate buttons on Home instead of a toggle at the top of the setup page, so Home has more to show). **Built:** the setup's "Record the game" card and its info sheet are gone; `SetupView` takes `kind` from `PokerLedger`'s `setupKind`, and is keyed by it so the two setups never share typed values. Home shows the green **Start a game** card (unchanged) and, under it, a glass **Quick ledger** card (`.home-ledger-card`: blue "Just the money" kicker, "Quick ledger" title, "Buy-ins and cash-outs, then count the chips", blue ♣ button); both show only with no game running. The quick ledger setup is headed **New Quick Ledger**. **Agent choice:** the Play tab opens the hand-by-hand setup, except that tapping it while on the quick ledger setup keeps that setup. Checked on the testbed at 375 px (dark and light): both cards, back gesture, the Play tab cases, and starting and discarding a quick ledger from the new card.

2026-10-13 quick ledger, remaining checks (user asked to test the four untested paths; testbed, Asha). **1. Restore on another device:** a cents quick ledger with a 25.50 rebuy was backed up; this browser was given a new device ID and its local copy removed; Home showed "Unfinished game · Game 12 · Quick ledger · 3 players · saved just now on another device", and Restore brought back the amounts, the rebuy, the log and the Undo step ("Game 12 restored"). **2. Cents:** a 50 buy-in made a cents game (stored as hundredths); Farhan cashed out with 60.25, the count 40 + 75.25 balanced at ₹115.25, and Game 12 saved as +₹10.25 / −₹0.25 / −₹10. **3. Admin dashboard:** the Overview's recent games read "Quick Ledger" with +₹10.25; the Games table shows "—" for hands and "Ledger" for blinds (hand-by-hand Game 79 unchanged), and Game 12's detail shows In ₹50 · Out ₹60.25. **4. Export and import:** the export (caught in the page, nothing downloaded) carries `kind`, `chipUnit` and buy-ins; importing it again said "Already up to date"; a copy of Game 12 under a new ID showed as "Quick ledger" on the review screen and saved intact ("Added 1 session", no Continue, no blind history); a tampered copy claiming 3 hands was refused with "A quick ledger game has no hands, blinds, ante, rake or rebuy limits" and nothing was saved. All four passed; no code changes were needed.

2026-10-13 chip value and settle up (user request: "except for the upi id and links, implement the chip value and settle up feature", item 4 of the feature review's build order). **Problem:** the currency sign was only a label for chips, so a game where 10,000 chips cost ₹500 showed results of "+₹12,000" and the app couldn't say who owes whom. **Decisions (agent's, under the user's standing "long-term, debuggable" preference; the user can overturn any):** (1) The chip value is optional per game, `{ chips, cents }`: that many stored chips (in the game's chip unit) cost that many hundredths of the host's currency. Absent means a chip is one unit, so every older game reads exactly as before. Two integers, not a rate, so nothing is lost to floating point and the stored value reads as the host typed it. (2) The host types the price of the starting stack ("A 10,000-chip buy-in costs ₹500"), the number people know; own amounts and rebuys of other sizes are priced through the same ratio. (3) A valued game shows plain chip counts while it's played (table, log, Home card, live link), because "₹10,000" would be wrong money; money appears on the saved game card, standings, Profile and group totals, and Settle up. This is done with a display code (`plainChipsCode`, `chipCurrency` in `lib/poker/money.ts`) rather than new formatting paths, so every existing formatter follows. The ranking is unchanged: returns are ratios. (4) The chip value isn't part of a game's content: it can be set or changed after saving (`PATCH /api/sessions`, action `chip-value`, audit event `session.chip_value` with the value before), is left out of `isSameSession` (a retry or re-import still matches), and saving a continued game doesn't overwrite it. It can't be changed while a game is played (a known gap; Settle up covers forgetting). (5) Money results are rounded to the hundredth so they add up exactly to minus the rake: each rounded down, the missing hundredths to the largest remainders (`sessionMoney` in `lib/poker/settle-up.ts`), with BigInt so large stacks can't overflow. Standings, Profile stats and group standings all use it, so every screen shows the same amount for a game. (6) Settle up gives the fewest transfers exactly: people are split into the most groups that settle among themselves (a bitmask search, at most 11 people with the house), then within a group the biggest debt pays the biggest credit; transfers ≤ people − 1. Rake is owed to "The house". (7) Settle up opens by itself after every save (the moment people settle) and from a **Settle up** button on each saved game card; **Share** sends a plain-text list (or **Copy** without a share menu). No payment links or UPI IDs, by the user's choice. A note under the list ("N payments settle everyone, the fewest possible. Pokerize only keeps score: no money goes through the app.") was removed at the user's request (2026-10-13). **Data:** `poker_sessions.chip_value_chips` and `chip_value_cents` (both NULL or both positive, price at most 100,000,000,000 hundredths), `friend_group_sessions()` returns `chipValue`; no other personal data is collected, so the privacy pages don't change. **Checked:** tsc, lint, 413 tests (21 new in `test/settle-up.test.ts`); on the testbed at 375 px as Asha: a quick ledger at ₹500 per 10,000 (Game 14: +₹600, +₹150, −₹250, −₹500, three payments, as in the review's example), the price changed to ₹1,000 from Settle up (everything doubled), Copy text, a hand-by-hand game at the same price showing plain chips in play and on the live link (`plainChips` in the view, no price sent) and settling at ₹2.50. Older games without a value read as before (Game 12's +₹10.25).


2026-10-13 results card (plan; user request: "plan the results card", item 5 of the feature review's build order). **Problem:** after a game, results reach the group chat as typed text or a screenshot of the app, if at all. Nothing shows where they came from, so the app is never seen by people who aren't already using it. Settle up's **Share** sends plain text (who pays whom), which is for paying people back, not something you'd want to show off. **Idea:** a picture of the night's results, made for WhatsApp, with the Pokerize name and `pokerize.app` on it. It's the cheapest way people outside the app will ever see it.

**Proposed behaviour:**
- **Where.** A **Results card** button in the Settle up sheet, beside **Share**. Settle up already opens by itself after every save and from every saved game card, so the card is reachable at the moment people want it and from any old game, with no new button on the game cards (decluttered the same day).
- **Who.** Only the host, for their own saved games (not discarded ones). A friend viewing a group never sees other players' money (section 20), so they get no card.
- **The sheet.** Tapping opens a **Results card** sheet that loads the picture and shows it (a spinner while it loads), with **Share image** (the phone's share menu with the PNG, plus the text "Scored with Pokerize · https://pokerize.app" where the target accepts text) and **Done**. Where the phone can't share files, the button reads **Save image** and downloads it. The picture is loaded *before* the Share button is pressed on purpose: iOS Safari refuses to share when the tap is followed by a network wait, so fetching on tap would fail there.
- **What's on it** (1080 × 1350, the 4:5 shape WhatsApp shows without cropping; always the dark Scoreboard look, whatever the phone's theme):
  - top: the Pokerize mark and name
  - the game's name, date and length ("Game 14 · 13 Oct 2026 · 3 h 20 min"), then one line on how it was played: "42 hands · Blinds ₹50/₹100 → ₹200/₹400" for hand by hand, "Quick ledger" for a quick ledger
  - the night's winner, large, with their avatar, result and return (for example "+12,000 · +120%")
  - everyone, biggest winner first, as on the saved game card: rank, avatar, name, result, return, and "2 rebuys" when they rebought. Up to 10 rows (a game's maximum, counting those who left); long names are cut with "…"
  - with rake, a line "House rake 1,200"
  - bottom: "Scored with Pokerize · pokerize.app"
- **What's left off:** chip value and who pays whom (that's Settle up's text), the standings, anything from other games, and email addresses or user codes.

**Build (proposed, long-term choice):** the server draws the picture. `GET /api/sessions/[id]/card` checks that the signed-in host owns the game and that it isn't discarded, then renders it with Next's `ImageResponse` (`next/og`: JSX and flexbox in, PNG out). It's sent with `Cache-Control: private, no-store` and never stored. What goes on the card is worked out by a pure function, `resultsCard(session, players)` in `lib/poker/results-card.ts`. It handles order, ranks, returns, rebuy counts, cut names, the length and the blinds line, and gets unit tests. The route only lays that out. Fonts are Barlow and Big Shoulders as TTF files in the repo (both under the Open Font License, the same faces the app uses). Avatars are the existing `/avatars/*.svg`. **Why the server, not the phone:** drawing on a canvas on the phone means laying out every line by hand, and fonts and SVGs draw differently on iOS and Android. Libraries that screenshot the page (`html-to-image`) break on iOS fonts. The server route looks the same on every phone, can be opened in a browser or fetched in a test to check it, and keeps the layout in JSX like the rest of the app. **Costs:** it needs the network, as saving already does. A cold start on Vercel and Neon Free makes the first card take a few seconds, which the spinner covers. The `ImageResponse` bundle has to stay under 500 KB, so the fonts are cut to the characters used.

**Open questions (user to decide):**
1. **Money or chips on the card?** *Recommendation: chips and return %, no currency sign.* Money stays in Settle up's text, which goes only to the table. The card is meant to be forwarded beyond the table and carries the Pokerize name. A branded picture of "+₹600 won" is the thing most likely to be read as advertising a money game, under India's Promotion and Regulation of Online Gaming Act 2025 and under Google Play's gambling rules, just before a Play Store listing. Return % is also what the app ranks by, and it means the same at any stakes. The alternative is the same amounts as the saved game card (money when the game has a chip value). If you choose that, the fonts need a ₹ glyph, which Barlow may lack, so a fallback font is needed.
2. **Format:** one 4:5 picture (recommended), or also a tall 9:16 version for WhatsApp Status. That's a second layout to maintain, so add it only if people ask.
3. **Players' own copy:** the live link ends when the game is saved, so players can't get the card themselves. *Recommendation:* host only for now. A public results page would need its own link, expiry and privacy rules.

**Later (not in this step):** biggest pot and most pots won. The saved game doesn't record hands, so this needs a new saved field and a migration. Also how a player's place in the standings moved, and the 9:16 Status version.

**Docs when built:** `FEATURES.md` section 11 (Settle up), a What's New item, and the Features page's saved-games group. No new data is collected or stored, so the privacy pages don't change.

**Acceptance:** unit tests for `resultsCard`: order and ranks with ties, returns with rebuys, a player who cashed out early, rake, a quick ledger (no hands or blinds line), a cents game, 10 players, long names, and a game whose chips don't add up (no card, with the same message as Settle up). Route tests: a stranger's game gives 404, a discarded game 404, signed-out 401. Checked on the testbed at 375 px: the preview, Share image on a phone share sheet, Save image on desktop, and the PNG fetched and looked at for a hand-by-hand game with 10 players and a quick ledger with 3.

2026-10-13 results card built (user: "go with your recommendations, build it"). **Answers taken:** chips and return %, no currency sign; one 4:5 picture; host only. **Built:** pure `resultsCard()` in `lib/poker/results-card.ts` (order, shared ranks, returns, rebuy counts, names cut at 18 characters, length, blinds line, rake; throws like Settle up when chips don't balance), 17 tests in `test/results-card.test.ts`. `GET /api/sessions/[id]/card` (`app/api/sessions/[id]/card/route.tsx`) renders it with `next/og` on the Node runtime; 401 signed out, 404 for another host's, missing or discarded game, 422 when chips don't balance; `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; dates in the phone's time zone (`?tz=`, checked, India time otherwise). The saved-session query moved from `app/api/sessions/route.ts` to `lib/poker/saved-sessions.ts` so both routes read games the same way. Fonts are subset static copies in `assets/fonts` (how they were made is in its README); avatars and the logo are read from `public`; `outputFileTracingIncludes` in `next.config.ts` ships them with the route (checked in the build's trace file). UI: Settle up's Share becomes a pair, **Share** | **Results card**; `ResultsCardSheet` fetches the PNG when it opens (iOS needs the share call straight after the tap) and offers **Share image** (`navigator.canShare({ files })`) or **Save image** (download). **Changes from the plan, found while checking:** (1) from 7 players the winner panel is dropped and the winner's row is lit instead, because ten rows under the panel came out about 56 px tall, too small to read on a phone; (2) the blinds read "50/100 to 400/800", since Barlow has no arrow glyph. **Checked:** tsc, lint (errors only in the local design folders, as before), 430 tests, `next build`; on the testbed as Asha, the card for Game 1 (3 players, 2 rebuys) and a 10-player game with rising blinds, rake and long names (`card-test-10`, with 7 test guests added to Asha's list), the in-app sheet at 375 px (Save image in the desktop pane), 401 signed out and 404 for an unknown game. **Not checked:** Share image on a real phone (the preview pane can't share files).

2026-10-13 one-tap share and game card layout (user: "i would prefer a single click for sharing results instead of two clicks via settle up page. i think we can redesign the game history cards as now there is settle up, continue game, details and share results"). **Card layout:** a green **Share** pill (share icon) top right beside the name, the main action; the results; then a footer under a line with a **Details** toggle on the left (a button with a turning triangle, replacing the native `<details>`, whose content opens below the footer) and **Settle up** / **Continue game** pills on the right; discarded games keep **Restore** / **Delete** there and have no Share. Share is hidden when the saved chips don't add up (no card can be made). **One tap (agent's design):** iOS Safari only lets a page open the share menu straight after a tap, so a tap that waits on the server can be refused. So: (1) cards are cached per page (`loadResultsCard`, keyed by game ID, end time and results, reused 5 minutes so renames show up soon); (2) the newest saved game's card is fetched in the background 1.5 s after Games opens, and a game's card 1 s after its Settle up opens (which happens after every save), so the usual share is instant; (3) a tap shares at once with the cached or freshly fetched file (`shareResultsCard`), downloads it where files can't be shared, and only if the phone refuses (anything but AbortError) opens the existing **Results Card** sheet with the picture ready for a second tap. Cost: one extra card render per Games visit and per save, cheap at today's use. Settle up's pair is now **Share list** (the payments text, primary) and **Share card** (the same one-tap share). **Checked** on the testbed at 375 px with `navigator.share` replaced in the page: the newest card was fetched in the background and shared without a new request; an older card was fetched on tap then shared; a refused share opened the sheet with the picture and **Share image**; Details opens and closes; quick ledger cards show only Settle up (and Details with a chip value). Not checked on a real phone.

2026-10-13 results card preview kept (user: "i didnt mean for you to remove the results card preview. keep that"). The user wanted Share on the game card itself, not a share that skips the preview. Now **Share** on a game card, and **Results card** in Settle up, open the **Results Card** sheet (picture, the "chips and returns, not money" note, **Share image** / **Save image**, **Done**); the one-tap share and its iOS fallback are removed. The background loading of the newest game's card (and on Settle up) stays, so the preview usually opens at once. The sheet is rendered on `document.body` (`createPortal`), because inside a game card the glass card's styling held the fixed-position sheet within the card. Settle up's pair is **Share list** | **Results card**. This replaces the one-tap part of the entry above; the card layout stands.

2026-10-13 results card sheet trimmed (user requests): under its title the sheet shows only the picture; the game's name and date and the note "A picture to post in the group. It shows chips and returns, not money." are removed.
