The sheet scrolls when the screen is too short.

2026-10-13 one New Game screen (user: the Play tab opened only the hand-by-hand setup, and Home already had both setups, "redundant and also incomplete"; user chose "the single card"). **Built:** a new `newGame` view (`NewGameView`, headed **New Game**) with the two existing cards: green **Hand by hand** (kicker "Blinds and bets") and the glass **Quick ledger**. Home's no-game state is now one green **Start a game** card ("Hand by hand, or a quick ledger") that opens it. The **Play** tab opens the game in progress, otherwise New Game; on New Game or a setup it does nothing (back returns to the choice). `navigate()` takes `setupKind` and stores it in the history entry, and `popstate` restores it, so back/forward reopen the setup that was chosen. This replaces the 2026-10-12 agent choice that the Play tab opens the hand-by-hand setup. Setups are unchanged and still have no switch between kinds. Checked on the testbed (3006) at 375 px as Asha, dark and light: Home card, Play → New Game → each setup, back/forward, Play on a setup, Play with a quick ledger running (opens it), discard returns Home.

2026-10-13 New Game cards made equal, tournament placeholder (user: the two cards "have the same styles, their dimensions are different" and hand by hand felt prioritised; "add a tournament card but keep it disabled ... coming soon"). **Built:** `NewGameView` renders `GAME_KINDS` as identical glass cards (`.game-kind-card`, accent from `--kind`: green `--g` hand by hand ♦, blue `--b` quick ledger ♣, grey `--ink3` tournament ♥); the grid uses `grid-auto-rows: 1fr` so every card takes the tallest card's height (128.5 px at 375 px). **Tournament** ("Last one standing · Rising blinds, play to a winner") is a disabled button with a **Coming soon** pill instead of the round go button; only its words are dimmed (the global disabled opacity is overridden). The old `.home-ledger-*` styles are removed; Home's green **Start a game** card is unchanged. Tournament play itself is not planned in detail yet: acceptance criteria to be written here before building it.

2026-10-16 results card loading (user report: cards "loading slowly and sometimes not even being generated even after long", with screenshots of "Making the card…" and "Couldn't make the card."). **Findings:** Vercel's request logs (Hobby keeps about an hour) held four of the user's card requests from Chrome on a Mac: all 200, function time 0.68 to 1.02 s, "response finished" 1.5 to 1.9 s, received in Mumbai and run in Washington (iad1) beside Neon (us-east-2). Drawing is not the cost: a local benchmark of the same next/og renderer took 140 ms for the first picture and about 50 ms after. The production build against the testbed loaded five cards in a row without a failure (about 0.8 s each). So the wait is the trip to the US plus the server or Neon waking up, and older failures had already left the logs. Two weaknesses could still produce "Couldn't make the card." with nothing logged: the picture was drawn while the body streamed, after the 200 was sent, so a drawing error reached the phone as a broken download; and the client waited on a stalled request forever, with no retry. **Changes:** (1) the route draws the PNG in full before replying, sends Content-Length, logs drawing errors as a 500 and adds `render` to Server-Timing; (2) the client drops a card request after 20 s and retries a failed one once after 0.5 s (not a 4xx refusal); (3) each game's card loads in the background when its Share button scrolls into view (IntersectionObserver), one at a time through a queue, replacing the newest-only preload; loaded cards are kept 30 minutes instead of 5. **Checked** on the production build (`next start`) against the testbed: cards on screen loaded in turn and more loaded on scroll; Server-Timing `render;dur=98`; a forced network failure on the first card was retried and the preview opened. Not checked: the 20 s limit (not waited out) and a real phone. **Cost:** up to one render per game card shown in Games (5 at first), at most once per 30 minutes per page.

2026-10-16 long game names on the results card (user report with a screenshot: "Recovered session from screenshot" wrapped to two lines and ran over the date and hands lines, because the title block had a fixed one-line height). **Fix:** the title block grows with the name and the rows' height budget counts it; `cardTitleSize()` in `lib/poker/results-card.ts` draws names of up to 22 characters at 72 px, up to 34 at 56 px on one line, and longer at 56 px on up to two lines; names are cut with "…" at 56 characters (`CARD_TITLE_LENGTH`; game names may be 80); a single very long word breaks inside the word. **Checked** on the testbed with six test games (`title-33`, `title-26w`, `title-34w`, `title-56`, `title-80`, `title-word`, saved in Asha's ledger for this): none overlap; 19 results-card tests.

2026-10-26 new players hidden in the standings (user report: a new player led their standings at +186.25% after 2 sessions; "we discarded the option to keep new players separate or mark them ... now i think we gotta bring it back"). **User's design:** a switch on the right of the "Player Standings" heading, off by default; off shows only players with more than 5 sessions, on adds players with 5 or fewer. **Agent's calls:** sessions means *eligible* (ranked) sessions, since only those feed the score; with the switch off the rest are re-ranked 1…N (ties shared) and their graph lines are hidden too, so list and graph agree; with it on the ranking is the full one as before and new players get a "New · N ranked sessions" line; the switch is not remembered (off on every visit); "View standings" on a new player opens with it on; a friend's group uses the same rule on the games the friend shared (its graph lines are filtered by row position); an **i** beside the switch (user request, same day) opens "About new players", which explains it, so the standings "i" stays about the ranking; **placement** (user, same day): the heading turned out crowded, so the switch and its "i" moved to their own row under the heading with a count beside it ("1 new player hidden"; user then asked for the switch on the left and the count on the right); a row above the graph was tried and rejected as "far reaching and quite away from the actual player standings"; when nobody qualifies, a note points to the switch. The host's own row is hidden too when the host is new: one rule for everyone. Pure rule in `lib/poker/standings.ts` (`NEW_PLAYER_MAX_SESSIONS = 5`, `isNewPlayer`, `withoutNewPlayers`), display only: no API, server or migration change, and `STANDINGS_METRIC_VERSION` stays 1 because scores don't change. **Why 5:** with session returns that swing by tens of percent, an average over 2–3 games is mostly luck; 6 games is about two months of a fortnightly game, so regulars qualify quickly while a newcomer's one or two big nights can't top the table. A higher bar (8–10) would hide too much of a small group. Revisit if groups grow or play more often. **Checked** on the testbed at 375 px as Bikram: off, 15 players ranked 1–15 (Bikram, 4 ranked sessions, hidden); on, 16 with Bikram at 13 marked "New · 4 Ranked Sessions"; Asha's group 6 players off, 11 on. 19 standings tests.

2026-10-26 new players: new groups and yourself (user: "for new users of the app, they themselves will have played less than 5 games"). **Problem:** a new host saw an empty list by default; a friend's group counts only shared games, so the viewer has the most sessions there and the whole list stayed hidden until they had played 6 games with that host; a new host with regulars (an import) was hidden from their own standings. **Options weighed:** A, filter only once there are regulars; B, always show yourself; C, a threshold relative to the group's longest record (self-adjusting but hard to explain, and the bar moves); D, turn the switch on when the list would be empty (fixes only the new host, and the switch seems to move by itself). **User chose A + B, with 3 regulars.** **Built:** `filterNewPlayers()` in `lib/poker/standings.ts` (`NEW_PLAYER_FILTER_MIN_REGULARS = 3`) decides for both views: with fewer than 3 players on 6+ ranked sessions everyone shows, the switch is greyed and shown on, the row reads "Everyone shown for now", and no "New" lines appear (in a new group everyone would be new); otherwise the switch works as before, and a hidden reader (`isMe`) comes back as `pinnedSelf`, an unranked card below Show all reading "New · 4 of 6 sessions to be ranked". The empty-list note is gone (it can no longer happen). The "About new players" sheet adds "Everyone is shown until 3 players have played 6 sessions." **Checked** on the testbed at 375 px: Bikram (4 ranked sessions in his own ledger of regulars) sees 15 ranked players and his own pinned card; Meera's ledger (3 players, 2 games) shows all 3 ranked with the switch greyed. 23 standings tests.

2026-10-26 regulars from 5 sessions (user: "players should be considered old once they have played 5 sessions and not 6"). `NEW_PLAYER_MAX_SESSIONS` is now 4: new means 4 ranked sessions or fewer, and hiding starts once 3 players have 5 or more. Every number the app shows comes from the constant ("Players with 4 sessions or fewer are hidden", "until 3 players have played 5 sessions", "New · 3 of 5 sessions to be ranked"). The two entries above keep the numbers they were written with.

2026-10-26 "About new players" reworded (user: say who is shown, not who is hidden, and drop the "Everyone is shown until 3 players have played 5 sessions" line). The sheet now reads: "Players with 5 or more sessions are **regulars** and are shown in the rankings. Players with fewer than 5 are **newcomers** and are hidden, so a few lucky games can't put them on top. Turn this on to include them." The 3-regulars rule is unchanged; only the row's "Everyone shown for now" hints at it.

2026-10-26 switch renamed **Newcomers** (user request), to match the sheet's wording. The row reads "1 newcomer hidden", "Showing 1 newcomer" or "No newcomers"; the sheet is "About newcomers". Player cards keep the short "New · …" line. Then (user: "looks like a big text blob") the sheet became two bullets, and (user: "too to the point ... i liked the previous simple english flow") they keep full sentences: "Players with 5 or more sessions are **regulars** and are shown in the rankings." and "Players with fewer than 5 sessions are **newcomers** and are hidden, so a few lucky games can't put them on top.", then "Turn this on to include newcomers in the rankings."

2026-10-26 quieter labels (user, with screenshots: "keep only 'new' for newcomers and remove the status updates of whether everyone and newcomers are being shown or hidden"). Newcomers' cards, including your own pinned card, now say just "New" under the name (no session counts), and the Newcomers row holds only the switch and its "i": no "1 newcomer hidden", "Showing 1 newcomer", "No newcomers" or "Everyone shown for now". While there are fewer than 3 regulars the switch is still shown on and greyed out, now without a line saying why. `filterNewPlayers()` no longer returns a count.

2026-10-26 Total on standing cards (user, with a screenshot of a card's details: "add total current amount i.e invested + profit/loss"). A **Total** stat sits between Invested and Net in every standing card's details (first placed after Net; the user then asked to swap them): Invested + Net, rounded to hundredths so amounts with cents add exactly, shown in the host's currency without a sign or colour. It needs both amounts, so in a friend's group it shows only on your own row, like Invested and Net; nothing new is sent. Named "Total" after the user's words; "Cashed out" was avoided because it already means leaving a game early.

2026-10-26 cards say "Newcomer" (user request), not "New", matching the switch and the "About newcomers" sheet.

2026-10-26 friends' games on Games (user request: "similar to how a user can see their rankings in games hosted by other users ... add the option to view those games"). **User decision:** a friend's game card shows **everyone's result**, as at the table. This relaxes the 2026-10-05 "group standings privacy" rule ("no money for anyone but the viewer") for single games only; the standings still send no other player's invested or net totals, though those can now be added up from the games. Other rules from that entry hold: only games the viewer played in, no game names or numbers, no host totals. **Built:** `buildGroupStandings` (`lib/friends/group-standings.ts`) adds `sharedGames` (newest first: date, hands, chip unit, starting stack, rake, chip value, and per player name, avatar, net, final chips, buy-ins, `isMe`; no IDs), so `/api/groups` carries them and no migration is needed. Games (`SessionsView` in `components/poker-ledger.tsx`) gets the Ranks picker (shared `HostPicker`); a friend's games render as `GroupGameCard` ("Name's game", date · hands · players, every result in money at the chip value, buy-ins, **You** tag; no buttons, no Export/Import). The title reads **Games** when the picker shows. Groups now refresh on opening Games too. Ranks' **i** list dropped "the host's game-by-game history" and now says other players' totals stay hidden. Docs: `FEATURES.md` section 11 and 20, privacy page (date 2026-10-26), Features page, What's New 2026-10-26. **Checks:** tsc, lint, group-standings tests (2 new); on the testbed (3006) at 375 px as Asha: picker lists My Hosted Games and Bikram Big Host's Hosted Games; Bikram's shows 26 shared games five at a time with "1 Hand" / "30 Hands", Win tag, You tag and buy-ins; Ranks unchanged; no console errors. **Not yet:** commit, push, a real phone.

2026-10-26 friends' games: Details and Settle up (user: "include game details and settle-up buttons for other host games too but continuing a game can only be done by the host"). **Built:** migration `0027_friend_game_details` re-creates `friend_group_sessions()` with each game's `kind`, `ante`, `blindHistory`, `rebuyRules`, `bigBlindAnte` and `rakeRules` (no table change, no name or number); `buildGroupStandings` passes them on in `sharedGames` (optional, absent before 0027). The client turns each friend's game into a `PokerSession` named "Name's game" with a made-up id (`groupGameSession`) and shows it on the same `SessionCard` (new `friendGame` and `youIndex` props: no results-card **Share**, a **You** tag; no `onContinue`, so no **Continue game**). **Settle up** opens the same `SettleUpSheet` (portalled to the body) without `onSaveChipValue`: no **Change**/**Set price**, "The host set no price.", and **Share list**/**Copy list** alone (the results card is made by the server for the host only). A hand-by-hand game with no big blind (a friend's game sent before 0027) skips Big blind and the blind sections instead of showing 0. **Agent choices:** a friend can't change the price, since it changes the host's saved game; the results card stays host only (its API serves only the owner's games). **Checks:** tsc, lint on changed files, tests (1 new); 0027 applied to the testbed only (scratch script with the testbed owner URL, no password rotation); at 375 px as Asha on Bikram's games: cards show **Details** and **Settle Up** only; Details lists Hands 1, Big blind, Finished at, blind plan and history; Settle Up shows "The host set no price.", results, who pays whom, **Copy list** and **Done**; Asha's own cards still show Share, Details, Settle Up, Continue Game and Set Price / Results Card; no console errors. **Not yet:** rehearse 0027 on development `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` and apply to production `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` (each with the user's go-ahead; the code works without it, showing Hands only under Details), commit, push, a real phone.

2026-10-26 friends' games: Share, and Settle up in the same place (user: "include the share button as well. also swap the places of continue game and settle up so that the positions remain similar between the users and other hosts' games"). **Built:** friends' cards get **Share** and Settle up gets **Results card** back. The picture is drawn by a new `GET /api/groups/card?host=<account id>&date=<start ms>&tz=` (the phone has no game IDs, so a game is found by host and start time among the caller's `friend_group_sessions()`, via `findSharedGame`; a game the caller didn't play in, another host or a bad request get 404/400). The drawing code moved from `app/api/sessions/[id]/card/route.tsx` to `lib/poker/results-card-image.tsx` (`renderResultsCard`), shared by both routes; `next.config.ts` traces the fonts, avatars and logo into the new route too. The client's made-up friend game id is `friend:<host>:<date>`, and `resultsCardPath` turns it into that URL. The card's title is "Name's game", with the names and avatars from that game. 0027 also returns `ended` now, for the card's game length (amended before it reached development or production; re-run on the testbed). On every card **Continue game** now sits left of **Settle up**, so Settle up is rightmost on both. **Checks:** tsc, lint on changed files, 443 tests (2 new), production build; testbed at 375 px as Asha: own card Share / Details / Continue Game / Settle Up; Bikram's card Share / Details / Settle Up; the friend's picture drawn (title, "3 min", winner panel, five rows); Settle Up Copy list / Results Card / Done; host cards still 200. **0027 rehearsed on development** `br-little-rain-ay5fufwv` the same day with the user's go-ahead (one transaction, counts unchanged, function runs for every account; `MIGRATION-REHEARSALS.md`). **0027 applied to production** `br-small-sea-ayumyssr` the same day with the user's go-ahead (one transaction, counts unchanged; read-only, 7 accounts see 104 friends' games, all with the new fields). Committed and pushed as `4a60af7` (Vercel success). **Not yet:** a real phone.

2026-10-26 starting stacks and buy-ins under Details (user: "include the starting stack of players. if its the same then write it once and if its different then write separately. shift buy-in's in the details tab as well"). **Built (`SessionCardBody`, every game card, own and friends'):** each player's buy-ins in seat order (none saved means the game's starting stack); the first is their starting stack. All the same: a **Starting stack** row first in Details; different (Own amounts, or a late join for another amount): a **Starting stacks** list, one row per player. **Buy-ins** lists each player who bought in more than once as "₹10,000 + ₹10,000 = ₹20,000"; the "Buy-ins ₹X" tag beside names is gone. Every game now has a Details toggle (quick ledgers without a chip value had none). No server change. **User question answered:** cash-outs and late joins aren't in Details because a saved game doesn't record them: per player it keeps the buy-in amounts in order, final chips and net (`buy_in_events.created_at` is the save time, not when it happened). Showing them would need the save to record when each buy-in and cash-out happened (a migration); not planned. **Checks:** tsc, lint; testbed at 375 px as Asha: Diwali Night Showdown shows "Starting stack ₹10,000" and two buy-in lines; Game 10 lists four starting stacks (Farhan ₹5,000); Bikram's 12 Oct game lists five different stacks and Asha's "$5,000 + $7,500 = $12,500"; no buy-in tags beside names.

2026-10-26 late joins, cash-outs and hands played (user: "add cash-outs and late join details as it is valuable info to look back at past games ... not every player will play the same number of hands so write that separately for each player similar to starting stacks. also instead of just writing Big Blind, write Blinds and give the values of small and big blinds"). **Design (agent's choices, long-term and debuggable):** a game started from today carries `GameState.tracksPlayers`; older games in progress and older saved games can't know these for hands already played, so they record nothing and show nothing new. On `Player`: `handsPlayed` (counted at each deal for players dealt in; Cancel hand takes it back, Undo last hand restores `CompletedHand.handsPlayedBefore`), `joinedHand`/`joinedAt` (a join after the first hand: the next hand's number and the time), `leftHand`/`leftAt` (a cash-out: the last hand dealt and the time; cleared by Return). Quick ledgers record times only. Saving (`savedPlayerRecord`) drops a hand still in progress (it's refunded) and keeps hand numbers within the saved hands. Saved per result in five nullable `session_results` columns (migration `0028_seat_records`), validated on the server (`parsePlayerRecord`: within the game's hands and times, a join from hand 2, a cash-out not before the join, no more hands than the player was there for, every player or none, no hands in a quick ledger) and part of the retry comparison. Continue game restores them, so a cashed-out player now stays out (before, everyone was seated again), and refuses a continuation that loses earlier hands or changes a join. Friends' games carry them. **Card:** Details shows **Blinds** "₹40/₹100" (was Big blind), a **Hands played** list when not everyone played every hand, **Joined late** ("From hand 16 · 10:25 am") and **Cashed out** ("After hand 22 · 10:50 am · ₹8,000"). Rejected: recording when each rebuy happened (not asked; would need a hand on every buy-in event). **Found and fixed on the way:** `continuationError` compared the small blind share and rake rules as JSON text, but the saved copy comes back from jsonb with its keys in another order, so a continued game with an odd small blind or with rake could never be saved again; now compared by value (test fails on the old code). Also `resolveSession` in the sessions API rebuilt results field by field and dropped the new fields; it now keeps the whole result. **Checks:** tsc, lint on changed files, 468 tests (24 new in `test/seat-records.test.ts`, one each in continue-session and group-standings; the ledger save test now expects the cash-out time), production build. Testbed (0028 applied): saved "Seat record check 2" through the API, Details showed every block; Continue kept Bikram cashed out, hand 41 dealt to the other three, saved through the app with 41/22/41/26 hands. An earlier test save "Seat record check" (made before the `resolveSession` fix) has no record. **Released 2026-10-26** with the user's go-ahead: 0028 rehearsed on development (behaviour check passed) and applied to production (one transaction, counts unchanged), then the code committed and pushed. **Not yet:** a real phone.

2026-10-27 undo across streets (user: Undo "works only in-between rounds and not across it. once the flop is dealt, the undo button goes away"; wanted undo for a whole hand, preflop to river). **Built:** `dealNextStreet()` in `lib/poker/game.ts` (replacing the component's inline street change) keeps the street just played in `Hand.streets` (`StreetRecord`: committed, acted, last actions, round high, raise size and who may raise, current player). `undoableStreet()` names the street once it is dealt and before anyone acts on it; `undoStreet()` restores the street before. No chips move on a deal, so stacks, pot and the log need no change. The one Undo button (`UndoTurnButton`, List and Table view) reads **Undo Flop/Turn/River** at that moment, then names the restored street's last action, so a hand steps back to its first preflop action. Hands dealt before the change have no `streets` and stop at their current street. No migration (live state only). **Checked:** 2 new tests (480 total), tsc, lint on the changed files; testbed (3006): call, check, Deal FLOP, Undo Flop, Undo check, Undo call, back to the opening decision with the pot unchanged; Undo Turn on a pre-change hand returned to the flop.

2026-10-27 betting card redesign (user, with a screenshot: remove "behind"; the stack and "In ₹x" are too small, "In" resets each street though players ask what they've put in this hand, To call and the minimum are too small, the slider's uneven steps go unused, and the amount box's greyed placeholder confused people). Agent's mockup reviewed in chat. **User decisions:** remove the slider; the amount box takes the raise-to total (empty until typed); the stack and hand total show on every player card. **Built:** the active card has the stack large at the top right and three tiles: In this hand (`handContributions`, blinds and ante included), To call, Min bet / Min raise to (with the i). The box is captioned Bet or Raise to, has no placeholder, and its value, the quick sizes and the button are all street totals; chips to put in are the total minus what's in this street. Bet toasts name totals ("Minimum is ₹200", "The most is ₹10,800 (all in)"). Other players' rows show the stack large with "₹700 in hand" under it. The round-closed card follows the same layout. `betStops` and `BET_PRESET_MULTIPLES` were removed with the slider. **Checked:** tsc, lint on changed files, 478 tests; testbed in Table and List view (½ Pot filled the box with the total, the button matched, Clear amount reset it). Not exercised in the browser: a raise over an existing bet (the testbed hand was someone's live river), the round-closed card and light theme. **Follow-up (user, same day):** the green "To act · street" pill (the card already shows whose turn it is; the list view's "Your turn" pill went with it) and the "₹x this street" line under In this hand were removed. Then (user: the empty strip at the bottom; the "Clear amount to call or fold" link read as an instruction, not a button) the link and its reserved 26 px slot are gone: with an amount in, the box's pencil becomes a round × button (`bet-input-clear`) that clears it. Checked on the testbed: ½ Pot on a ₹500 bet filled "Raise to 1000" with Fold and Call off; × emptied the box and turned them on; the card stayed 339.5 px either way. Then (user: To call and Min raise to repeat the Call and Raise buttons; the amount box is too big) the To call and minimum tiles are gone, and **In this hand** and the amount box sit side by side at half width each. The box is now the bordered tile itself (`.bet-input`, a `div`, not a `label`, since it holds the i button), captioned Bet / Raise to with the i beside the caption; the number is 22 px. Checked at 375 px: All in's 10050 fits; the i opens the raise help. A short all-in (call or fold only) leaves In this hand full width. The round-closed card shows In this hand beside a disabled box captioned "Round complete". Then (user: put In this hand beside the stack so the money is together; take "Raise to" and the i out of the box, beside it) **In this hand** (20 px, muted) and **Stack** (26 px) sit side by side at the top right (`.seat-money`); the bet row is "Raise to ⓘ" then the bordered box filling the rest (24 px number). At 375 px the name gets about 88 px, so on this card it wraps to two lines instead of being cut ("Bikram Bhattacharya" checked). Card height at 375 px: 239 px, down from about 340. Then (user: use tiles like the removed To call / Min raise ones, Stack on the left, above Raise to) the header holds only the avatar and name again, and **Stack** and **In this hand** are two equal `.bet-stat` tiles (22 px values) at the top of the betting panel, above the "Raise to ⓘ" row; the round-closed card has the same tiles. The two-line name rule went with the header layout. Then (user: "make the UI of raise to and its text box feel more like they are together. make a div in a div") the "Raise to ⓘ" row is itself a shaded tile (`.bet-input`, the tiles' `--field` background and radius) with the bordered box inside it.

2026-10-27 split button easier to see (user, with a screenshot: "Split between two or more" was "too greyed out. users can miss it"). It and a side pot's "Split this pot" now use `.split-button`: a solid 1.5 px blue (`--b`) outline, a light blue tint, blue bold text and a fork icon (`SplitIcon`). Blue because the rest of the split flow (selected players, the "Split ₹x N ways" button) is blue. Then (user: "Back to one winner has the same problem") both "Back to one winner" buttons (split mode and a side pot's split) became solid `glass-button`s like Undo and Cancel hand; `.dashed-button` had no other users and was removed. Checked on the testbed showdown in the dark theme, and with the light theme forced on the page.

2026-10-27 full names on the table seats (user, with screenshots of a live hand: the table read "Arjun", "Arjun", "Bikram" while the standings and Pick the winner read "Arjun G.", "Arjun S.", "Bikram Big Host"; the chosen or host-given name is final and should match everywhere). **Cause:** all three read the same name from the player list, but each Table view seat showed only its first word (`player.name.split(" ")[0]`, since the Table view was added), so two Arjuns looked alike. **User decision:** show the full name, fitted to the seat label without cutting it to the first word. **Built:** the seat label shows `player.name` in full, centred and wrapping by word; names over 14 characters are 10 px and over 28 are 9 px (`nameLength()`), and a label holding a long name may be 104 px wide (92 px on a crowded table) instead of 84 (76). A single word too long for the label breaks inside the word rather than being hidden. Client-only. **Checked on the testbed at 375 px:** the live hand reads Bikram Big Host (one line), Arjun G., Chirag R., Arjun S.; with names swapped in the page for "Venkataraghavan Subramaniam" (two lines) and a 46-character guest name (three lines) the seats stayed clear of each other. Not checked: a crowded table (7+ players) with long names. `FEATURES.md` Table view and What's New updated.

2026-10-27 seats don't pick the winner (user decision: keep the table's player seats "mostly for visual and informative stuff", so they don't confuse). At the showdown a seat tap no longer awards the pot, and in a split it no longer adds or removes a player; seats read "In the hand" (or "In split" for players picked on the card) and are not buttons there. Picking happens only on the Pick the winner / Split the pot / side pot cards. The card's "Tap a seat on the table or a name below." line and `WinnerPicker`'s `hint` prop are gone. **Still tappable on a seat:** buying in a busted player ("Tap to buy in"); the table's round arrow (turn the seats) is a separate button. Checked on the testbed showdown: both seats disabled, labelled "In the hand".

2026-10-27 one place to buy in during a hand (user: buying in during a hand was a seat tap in Table view but a button on the player's card in List view, "inconsistent action locations"; asked for a separate buy-in card at a similar place in both views). The between-hands `BuyInOptions` block now also renders during a hand as its own glass card, just above Undo in both List and Table view; during a hand it adds "They sit out this hand and play from the next one.", lists only players bust at the deal (the blocked list skips anyone still in the hand), and hides while a pot is being split. The seat tap and the List card's Buy in pill are removed, so table seats are display-only `div`s (`role="group"`, same aria-label) with no tap at all. Checked on the testbed with a live 4-player hand: Chirag R. (bust at the deal) offered "Buy in · ₹10,000" in both views at the same place, Arjun G. (all in ₹0 this hand) not offered, Chirag's seat and card read "Busted". Not tapped (the hand was the user's).

2026-10-27 taller quick sizes (user: make ⅓ Pot, ½ Pot, Pot and All in "a bit taller so that the 1/3 and 1/2 texts are a bit more visible"). `.quick-sizes button` goes from 32 to 40 px tall and 12 to 14 px text; at 375 px each is 74 px wide and every label fits.

2026-10-27 pick the winner, select then award (user, with a screenshot: the pot repeated on every row was redundant, unlike a split where it shows once a player is tapped; "Pick the winner" and the amount had a weak hierarchy, wanted a white heading-sized title with the amount beside it, centred; the blue Split button looked like it was meant to be tapped regardless; tapping a player opened a popup, wanted a greyed-out button that turns green once a player is tapped, with Split below it). **Built:** `WinnerPicker` keeps the picked player for the current hand in local state; rows read "Tap to pick" and the picked row gets a green outline (`.contender.won`) and the pot. 2026-10-28 more on the live standings link (user asked what players would want to see before a redesign; "implement all the suggestions"). **Game state:** `GameState.handResults` (new, optional) gets one entry per finished hand from `recordHandResult()` (all three award paths: one winner, a split, side pots): hand number, pot paid out, winners and pots as on the winner card, and every seat's net; Undo hand drops the undone hand's entry. Shared rules moved to `lib/poker/game.ts`: `isShowdown`, `seatRole`, `seatStatusParts` (with `SEAT_STATUS_LABELS`), plus the new `handsUntilBigBlind()` (deal order over the seats with chips now; heads-up the dealer posts the small blind). **Snapshot** (host → server, `buildLiveSnapshot`): `startedAt`; during a hand `hand` (street, pot, seat to act, showdown) and per player `status`, `inHand`, `role`; per player `bigBlindIn`; `nextBlinds` (next level with `handsLeft`, `endsAt` on the host's clock, `pausedMsLeft` or `dueNow`, so the snapshot only changes with the game); `lastHand`; `biggestPot`; `history` (the last 50 hands' nets). **Server checks** (`validateLiveSnapshot`): statuses only from the app's own words, positions only D/SB/BB, chips in the hand equal settled less current stack, winner names must be players of the game, pot names only "Pot", "Main pot" or "Side pot n", at most 50 history hands, the next level not below the current blinds and exactly one kind of timing. **View** (`deriveLiveView`): rank movement against the hand before the last, rebuys left from the host's limits (`rebuysClosed` once the closing big blind is reached), nets lined up with `historyHands`, chips in play and the average stack of those with chips. **Page:** a hand card, the next level under the blinds (ticking for timed levels), a last-hand card, badges, statuses and ▲/▼ moves on the rows, a "To act" highlight, your own card's notes and net chart, and a game-so-far card. With the line gone, so is the 8 px space under the heading (user request). **Order made clear with numbers** (user: hard to tell whether the log is ascending or descending; offered play order inside each hand, user chose numbering only, "starting fresh for each hand, starting from 1"): the order stays newest first, and each betting action (fold, check, call, bet, raise, all in) has a small grey number before its avatar, 1 for the hand's first, so the top row of a hand has the highest number. Wins, splits, buy-ins and notes are not numbered; rows in a hand keep the number's space so avatars line up. Numbers are counted over the whole log (`groupLog`'s `limit`), so the collapsed view shows true numbers. Blinds and antes aren't logged, so 1 is the first player's decision. The cloud backup refused logs over 200 lines. Saved games kept no log, so Continue game started a new one. **Built:** (1) `LOG_LIMIT` = 5,000 in `lib/poker/action-log.ts`, used by every log writer (game, ledger, UI) and by the backup check; the backup body limit is now 1 MB (a long night's game with its log is about 150 KB). The players are saved with the lines because lines name players as they were at the table, which may differ from today's names. Saving sends it (`savedActionLog`, the quick ledger's `ledgerSession` too); Continue game's update replaces it, or keeps the saved one when none is sent (`COALESCE`). (4) Continue game loads the saved log first and stops with a message if it can't, since saving would otherwise replace the saved log with only the new lines; `gameFromSession` puts the saved lines under the "Continued from" line. (5) Saved game **Details** ends with **Action log** (`SavedLog`, loaded on first open and kept for the visit, dropped when that game is saved again), the same `ActionLog` component as the live game (now taking `lines`, `players`, `liveHand`); older games say none was saved. 2026-10-28 keep or take back rebuys nobody played with (user: a player may rebuy and then the group stops before a hand with that money finishes; real poker never reverses a buy-in, since the player just cashes out their chips, so the money is right either way, but the unplayed buy-in raises money invested and lowers return %; offered an automatic drop or leaving it, user chose: "let the app ask the users what to do because this itself will be a pretty rare scenario ... the users will appreciate that the app has even thought of this"). Only the save-time question was built; a separate Undo buy-in button was discussed and not requested. **Rule:** a rebuy is unplayed when no hand the player was dealt into has finished since it (a hand cut off by saving doesn't count); a first buy-in is never offered. **Built:** `Player.buyInsPlayed` (buy-ins the player had in their last finished hand; set for everyone in `Hand.dealtIn` by `recordHandResult`, baseline on the first rebuy in `buyInPlayer`; `CompletedHand.buyInsPlayedBefore` lets Undo hand restore it), pure `unplayedRebuys(game)` and `takeBackRebuys(game, players, money)` in `lib/poker/game.ts` (removes the trailing buy-ins and their chips from the stack and the hand's starting stacks, logs "*Name* takes back a buy-in of ₹X when saving (no hand finished with it)", parsed as a `take-back` row). `endSession` adds a `rebuys` choice to the confirm modal (Keep / Take back per player, Keep first); taking any back saves a copy of the game (`finishSession(completedHands, saving)`), so a cancelled or failed save leaves the game on screen untouched. No migration: saved results simply have one buy-in fewer. 7 new tests. **Checked on the testbed:** Game 77 continued; in hand 30 Chirag went all in and lost to Bikram; he rebought ₹10,000; hand 31 was dealt; Finish and Save showed the panel for Chirag; Take back saved 30 hands with Chirag on 3 buy-ins, ₹0, net −₹30,000, nets adding to 0, no hand 31 lines, and the take-back row on top of the log. 2026-10-28 discarded game no longer shown as unfinished (found in passing on the testbed: after Discard game, Home showed "Unfinished game · Game 79 · Saved just now" with Restore / Delete). Cause: `forgetGameBackup` waits for a backup update in flight before sending its `DELETE`, but going Home fetches the backup list straight away, so the list could answer before the delete landed and keep showing the game. The row itself was deleted (a fresh GET returned none; Restore would have said it was gone), so only Home's list was stale until the next refresh. Reproduced on the testbed by discarding 2.3 s after a change: PUT 2.0–2.6 s, Home's GET 2.3–2.8 s, DELETE 2.6–3.2 s. Fix (client only): `forgetGameBackup` marks the game in `forgettingBackups`, takes it off Home's list at once, and refreshes the list once the delete is done (or failed, so a backup that really remains still shows); `refreshGameBackups` leaves marked games out via the pure `withoutForgottenBackups` (tested) and ignores answers older than its newest request; `sendGameBackup` sends nothing for a marked game. Covers Discard game, Stop continuing and Close game (no new hands), which share the helper; saving already deletes the backup on the server and refuses late updates (410), and Home's list leaves out saved games. Checked on a worktree testbed server (port 3007) with the same timing: Home's GET answered before the DELETE and Home showed no unfinished game. tsc, lint and 507 tests pass. Not known whether it happened on production; the code is the same there.

Before, tapping an avatar in a guest's sheet (Profile, ⋯ → Change avatar) saved it at once. First built as a confirmation after each tap; the user rejected that the same day ("for every tap it asks me for confirmation, but that's not how usually users select an avatar") and asked for select-then-save. **Built:** in `AvatarSheet` a tap only moves the green ring (`chosen`) and a full-width **Save Avatar** button under the grid saves it through `onPick` and closes the sheet. The button is disabled while the ringed avatar is the current one (so on opening) or while saving. Closing (backdrop, Escape) saves nothing. First only for guests; asked whether your own avatar sheet should match, the user said "Mine too", so both sheets work this way and tap-to-save is gone. No server or migration change. **Checked** on the testbed (3006) at 375 px: Save Avatar disabled on opening, enabled after tapping two others, disabled again on tapping back to the current one; saving changed Asha's avatar with "Avatar Saved"; Escape after picking left it unchanged; Asha's avatar was put back after. Your own sheet ("Your avatar") checked the same way: disabled on opening, enabled after two taps without saving, disabled back on the current one, saved on the button; the test account's avatar was put back after.

2026-10-29 Next.js 16.4.3 security upgrade (step 1 of the pre-launch audit, finding C1; user: "Yes, upgrade Next.js"). `npm audit` listed 11 advisories for `next` 16.3.5, 2 of them critical: remote code execution in `next/og` `ImageResponse`, which both results-card routes use with names any signed-in person controls, and in the Image Optimization API, which `next/image` in `components/brand-mark.tsx` keeps live; also a Server Actions denial of service (the app has the sign-out action). **Done:** `next` and `eslint-config-next` 16.3.5 → 16.4.3 (exact pins, released 2026-10-23); `npm audit fix` without `--force` also replaced `sharp` and `source-map-js`. No code change: the 16.4 upgrade notes in `node_modules/next/dist/docs` change nothing this app uses (no Cache Components; `turbopack.root` and `outputFileTracingIncludes` unchanged). **Left (no upstream fix):** 11 advisories, 1 critical, all inside `@neondatabase/auth` 0.5.0-beta's dependencies. The critical one is in `better-auth` (*corrected 2026-10-29 in the entry "CI pipeline": it is GHSA-965c-763c-88jm, an account takeover through magic links when a social or OAuth provider is enabled, not the device-login and rate-limit advisories named here; this summary was read from a truncated audit line*), the highs are `zod` memory use and the auth packages that pull these in, and the lows are `deepmerge` through the unused `@neondatabase/auth-ui` email templates. Even the newest SDK, 0.7.0-beta, pins the same `better-auth` 1.7.6 and an affected `zod`, so upgrading the SDK doesn't help. These features run in Neon's hosted auth service, not in this app, and the app never validates its own input with `zod`. Not forced with `overrides`, since the SDK pins exact versions. Recheck when a new SDK release comes out. **Checked** in a scratch copy, so the testbed server another session left on 3006 kept running: `npm run lint`, `tsc`, 508 tests and `next build` pass. On a testbed server from the copy (3008), signed in as Bikram Big Host: Home, Games and Ranks load; the host's results card (Game 78) and a friend's card return valid PNGs (184 KB and 195 KB), and the host's card draws correctly with fonts, avatars and logo; a same-origin POST to `/api/account` reaches the handler (400 for an unknown action); no server errors. The repo's `node_modules` was then installed from the same lockfile (byte-identical), and `tsc`, tests and ESLint on the app code pass there. The testbed server on 3006 still runs the old 16.3.5 modules until restarted. Committed and pushed to `main` with the user's go-ahead the same day.

2026-10-29 security headers and report-only CSP (step 2 of the pre-launch audit, finding H1; user: "Start step 2"). Production sent only Vercel's HSTS, plus `X-Powered-By: Next.js`. **Built:** (1) `next.config.ts` sends with every response `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains` (not `preload`: joining the browsers' preload list is a separate step that is slow to undo), `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `Permissions-Policy` (camera only for the app's own pages, for the QR scanner; microphone, location, payment, USB and ad topics off) and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin`; `poweredByHeader: false`. Pages that set their own referrer policy (live and invite links: `no-referrer` meta tag) keep it. (2) A Content Security Policy with a fresh nonce per page (`lib/security/content-security-policy.ts`), sent as **Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only**. Scripts need the nonce or must be loaded by a script that has it (`'strict-dynamic'`, for Next's chunks and Vercel Analytics); `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `form-action 'self'`; images from the app, `blob:` (the results card preview) and `data:`; everything else from the app only. Styles may be inline: server-rendered `style` attributes can't carry a nonce, and style injection is low risk. **Agent's calls (explained to the user):** a nonce, not `'unsafe-inline'`, because only a nonce stops injected scripts. The cost is that every page now renders per request: the root layout reads the nonce, and the info pages are no longer prerendered (a few milliseconds each). Reports go through `report-uri` only: Chrome ignores `report-uri` when `report-to` is present, and its batched `report-to` reports never arrived in testing, while `report-uri` arrived at once and is the only form Firefox and Safari send. (3) `proxy.ts` now runs on every page (matcher excludes build output, `/_vercel`, `/csp-report` and files with an extension). Sign-in is still required only on `/` and `/admin` (`SIGNED_IN_PAGES`). The sign-in check gets a header-only copy of the request carrying the policy, so the page receives the nonce and a form post's body is untouched. A `Content-Security-Policy` header sent by the client is dropped, because Next.js would read it first. (4) `app/csp-report/route.ts` takes reports in either browser format, up to 64 KB and 20 per request, and logs one `[csp]` line. Through `lib/security/csp-report.ts`, live and invite tokens become `[token]`, query strings, fragments and script samples are dropped, and `data:`/`blob:` addresses are shown as their kind. It sits outside `/api`, so the same-origin check and the firewall's write limit don't apply; it changes no data. `readJsonBody` gained an optional content-type pattern for it. (5) The auth client is loaded with `import()` only when the app or the locked-account page emails a sign-in link. Its bundled zod (4.3.6, inside `@neondatabase/auth`) runs `Function("")` to test whether eval is allowed, which the policy reported on every Home load; that zod copy keeps its settings private, so it can't be switched off from the app. The sign-in page still loads it, so each sign-in page visit reports one harmless `eval` (zod falls back when it's blocked). 11 new tests (519 in all). **To enforce** after a few days of clean `[csp]` lines in Vercel's logs: set `CSP_HEADER` to `"Content-Security-Policy"`. **Checked** on the production build (`next start`) and in development, both against the testbed from a scratch copy (3009 and 3008). The headers are on pages, API responses and static files, with no `X-Powered-By`. Every `<script>` on Home, sign-in, the five info pages, a live link, an invite link and `/admin` carries the page's nonce. With Bikram Big Host signed in, nothing was reported on Home, Play, Ranks, Games, Profile, Settings, the results card preview or What's New. Sign-out and persona sign-in through the reworked proxy both work; no hydration warnings in development. Test posts to `/csp-report` in both formats were logged with tokens as `[token]`, and junk, wrong types and oversized posts were ignored. Before the auth client change, the zod `eval` probe was traced from a real report (`/_next/static/chunks/…js`, line 14, column 71403). After it, Home loads 12 chunks without the auth client. With a session over 10 minutes old, Settings → Delete My Account was refused by the server and offered a fresh sign-in link; **Email Me A Sign-In Link** then loaded the auth chunk, reported the probe once and sent the request. The testbed answers that request with 404, because `testbed/setup.mjs` turns magic links off there; the same request gets the same 404 from the repo's dev server. `/auth/callback` through the new proxy: no verifier → `INVALID_CALLBACK`, a fake one → `INVALID_TOKEN`, as before. **Not checked:** Vercel (headers on the real domain, including whether Vercel's own HSTS header is replaced) and a real phone; the QR scanner's camera under the new `Permissions-Policy`.

2026-10-29 invite tokens kept out of analytics (found while building the CSP report endpoint; user: "Fix analytics leak too"). `scrubUrl` in `components/web-analytics.tsx` replaced the token in `/live/<token>` page addresses but sent `/join/<token>` invite addresses to Vercel Web Analytics whole, although the privacy page says page addresses are sent "with private links blanked out". Only the account owner sees them, in the Vercel dashboard; an invite works once and expires after 30 days, so leaked ones stop working on their own. **Fix:** `lib/security/token-paths.ts` (`redactTokenPath`) is now the one rule for paths that carry a token (`/live/`, `/api/live/`, `/join/`), used by both analytics and the CSP report summaries; 2 new tests (521 in all). The privacy wording is now true, so it is unchanged. **Checked** in development against the testbed: opening `/join/<token>?from=test` sent the page view as `/join/[token]` with no query string. Vercel's analytics script (`va.vercel-scripts.com/v1/script.debug.js`, read, not run) sends a referrer only when it comes from another host, so a sign-in address carrying `?invite=` can't reach analytics either.

2026-10-29 CI pipeline (step 3 of the pre-launch audit: H4, M1, L4; user: "Start step 3"). **Built:** (1) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs on every push to `main` and every pull request: `npm ci`, `npm run lint`, `next typegen` then `tsc --noEmit`, `npm test`, `npm run build`, `npm run audit:check`. Read-only token, credentials not kept after checkout, superseded runs cancelled, 15-minute limit, actions pinned to release commits (`actions/checkout` v7.0.1, `actions/setup-node` v7.1.0). No secrets: the build gets a fake `NEON_AUTH_BASE_URL` (`.invalid`) and a placeholder cookie secret, because `lib/auth/server.ts` checks them as it loads. (2) `npm run audit:check` (`scripts/check-audit.mjs`) fails on any high or critical advisory missing from `scripts/audit-exceptions.json`, and on any exception past its `reviewBy` date; it warns when an exception's advisory is gone. A plain `npm audit --audit-level=high` would fail on every run because of the auth SDK's unfixed advisories, and a check that's always red gets ignored. (3) `npm run lint` passes again: `eslint.config.mjs` skips what `.gitignore` lists (`includeIgnoreFile` from `@eslint/compat` 2.1.1, a new dev dependency) and `.claude/**` (agent worktrees, which made up most of the 36,678 problems). It checks exactly the 173 tracked source files. (4) Node 24 pinned (`engines: 24.x`, `.nvmrc`), matching Vercel's setting (user checked: 24.x), so production's runtime is unchanged; `@types/node` ^20 → ^24.19.2 to match. 5 new tests (526 in all). **Advisories reviewed (correction to the entry "Next.js 16.4.3 security upgrade"):** reading the full audit JSON showed the critical `better-auth` advisory is GHSA-965c-763c-88jm, published 2026-10-27: OAuth sign-in state accepted as a magic link token, so anyone who knows an email can sign in as that person. It affects a Better Auth server with magic links and at least one social or Generic OAuth provider. GHSA-r4xp-prcw-77qf (high) needs the OAuth Proxy plugin. GHSA-q84f-53jg-9ppm (high) needs the device authorization plugin. GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5 (high) is `zod` memory use. The fix for the first two is planned for better-auth 1.7.7, not yet released. This app runs no Better Auth server; Neon's hosted Neon Auth does. Checked read-only through the Neon connector: no social or OAuth provider on production (`br-small-sea-ayumyssr`), `cutover-rehearsal` (`br-tiny-forest-ayt6f3fe`), `multi-user-auth` (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`) or `claim-test-throwaway`. The other three branches have no Neon Auth, production's OAuth server plugin is off, and the testbed project has magic links off. So the app is not exposed. **Rule: enable no social or OAuth sign-in on any Neon branch until Neon runs better-auth 1.7.7 or later.** All four are exceptions until 2026-11-30. **Checked:** a clean copy (tracked files only, no `.env` files, fresh `npm ci`, empty environment) passes every CI step on Node 26; GitHub will be the first run on Node 24. The workflow parses as YAML. **Not done, user decisions:** branch protection on `main` (it would block direct pushes, the current way of working, unless work moves to pull requests); Dependabot (weekly update pull requests); making Vercel wait for CI before production.

2026-10-29 rate limits and storage caps: plan (step 4 of the pre-launch audit, findings H2 and H3; user: "Plan step 4", then decided each point below). Not built: plans are not implementation authorization. **Findings.** A host playing with the live link writes about 26 times a minute: live updates at most every 3 s, backups at most every 10 s (`GAME_BACKUP_MIN_INTERVAL_MS`). The one firewall rule allows 30 writes a minute per IP, so in blocking mode two hosts on one Wi-Fi or carrier address would be cut off mid-game. Nothing limits reads, including results-card renders, which the Games tab preloads for every Share button that scrolls into view. Nothing caps what an account stores: a saved game can reach about 1.5 MB (5,000 log lines × 300 characters), a backup 1 MB, and the number of games, players and backups is unlimited. Neon Free gives each branch 1 GB; production uses about 35 MB (project settings, 2026-10-29), with 6 hours of restore history. Production's per-account counts were not read: the session's permissions blocked production reads, so the caps are sized from the testbed's heaviest host (18 players, 78 games) and the hard request limits. **Decisions (user, 2026-10-29; all the recommended options).** (1) Per-account rate limits, counted in the database, reversing the 2026-09-24 "no app-side counters" choice: **60 writes a minute** (any non-GET API request by a signed-in account); **120 results-card pictures per 10 minutes** (host and friend cards together); **30 friend-code lookups and sends per 10 minutes**; **25 MB of saved-game data a day** (the bytes of requests that save, import or edit games; a long night is about 0.1 MB). (2) Storage caps per account: **500 players**, **5,000 saved games** (discarded ones included), **5 unfinished-game backups**. (3) A 6th unfinished game **drops the backup touched longest ago** instead of refusing: no error is shown, the phone keeps its own copy, and backups expire after 7 days anyway. (4) The Vercel firewall rule becomes **all `/api/*` requests except `/api/live/*`, 300 a minute per IP, action 429**. It covers reads too and leaves the cached public live-link API alone, so a full table of phones on one Wi-Fi isn't blocked. Hobby allows one rule; the user changes it in the dashboard, since the Vercel connector can't see this project. **Design.** Migration 0033: a `rate_limits` table (account, bucket, window start, used; cascades with the account, row security, no direct grants) and a `SECURITY DEFINER` function `consume_rate_limit(bucket, cost, limit, window)` with a fixed window, which raises `rate:limited` with the seconds left. The caps are enforced by triggers on `players`, `poker_sessions` and `game_backups`; backups drop the oldest instead of raising. `requireHostAccount` takes the request's bucket and cost and runs the check in the transaction that already loads the account, so it adds no database round trip. A refused request gets 429 with `Retry-After` and the app's existing "Too many requests" message; a cap gets a 409 with a plain message (for example "You can have up to 500 players"). The client stops preloading cards after a 429, and live and backup sync already retry on their own. The daily purge job also reports the database size and fails its Healthchecks.io ping above 700 MB (70% of 1 GB), so the user gets an email well before the plan fills. Unchanged: sign-in link requests are limited by Neon Auth and the firewall (a per-email limit needs custom SMTP, audit step 7); `/csp-report` writes no data. **Considered and not chosen:** Upstash Redis (a new provider and privacy change, and an extra network hop per request); Vercel Pro firewall rules keyed by header (needs Pro, and a key the client controls can't stand for an account). **Build steps when approved:** (a) migration 0033 rehearsed on `multi-user-auth` (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`) and the testbed, including the app role refusing direct writes to `rate_limits` and each cap's boundary; rollback is to revert the app first, then drop the triggers, function and table. (b) App changes and tests. Docs: `FEATURES.md` with the exact limits; the privacy page, since per-account request counters are new data (kept for the current window only, deleted with the account), and its date in `lib/info/legal.ts`. A What's New entry only if the user sees a change. (c) Production migration with the user's go-ahead, then the app push. (d) The user switches the firewall rule. Open: whether to tell a host when their oldest backup was dropped (the decision above says no).

2026-10-29 rate limits and storage caps: built (audit step 4; user: "Build step 4", as decided in the plan entry above). **Database:** migration 0033 (`migrations/README.md` has the details, deploy order and rollback): `rate_limits` plus `consume_rate_limit()`, and cap triggers on `players` (500), `poker_sessions` (5,000) and `game_backups` (5, the oldest dropped). Applied to `multi-user-auth` (`br-little-rain-ay5fufwv`) through the Neon connector and to the testbed with `npm run testbed:setup`, which also rotates the testbed app password, so a running testbed server needs a restart. A script checked it on both as `menoka_app` with a throwaway account, in a transaction that was rolled back: 21 checks, all pass. The first runs failed only on the script's own test data (backup expiry, game kind and results rules). **App:** `lib/security/limits.ts` holds the numbers, the messages and `limitErrorResponse` (429 with `Retry-After`; 409 with `quota-players` / `quota-games`). `requireHostAccount`, `requireAccountSession` and `requireRecentHostAccount` take a bucket and count the request in the transaction that already loads the account, so there's no extra round trip. Every changing route counts as `writes`, the two card routes as `cards`, and reads aren't counted. Saving and continuing games (`POST`/`PUT /api/sessions`) add the request's bytes to `game-data` in their first transaction (`readJsonBody` now returns `bytes`). Friend `find`, `send` and `send-coplayer` count `friend-lookups` in a transaction of their own first, so a code that finds nobody still counts (otherwise its error would roll the count back). `apiErrorMessage` now shows the server's message for a 429 when there is one, and the firewall's message-less 429 still gets the fixed text. The results card preview shows the limit message, and preloading pauses until `Retry-After`. The daily purge job reports the branch size (all databases, read by `menoka_purge`: about 28 MB on the development branch, checked) and fails its Healthchecks.io ping from 70% of 1 GB. Docs: `FEATURES.md` section 24 "Limits on use" and section 10 (5 copies). The privacy page lists the usage counts, and the account-deletion page says they're deleted too, both dated 2026-10-29. The terms already forbid getting round limits. No What's New entry: a host only sees this at a limit. 14 new tests (535 in all). **Checked** against the testbed (development server from a scratch copy, as Bikram Big Host): 65 quick writes gave 60 × 200 then 429 with `Retry-After: 27` and the plain message, and reads kept working. 125 card pictures gave 120 × 200 then 429 with `Retry-After: 585`. In the Games tab, Share showed "Too many results cards in a short time…", and preloading sent one request, was refused, and stopped. 32 lookups of made-up codes gave 30 × 404 then 429. Re-sending a saved game by POST and PUT returned `saved: 0` with nothing changed. No server errors. **Not checked:** the players and games caps through the API (checked in the database, and the replies by unit test), 25 MB of game data, the backup cap through the API, and the daily job's report (its query was run as `menoka_purge`). **Next:** (c) apply 0033 to production (`br-small-sea-ayumyssr`) and the preview branch (`br-tiny-forest-ayt6f3fe`) with the user's go-ahead, before pushing the app. (d) The user replaces the firewall rule: all `/api/*` except `/api/live/*`, 300 a minute per IP, action 429. Then update `FEATURES.md` section 24's last line.

2026-10-29 rate limits and storage caps: released (user: "Migrate, then push"). Production (`br-small-sea-ayumyssr`) had 0001–0032; 0033 was applied there in one transaction through the Neon connector and checked from the catalog (recorded, row security, function grants, triggers). Then the app was committed and pushed. The preview branch (`br-tiny-forest-ayt6f3fe`) was **not** migrated: it is at `0012_group_standings`, 20 migrations behind and without `game_backups`, so Vercel previews have been behind the code since 0013. Catching it up is a separate task. **Next:** the user replaces the Vercel firewall rule (all `/api/*` except `/api/live/*`, 300 a minute per IP, action 429), then `FEATURES.md` section 24's last line changes to say it blocks.

2026-10-29 sign-in and device safety (step 5 of the pre-launch audit: M2, M6, L1, L2; user: "Start step 5", plan first and ask about anything user-visible). **Decisions (user, 2026-10-29; all the recommended options):** the admin dashboard needs an email sign-in from the last **1 hour**; signing out clears **everything of yours** from the phone; add **Sign Out Of All Devices**; a sign-in ends after **90 days**. **Built.** (1) `/admin`: `loadAdminData` first checks admin status with `admin_system()` (cheap, refuses non-admins), so others still get a plain 404. An admin whose session is older than `ADMIN_SIGN_IN_WINDOW_MS` gets `AdminSignInAgain` (`components/admin-sign-in-again.tsx`), whose link uses `callbackURL /auth/callback?next=admin`. `afterSignInPath` (`lib/auth/sign-in-destination.ts`) allows only an invite, `/admin` or Home. (2) Sign-out in the app (Profile ⋯ and Settings) runs `clearDeviceForSignOut`: a last backup PUT, then removing the game and the live token from local storage. The game is kept when the backup fails (offline), and removed when it's saved (410) or moved (409). **Agent's call, flagged to the user:** data from before accounts (`pokerLedger.v1`, `pokerLedger.history.v1`) is not cleared. Adoption is opt-in and partial, so it may exist only on that phone, and clearing it would destroy it. The name and locked screens' sign-out forms are unchanged: no game can be on the device there. (3) `signOutEverywhere` server action: `auth.revokeSessions()`, then `auth.signOut()`. If revoking fails nothing is signed out and the app shows a message. Confirmation sheet first. (4) `getHostSession` treats a session older than `MAX_SESSION_AGE_MS` (90 days) as signed out, and revokes it where cookies can change. An unreadable `createdAt` doesn't count as expired, so a library change can't lock everyone out. (5) `visibleText` (`lib/visible-text.ts`), used for account, player and game names: NFC, controls, direction marks and invisible fillers removed, the zero-width joiner and non-joiner kept for Bengali, Hindi and emoji. A name made only of hidden characters is refused. Action-log names are untouched: lines are matched as exact text. 11 new tests (546 in all). Docs: `FEATURES.md` sections 1, 21 and 22, What's New 2026-10-29, the Features page (signed in "for up to 90 days"), privacy (the game on the phone is removed at sign-out). **Checked** on the testbed (development server from a scratch copy; Bikram made an admin there for the test with SQL and removed again afterwards). An old session gave the Sign in again card at `/admin`; with the admin row removed it gave 404 with no hint; after a fresh persona sign-in the dashboard opened. Sign Out from Settings with a quick-ledger game running: back on the sign-in page, the account's game key was gone from local storage (only the device ID and the bell's timestamp were left), and after signing in again Home offered Game 79 as an Unfinished game, "saved just now" (deleted afterwards). A name with a zero-width space and a direction override saved as "Bikram Big Host" (17 characters in, 15 out); a name of only hidden characters got 400. Sign Out Of All Devices: confirmation, then the sign-in page. A second session made by script still read `/api/account` (200) 4 seconds later, while Neon Auth's uncached `get-session` already returned `null`. The auth library trusts its signed session cookie for 5 minutes (`sessionDataTtl` 300). At 10:32:32, once its cached copy (made at sign-in, 10:27:10) had expired, the same session got 401. So other devices are cut off within 5 minutes; the same lag already applied to account deletion. Shortening it would mean setting `sessionDataTtl` lower, at the cost of a Neon Auth round trip more often (not done). **Not checked:** a 90-day-old session (unit tested), the admin link's email (the testbed has magic links off), a real phone. Committed and pushed the same day with the user's go-ahead.

2026-10-29 monitoring, error pages and security.txt (step 6 of the pre-launch audit: M3, L5, L7; user: "Start step 6", plan first and ask before signing up for anything). **Decisions (user):** Sentry in the US region for errors and uptime; source maps uploaded; `security.txt` names therajarshiroy@gmail.com. **Built.** (1) `@sentry/nextjs` 11.8.0 (exact pin; the newest release at least two weeks old). `instrumentation-client.ts` and `instrumentation.ts` start it from `lib/monitoring/sentry-options.ts`: on only when `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` is set; errors only (no tracing, no replay); `sendDefaultPii: false`; `beforeSend` = `scrubEvent` (`lib/monitoring/scrub.ts`), which keeps the request's scrubbed address, method and browser only, drops the user, and removes emails, link tokens and query strings from every string. The server also reports every `console.error` (`captureConsoleIntegration`), because the API routes catch their own errors and log them that way. `onRequestError` covers what Next catches itself. `app/error.tsx` and `app/global-error.tsx` report through `reportError`. `next.config.ts` is wrapped in `withSentryConfig` (imported from `@sentry/nextjs/config` in v11): source maps are uploaded when `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` is set and deleted from the deployment after; `bundleSizeOptimizations` leaves out tracing, replay and debug code. Measured over all client chunks, gzipped: 440 KB before, 513 KB with Sentry untrimmed, 492 KB trimmed, so about **52 KB more** for phones to download. The CSP's `connect-src` gains Sentry's ingest origin, derived from the DSN, only when it is set. (2) `app/not-found.tsx` (branded "Page not found"), `app/error.tsx` (Try again, Go to Pokerize, error code), `app/global-error.tsx` (its own minimal styles; a hard reload button); styles `.error-page-*`. Error pages in this Next version get `retry`, not `reset`. (3) `public/.well-known/security.txt` (RFC 9116; Expires 2027-10-28; a test fails 30 days before). (4) `GET /api/health`: 200 `{ok:true}` when the database answers `SELECT 1`, else 503; for the uptime monitor. (5) `lib/security/security-log.ts`: one `[security]` JSON warning line each for refused cross-site requests, reached rate limits and caps, required recent sign-ins, too-old sessions, refused admins and admin sign-in-again, sign-out everywhere and unauthorised cron calls. IDs and kinds only. 7 new tests (553 in all). Docs: privacy page (Sentry: what a report holds, what it never holds, 30 days), `FEATURES.md` sections 10 and 22, `.env.example`. No What's New entry: nothing a player notices. **Checked** on a clean copy (fresh `npm ci`, empty environment): every CI step passes; the build only warns that there's no auth token, so no source maps are uploaded. On the testbed (development server from a scratch copy): `security.txt` is served as `text/plain` with `nosniff`; `/api/health` gives 200; an unknown address shows the branded Page not found; a cross-site POST gives 403 and logs `[security] {"event":"cross-site-request-refused","method":"POST","path":"/api/account"}`; with no DSN the Sentry code loads but sends nothing, and Home works with no server errors. **Not checked:** reports arriving in Sentry (no DSN yet), the source map upload, `app/error.tsx` and `global-error.tsx` showing (no way to make a page fail on purpose), the uptime monitor. **For the user:** create the Sentry account (US) and a Next.js project; set `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` (Production and Preview), `SENTRY_ORG`, `SENTRY_PROJECT` and `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` (an organisation token) in Vercel; turn on "Prevent storing of IP addresses" in the project's security settings; add an uptime monitor for `https://www.pokerize.app/api/health`; keep the default email alerts. Committed and pushed the same day with the user's go-ahead.

2026-10-29 Sentry set up, and IP addresses caught (user: "i've added the sentry connector. do the steps yourself"). **Done through the connectors:** Sentry project `pokerize` (org `rajarshi-roy`, US) with its DSN. Vercel Production and Preview got `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN`, `SENTRY_ORG` and `SENTRY_PROJECT` as plain variables (none is secret; the Vercel connector finds the project only without a `teamId`). The default issue alert ("high priority issues", email) was already there. Uptime monitor "Pokerize is up" checks `https://www.pokerize.app/privacy` every 5 minutes and marks the site down after 2 failures. **Agent's call, explained to the user:** not `/api/health`, because a database query every 5 minutes would stop Neon Free's compute from ever suspending and use up its monthly allowance. Database failures still show as errors; switch the monitor to `/api/health` on a paid Neon plan. Production was redeployed from `d8978ac` so the DSN was built in. A deliberate test error from the live sign-in page arrived as POKERIZE-1, with the message scrubbed (`[email]`, `/join/[token]`, no query string). **Finding:** the event held `user: ip:…` and a city. In Sentry SDK v11, `sendDefaultPii` is no longer read. The new `dataCollection` option defaults to collecting user info (so the browser sends `infer_ip: "auto"`, confirmed on the live page), cookies, headers, bodies, query strings, database query data and stack-frame variables. **Mitigation:** an attempt to roll production back was refused by the session's permissions (production deploy). Instead the Sentry DSN key was set inactive with `update_dsn`, so Sentry rejects every report until the fix is live. **Fix (committed, waiting for the user's go-ahead to push):** `DATA_COLLECTION` in `lib/monitoring/sentry-options.ts` turns every kind off. A test reads the SDK's own list of kinds, so a new one fails CI. The scrubber also drops stack-frame `vars`. On the testbed with the real (inactive) DSN, the built SDK reports `infer_ip: "never"` with all collection off. 3 new tests (556 in all). **For the user:** delete POKERIZE-1 in Sentry (two test events holding this machine's IP; otherwise kept 30 days). Turn on Settings → Security & Privacy → "Prevent Storing of IP Addresses" (no connector tool for it). Add `SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN` (an organisation token, Sensitive) in Vercel for source maps. After the push: reactivate the DSN key and send one test error to confirm it has no IP.
